### A tick's working cell shows the previous run's turn, context, and tool until the new pi run writes its first line, and its log-mtime "no pi output for N" raises a false "looks stuck" alert during the baseline check of every tick that wakes after 5+ minutes idle (found by human-directed investigation 2026-09-30), fixed 2026-09-30

- **Symptom:** at about 02:09:43 PDT on 2026-09-30, the dashboard's alert strip read "bugfix looks stuck — bugfix: no pi output for 29m22s", and the row read `working 1m39s · turn 26 · ctx 44.2k · read change-preview.ts · no pi output for 29m22s`. Tick 632 had started at 02:08:04 and was still in its baseline check (`build_check` scope `baseline`, 02:08:04 → 02:10:18, 134 s). Its pi `session` line is stamped 02:10:18. Turn 26, 44.2k, and change-preview.ts all belonged to tick 631's run (01:39:11–01:40:20), and 29m22s was the time since that run's last write. The alert cleared on its own once pi started.
- **Cause:** `readLiveProgress` (src/ui/progress.ts) returns the log's newest `session` run, with `quietMs` taken from the log file's mtime. Nothing compared either one to the tick's start (`LoopState.lastTickStartedAt`). `inFlightDetail` (src/ui/status-model.ts) then appended the stale turn, context, and tool, and past 5 minutes it added the "no pi output" flag. `fleetAlerts`' STALL pattern (src/ui/fleet-alerts.ts, mirrored in src/ui/gui-client-model.ts) turned that flag into the stuck alert. So a code-role tick whose previous run ended more than 5 minutes earlier showed this for the whole length of its pre-pi steps: worktree reset plus baseline check, 30–130 s at today's suite speed. That made it routine on wake-ups after a sleep. The parked-waiter fix (`parkedSince`, under Fixed) closed the same stale-detail leak for a tick still waiting on the semaphore, but not for one that holds its permit and has not spawned pi yet.
- **How to reproduce:** let a code role wake after 5+ minutes idle. During its baseline check, `curl -s 127.0.0.1:7180/api/status` showed the previous tick's turn and ctx in the row, plus the `stuck` alert. Deterministically: a `LoopState` with `running: true`, `lastTickStartedAt` = now, and a role log whose last lines are 10 minutes old rendered `no pi output for 10m…` through `loopPhase`. The regression test (test/status-model.test.ts, "a tick's baseline check shows no previous run's detail and no false quiet alarm" plus its token-metrics sibling) pins exactly this shape: a log 29 minutes old carrying the previous run's turn/ctx/tool against a tick start 99 s old reproduced `working 1m39s · turn 2 · ctx 44.2k · read change-preview.ts · no pi output for 29m00s` before the fix and bare `working 1m39s` after.
- **Expected:** until this tick's first pi output, the cell reads as a pre-run phase (bare `working 1m39s`, or a named step). It carries no turn, context, or tool from an earlier run, and no quiet time measured from before the tick started.
- **Fix (as landed 2026-09-30):** one rule in status-model.ts — `progressOfTick(s, p)`: a log tail's progress belongs to this tick only when its newest write happened after the tick started, which `readLiveProgress`'s mtime-derived `quietMs` already distinguishes exactly (a run writing within the tick keeps `quietMs` at or below the tick's own age; anything older is a previous run). A tail predating the tick is treated as absent — the cell falls back to the bare phase label with its own elapsed — and a tail within the tick keeps its honest quiet measure. Applied by every status-model reader: `workingDetail`, the reviewing branch of `loopPhase`, the token metrics (`displayTokenMetrics`, so a stale tail's output/peak can no longer ride into a new tick's `generated` figure), and `loopRowCells`' precomputed tail that the GUI payload and status-render's state cell consume — so the TUI, the GUI, and the alert cannot drift apart. progress.ts is unchanged. A state with no recorded start (a stale `running` flag after a crash) cannot be judged and keeps the old show-the-tail behavior.

- **Validation gap:** no-repro (closest fit) — the existing suite stayed green while the bug stood because nothing tied a log tail to `lastTickStartedAt`: the stale window shows only in a live dashboard, during a post-idle tick's 30–130 s baseline, and no fixture modeled the tick-start boundary at all; once the regression fixture pinned it (a previous run's log 29 minutes old against a 99-second-old tick start) the failure reproduced deterministically and ran red before the fix.

### A compiled test tree run from outside the checkout silently replaces every fake command with the real binary: `SCRIPT_SHIM` resolves relative to the compiled file and nothing checks that it exists, so on 2026-09-30 the coverage loop's `/tmp` build ran 291 real `pi` agents against oMLX (found by human-directed investigation 2026-09-30), fixed 2026-09-30

- **Symptom:** from 00:42 to past 02:21 PDT on 2026-09-30, oMLX served a steady stream of small (2–5k-token) prompts for "A test project". The day's budget was nearly untouched ($1.91 of $15), and no fleet tick used the fallback after 01:20. Every client on :8000 was the real `/opt/homebrew/bin/pi`, with a `--session-dir` under `$TMPDIR/tumwater-test-run-*/…` and `-e /private/tmp/tumwater-cov-dist/src/pi-extension/bounded-output.js`. 291 of the 756 test-fixture session files written after midnight record `"provider":"omlx"`. Up to 15 were connected at once against the server's 3 slots. These were real agents with real tools, running in fixture repos and driven by the fixture prompts of the fake-pi tests (loop*, pi, review, review-precheck).
- **How it happened:** coverage tick 410 (00:30–02:11) compiled src with `npx tsc --incremental false --outDir /tmp/tumwater-cov-dist`. It then ran `node --experimental-test-coverage --test /tmp/tumwater-cov-dist/test/*.test.js` under `env -u TUMWATER_PI_BIN`, so the 2026-09-28 `TUMWATER_PI_BIN` guard was not the gap. `SCRIPT_SHIM` in test/fake-commands.ts is `new URL("../../test/fixtures/script-shim", import.meta.url)`. From `/tmp/tumwater-cov-dist/test/fake-commands.js` that resolves to `/tmp/test/fixtures/script-shim`, which does not exist. `writeScript` symlinks every fake to it anyway; the module-load `chmodSync` is in a try/catch that swallows the ENOENT. So each fake `pi` was a dangling link. A PATH lookup skips a dangling entry and resolves the next `pi` on PATH: the real one, whose `~/.pi/agent/settings.json` names `omlx` as `defaultProvider`. The same fall-through applies to every other fake the suite installs (git, npm, and the rest).
- **How to reproduce:** in a worktree, `npx tsc --outDir "$(mktemp -d)/d"`, then `node --test <that dir>/test/pi.test.js` with pi installed. `ls -la $TMPDIR/tumwater-test-run-*/fake-pi-*/pi` shows links to a nonexistent `…/test/fixtures/script-shim`, and `lsof -nP -iTCP:8000` shows the spawned pi connecting. Pointing `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` at an empty directory does NOT keep a model from answering — a 2026-09-30 review check found real pi 0.87.1 still ran a real agent that way, picking provider `huggingface` from the inherited `HF_TOKEN` (the agent dir holds configured providers; env-token providers need none). No environment flag disarms the real pi, so the guard must be the loud import failure, not fall-through mitigation.
- **Expected:** a fake command either runs its body or its test fails loudly. A fixture file missing from where the compiled tree assumed must never let a test reach a real agent, git, or npm.
- **Fix direction (as landed 2026-09-30):** test/fake-commands.ts checks at module load that `SCRIPT_SHIM` exists and throws with the resolved path and the rule: compiled tests run from a `dist/` inside the checkout, so no `--outDir` elsewhere — a relocated tree fails on its first import instead of quietly disabling every fake (test/fake-commands.test.ts copies the compiled module into a scratch relocated `dist/test` and pins the refusal). The entry's original part (2) — setting `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` to an empty scratch dir wherever the suite drops `TUMWATER_PI_BIN`, so a fall-through pi "finds no providers and exits at once" — was retracted after review: an empty agent dir leaves pi's built-in and env-token providers (e.g. `huggingface` via an inherited `HF_TOKEN`) fully live, so it neuters nothing, and no environment flag disarms the real pi. Separately, PLANS.md's `npm run test:coverage` plan removes the reason to build out of tree at all.

- **Validation gap:** real-run-needed — the in-checkout suite was structurally blind to this (it always runs beside the fixtures, and every test stayed green while its fakes were dangling links), so confirming the bug took a real coverage-loop run whose 291 agents surfaced as live traffic on the oMLX server; the landed regression test reproduces the relocation offline by importing a copy of the compiled module from a scratch `dist/test`.
### The budget gate could engage the fallback while every observer still showed spend under the cap: the scheduler summed its runners' in-memory states — which charge each pi run's cost the moment the run folds — while the dashboards read the loop-state file persisted only at the saves; the gate now publishes its own spentUsd/capUsd into the orchestrator info file on change, and the snapshot prefers that published figure while the fleet runs (found by director 2026-09-30 while answering a user question — "why is the oMLX fallback running when the budget has not been spent today?" — fixed 2026-09-30)

- **Symptom:** role loops ran on the free fallback (oMLX visibly serving requests) while the TUI/GUI header still read `· budget: $X/$Y today` with X under the cap — no `· fallback:` fragment, so nothing on screen explained the oMLX traffic. The event feed told the two apart: `tumwater logs --grep budget_fallback` stamped the `spentUsd`/`capUsd` the gate actually tripped on at the transition.
- **Cause:** two live views over one data source. The gate (`src/orchestrator.ts` passes `runners.map((r) => r.state)` to `pollBudgetGate` → `fleetDailyCost`, `src/budget-gates.ts`/`src/budget.ts`) summed the in-memory `LoopState` objects that `foldUsage` charges run-by-run (`src/tick-usage.ts` `fold` → `recordDailyCost`) as each pi run of an in-flight tick completes; every observer (`src/ui/status.ts` → the state files on disk) read the persisted copies, written only at the loops' `save()` points. With `maxConcurrent` ticks in flight plus landing runs folding into their authoring loops, the gate's figure crossed the cap minutes before any persisted file showed it: the fallback engaged while every dashboard, the per-loop `today` columns, and `tumwater report` still read under cap.
- **Fix:** the gate's own figures are now first-class. `budgetSpend` (src/budget.ts) returns the `{spentUsd, capUsd}` pair the gate evaluates — `budgetPaused` is `budgetReached(budgetSpend(...))`, one definition — and `pollBudgetGate` returns the pair with its decision (the same single read serves the verdict, the transition event, and the return). The orchestrator publishes it into the orchestrator info file whenever it changes, beside the demotion it already published (removed with the file at exit, so no stale figure survives a stop). `snapshot` prefers the published spend while the orchestrator is alive (`running && info.budget`) and keeps the persisted sum when it is not — a stopped fleet's files are final. The rendered totals row reads the same budget block as the header badge, so table and badge cannot drift; the per-loop `today` cells keep their persisted copies, so while ticks are in flight the cells can sit under the header/total figure — that visible gap is exactly the in-flight charge the gate has already counted, displayed instead of hidden.
- **Tests:** test/budget-gates.test.ts asserts the returned pair on an open poll and on the tripped-on fallback poll; test/status.test.ts publishes $60 over a $1 persisted sum and asserts the snapshot, the header badge, the lagging per-loop cell, the agreeing total row, and that a dead-pid marker is not trusted; test/orchestrator-budget.e2e.test.ts asserts the live orchestrator's published block after the paused poll.
- **Left as designed:** the midnight shape — an in-flight tick keeps the fallback config it was assigned (`r.config` is set per poll, not per run), so a tick that started under yesterday's spent budget keeps running oMLX past local midnight while the fresh day's badge reads `$0.00`. The publish makes the scheduler's spend legible; it does not re-assign mid-tick configs.

**Validation gap:** real-run-needed — the divergence exists only mid-tick between the scheduler's memory and the persisted files, so the offline suite tested each view in isolation and passed while the bug stood; only the live fleet exposed it, and the new regression tests pin the publish path the fix created, not the old failure itself.

### `history --grep` with zero matches prints the empty-log prose — `no ticks yet` or `no ticks in 2h` — falsely telling the operator the log or window held no ticks: a grep over scanned rows that matched nothing must name the pattern, as its sibling `logs --grep` already does with `no events matching "<pattern>"` (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-30 latent-bug hunt over the same day's `history --grep` feature commit 2ba41a49, fixed 2026-09-30)

- **Symptom:** on a log full of ticks, `history -n 2 --grep widget` printed `no ticks yet` and `history --since 1h --grep old work` printed `no ticks in 1h` — both false statements about a log the command had just scanned and filtered, so an operator grepping for a failure summary reads a quiet fleet (or an empty window) instead of `no match`. The sibling view states the truth (`logs --grep` with no matches: `no events matching "<pattern>"`, src/ui/log-commands.ts), breaking the parity the feature's own doc comment claims ("both observing views answer substring questions the same way"); the feature's tests even pinned the wrong prose as "the existing empty-log prose, unchanged in wording".
- **Suspected cause:** the empty-rows branch predates `--grep` and was reused unchanged for the filtered case, so the grep-empty case borrowed wording whose claim ("the log/window is empty") it cannot make.
- **Reproduce:** seed a repo with matching ticks (test/cli-history.test.ts's seededHistoryRepo) and run `history --grep "no such text"` — pre-fix output: `no ticks yet`, while the same repo without `--grep` prints three rows.
- **Fix:** when `--grep` is present and no row matches, `cmdHistory` prints `no ticks matching "<pattern>"` in both the -n and --since shapes (src/ui/history.ts); the non-grep empty prose (`no ticks yet` / `no ticks in <window>`) is untouched, and `--json` stays a bare `{"rows":[]}` document. Regression-tested in test/cli-history.test.ts (the four grep-empty shapes now assert the pattern-naming wording; a plain `--since` empty window still asserts the old prose, so the branches cannot drift into each other).
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the suite was green while asserting the misleading prose, because the invariant the wording guards (a filtered-empty result must never read as an empty log or window) had to be reconstructed from the logs --grep sibling before the pinned `no ticks yet` assertions read as a gap.

### A wide-shallow connection storm — many roles each failing a few times on one backend kind — still trips no fleet-level alarm (part 2/2 of the 2026-09-29 connection-storm entry; its sibling per-run warning floor is Fixed below): every alarm bar the fleet has for this failure kind needs a depth or coalescence the spread shape has none of — add the windowed fleet-wide alarm the fix sketch's part (2) named (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-29, decomposed and fixed 2026-09-30)

- **Symptom:** the 2026-09-29 digest's Top error clusters carry **10×** `bugfix, clean, feature, improve +3 more — Connection error.` — ten errored ticks on one normalized cause across seven roles — and the digest's warning section (17 clusters) names no connection-related cluster among its ten listed: no per-run warning, no per-role `consecutive tick failures` alarm for it, no fleet hold in the state section. The 429 twin cluster (**6×** `... — Rate limit exceeded ...`) is the control: it is accompanied by **29×** `provider rate-limited the request (429, no hint — waiting 60s) — retrying the pi run once` and a `3 consecutive tick failures` alarm, so an identical-magnitude storm on that kind is loud while this one is silent (at most one warning for this cause could sit in the 7 unlisted single-line warnings, against 30 listed for the 429 twin). The only fleet-level alarm of the day for the connection kind is the budget breaker's `budget paused — 3 ticks failed on fallback omlx/Qwen3.8-27B-MLX-oQ4` (20:43), which fired only because the fallback was engaged (`$15.02 of $15.00 daily cost reached`, 20:41); a connection storm on the primary model would have alarmed nothing. Cost of the day's whole error class (connection + 429 + review failures): 0.6 h · $0.21 across the nine roles that errored.
- **Why the response was wrong:** this is the 2026-09-15 entry in Fixed — `Repeated tick failures raise no alarm: 44 errors across all 13 loops looked exactly like a quiet fleet` (commit 4dba580) — re-enacting in the shape that fix never covered. The 09-15 fix alarms on DEPTH within one role: one `N consecutive tick failures` warning when a role's streak crosses 3 (src/loop.ts, ERROR_STREAK_WARN in src/tick-outcome.ts). The 2026-09-22 meltdown's fleet-wide extension — the error-storm warning (src/error-storm.ts: 3+ roles EACH at 3+ consecutive on one normalized key, merged 2026-09-29 15:40 fb5669d3) — also alarms only when several roles stand deep. A WIDE-SHALLOW storm — many roles each failing a few times, the shape a degraded local model backend produces against a whole fleet — trips neither: no role reaches 3 consecutive, and no three roles stand at 3. The 2-in-2-minutes backend hold (src/fleet-hold.ts) coalesces only where two failures fall inside the window. And the per-run path is silent for this kind BY DESIGN: src/loop-pi.ts's runWithTransientRetry retries and warns exactly three transient kinds (server timeout, pi crash, 429); a backend-kind failure (src/pi-stream.ts's backendKind: connection/timeout/server/model-load) gets neither the retry nor a warning line, so the storm's only trace is per-tick error events the operator must aggregate by hand. On the day it happened, the running build predated the two fleet-level alarms entirely: the last self-restart was 15:00 (the ENOENT-pin correction entry in Fixed: `the redeployer then restarted itself at 01:17 and 15:00`), so at 20:41 the running process carried the 429-ONLY hold and no storm warning — the 12 h restart cooldown (the 2× `auto-restart of 35b027d4 deferred — cooldown until 2026-09-29T20:17:54` warnings in the digest) kept the 15:40/16:33 fixes out of the running process until the operator's 21:47 restart onto 8c1a5764. The silent ten were spread across that blind window.
- **Suspected cause:** alarm coverage is defined per STREAK (consecutive within a role) or per deep COINCIDENCE (3 roles × 3), never per WINDOW (failures fleet-wide on one normalized cause within a time span), so a degraded backend can fail the fleet for hours without crossing any bar; the backend-kind per-run path's silence (no retry, no warning — part (1) of this entry, now Fixed) removed the last per-run floor. The 20:41–20:43 window shows the missing floor in practice: three fast fallback-tick failures alarmed only through the budget breaker, which is fallback-engagement-conditional and cost-metered — a connection storm near the spend cap presents as budget trouble, not as a backend failure.
- **Reproduce:** the 2026-09-29 digest is the recorded instance — normalized key `Connection error.`, 10× across bugfix/clean/feature/improve +3 more, no warning cluster for it (the per-run floor of part (1) is now Fixed below and would have named each failure; the fleet-level silence remains), correlated merged commits fb5669d3 (error-storm warning, 15:40) and 5377e34d (hold generalization, 16:33), both landed AFTER the last self-restart (15:00) and therefore absent from the running build when the 20:41–20:43 connection window fired. Synthetic repro against the current tree: drive ≥6 roles' ticks to fail on `Connection error.` spread — each role 1–2 times, no role 3 consecutive, failures spaced >2 min apart — and the code emits no warning line for the episode: per-run none (backend kind excluded from runWithTransientRetry), per-role streak not reached, storm needs 3 roles × 3, hold needs 2-in-2-min.
- **Fix:** a fourth alarm bar, the windowed fleet-wide spread alarm (src/failure-spread.ts, wired as pollFailureSpread in src/tick-timing.ts beside its two siblings and called from the orchestrator poll): 6 failures (FAILURE_SPREAD_COUNT) of one provider failure kind — the rate-limit and backend kinds the fleet hold groups by — within 30 min (FAILURE_SPREAD_WINDOW_MS), counting raw failures across roles regardless of any role's streak depth and deduplicating per (role, kind, at) so a runner's re-read latest-failure observation counts once. Edge-triggered like every warning: one warning event per episode (a different kind becoming the episode names itself), a silent clear and re-arm. The recorded repro now trips: 10× `Connection error.` across 7 roles spaced past the hold's 2-minute window, no role at 3 consecutive. Regression-tested in test/failure-spread.test.ts (the wide-shallow shape, the window edge, the dedupe, per-kind separation, the re-arm, the new-kind episode, and the poll seam's one-event-per-episode).
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — nothing in the suite failed, since every existing bar (per-role streak, 3×3 error storm, 2-in-2-min hold) had its own passing tests; the invariant the missing bar guards — a spread of same-kind failures across roles leaves a fleet-visible trace at ANY role depth — had to be reconstructed from the digest's cluster shape before the absence read as a gap.

### A backend-kind tick failure (connection down, 5xx, model load) warned nothing at the per-run level — part 1/2 of the 2026-09-29 connection-storm entry: `runWithTransientRetry` retried and warned exactly three transient kinds (server timeout, pi crash, 429) and let a backend-kind failure fall through silently, so the storm's only trace was per-tick error events an operator had to aggregate by hand; the per-run floor now warns on every failed backend-kind run (`provider backend failure (connection error) — the transient retry does not cover this kind; the fleet-wide hold watches for a storm`, src/loop-pi.ts), no retry — a dead backend is not something an immediate re-run beats, and the fleet-wide hold and storm alarms judge the spread (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-29, decomposed and this half fixed 2026-09-30)

- **Symptom:** the 2026-09-29 digest's Top error clusters carried 10× `Connection error.` across 7 roles with no warning line for it, while the 429 twin cluster (6×) carried 29 retry warnings and a streak alarm — the per-run path was silent for the backend kinds by design (see the sibling spread-alarm entry above for the full diagnosis; its remaining half, the fleet-level spread alarm, is Fixed 2026-09-30 too).
- **Fix:** a warning line on the uncovered backend-kind path in `runWithTransientRetry`, parity with the 429 branch: any episode is now visible from the feed alone. A run that merely saw a backend error mid-stream and still completed ok stays silent — nothing failed, nothing to warn about. Regression-tested in test/loop-pi.test.ts (warns once and earns no retry; the recovered run stays silent).
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the suite pinned the three covered transient kinds end to end but nothing stated the invariant this floor guards (every failed run leaves a feed-visible trace), so the silent path had to be reconstructed from the digest before the missing warning was visible as a gap.

### The operator-stop drain test fails intermittently with `the drain waited out the reserved tick`: test/tick-timing.test.ts's `drainInFlightWork on an operator stop waits out ticks and landings` failed its assert 5 times on record (2026-09-28 12:50 → 2026-09-29 01:20 PDT), every occurrence inside a landing gate check and every retry run green (found by director log analysis 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-30)

- **Repro:** full-suite runs under fleet load only (`gate check failed then passed on retry — flaky` ×5 in the live fleet's events.jsonl); never in isolation.
- **Pinned cause:** all five failures predate commit 53745873 (2026-09-29 01:50 PDT — the last failure is 01:20) and ran that commit's *old* assertion: an elapsed-wall-time check `Date.now() - startedAt >= 30` carrying the same message, with `startedAt` read *after* the 30 ms timer was created — so a tick settling at timer-creation+30 ms leaves the later-read `startedAt` as little as 29 ms behind it (1 ms of event-loop stall between the two statements, routine under full-suite load, or libuv's ~1 ms timer granularity, either suffices). The record's "tickSettledAt still 0 at the assert" was a paraphrase against the post-fix settled-flag assert, which is why the mechanism looked impossible ("the composition shown cannot produce it") — the composition shown was not the one that failed.
- **Fix:** already landed as 53745873 (2026-09-29), test-only: the elapsed checks in both drain tests became settled-flag asserts (`tickSettledAt > 0` / `landingSettledAt > 0`), which cannot race — the timer callback assigns the flag before resolving, and the drain's `await Promise.allSettled([...])` cannot return before that resolution, so the restart-forever regression guard keeps its full strength. No code change in this close-out; the regression test is 53745873's own updated assertion (10 consecutive isolated runs green, 3/3 pass, and the built dist copy verified current).
- **Validation gap:** no-repro — the race is a sub-millisecond load-timing flake that never reproduced in isolation, so the close-out was confirmed by correlating the failures' window (last 01:20 PDT) with commit 53745873's landing (01:50 PDT) and the airtight settled-flag semantics, not by a reproduction.

### The `logs -f` follow e2e test intermittently fails the landing gate: `logs -f prints the current window and follows newly appended events` (test/cli-logs.test.ts) blew test/cli-harness.ts's default 10 s `waitFor` deadline 5 times on record — the failed runs ran 10.4–11.9 s, just past the deadline, all inside landing gate checks under full-suite load, and every retry run passed the same test green (found by director log analysis 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-30)

- **Repro:** run the full suite while the machine is under the fleet's own load (all 5 recorded occurrences are `gate check failed then passed on retry — flaky` warnings in the live fleet's events.jsonl, 2026-09-25 05:50 → 2026-09-29 17:24 PDT; it never failed in an isolated rerun). The spawned `tumwater logs -f` child must start up, render the seeded window, poll (500 ms), and deliver the followed event within the 10 s `waitFor` (ms param, test/cli-harness.ts line 61's default); under parallel-suite load the deadline expires.
- **Cost:** each flake cost a landing a second full suite run (2,266 tests) plus the reviewer's re-check — the single largest recurring landing cost in the day's failure digest.
- **Fix:** test-only, no src change: the two `waitFor` calls in that test now pass an explicit 30 s deadline instead of the harness's 10 s default (~3× headroom over the observed 10.4–11.9 s worst cases), so the spawned child's startup and the 500 ms follow poll have room under load; the product's follow path is untouched, and the other waitFors keep the tight default so real hangs still fail fast.
- **Validation gap:** no-repro — the flake reproduced only under the live fleet's full-suite load (5 recorded gate occurrences, every isolated rerun green), so no deterministic offline check could confirm the widened deadline; only continued landing-gate runs under load can.

### `tumwater help --help` / `help -h` fails with "no help topic: --help" (exit 1) while every other command prints its own topic: the dispatcher intercept that wired the flag to all commands on 2026-09-29 carried an explicit `command !== "help"` exclusion, so the flag fell through to `help`'s topic lookup for the literal flag text — exit 1 and "did you mean `help`?" for the one command whose own topic the listing does name (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's --help feature commit, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** commit 80c749e2's intercept condition excluded `help` with no reason recorded in the commit message, the code comment, or PLANS.md; its regression tests asserted the convention for status/pause/diff but never for `help` itself, so the exclusion shipped untested and its failure — a plausible-looking "no help topic" error for a non-topic — read as intended rather than as a gap.
- **Fix:** src/cli.ts's intercept no longer excludes `help`, so `--help`/`-h` answers with the help command's own topic (exit 0, before any repo gate, like every other command); `help` bare and `help <command>` are untouched. Regression test in test/cli.test.ts asserts both spellings print the `tumwater help [<command>]` stanza with exit 0.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the commit message and the code comment both claimed the flag reached "every command at once" while the code carved out `help`; the intended contract (help answers the flag with its own topic, since the listing names a `help` stanza and `help help` works) had to be reconstructed from those before the exclusion was confirmable as a bug rather than a deliberate special case.

### A suite run in the live fleet's own checkout detached `tumwater gui` from self-reload for good: bugfix tick 558 ran `cd /Users/zach/tumwater && npm run test` from outside its worktree (15:48 PDT), which recompiled and restamped that checkout's dist/, and then test/cli-gui.test.ts's reload e2e wrote a fake `"e".repeat(40)` stamp into the same dist/build-info.json — the live dashboard re-exec'd onto it, captured "eeee…" as its startup build, and because the watch armed only when the startup sha resolved to a commit, never watched again: /api/status reported `serverBuildSha: "eeee…"` and the page stayed old through every later redeploy, curable only by a hand restart (found by human log analysis 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** three layers. The reload e2e ran the real CLI against the checkout running the suite and swapped that checkout's real stamp (restoring it in finally), so any suite run where a live gui serves drove that gui. Nothing kept suites out of that checkout: the tick prompt's worktree rule put the borrowed install "two levels up (`../../node_modules`)", which from `.tumwater/worktrees/<role>` is `.tumwater/` itself — the bugfix run followed it (`cd ../..`), got empty `git show` output there, and fell back to the checkout's absolute path for every later command, the suite included (the retained sessions hold one more such suite run, readme 2026-09-23). And the watch re-exec'd onto ANY changed stamp, bogus ones included, while refusing to arm for a startup stamp whose commit does not resolve — so one bogus stamp detached a dashboard permanently.
- **Fix:** the reload e2e now serves a copy of `dist/src` from a fixture repo stamped with the fixture's own commits, never touching the running checkout's dist/ or tumwater.json (test/doctor.test.ts's `harness build` mask existed only for the swap and is gone). `npm test` and `npm run test:e2e` start with scripts/live-checkout-guard.mjs, which refuses to run where `.tumwater/state/orchestrator.json` names a live pid. The tick/director rule derives the root's path from worktreePath (`../../..`) and says to run commands from the worktree (or a scratch temp dir), never cd to the repo root, and never build, test, or edit files in that primary checkout. src/ui/self-reload.ts re-execs only onto a stamp naming a real commit of the repo (one git call per new stamp) and arms for a startup stamp compiled from this root even when its commit does not resolve, so a dashboard already on a bogus stamp recovers on the next real build.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the watch disarmed silently (no event, no log line); only the unresolvable `serverBuildSha` in /api/status gave it away, and the chain had to be rebuilt from the pi session's command log and the 15:48:19 dist stamp.

### An epitaph bullet's completion is its trailing parenthetical's FIRST dated verb, so a sibling cross-reference inside the epitaph steals the day: the heading branch takes the meta's LAST dated verb (the heading-side fix of 2026-09-29), but the bullet branch still used `tail.match(dateRe)` — an epitaph like "- <desc> (decomposed from the sibling bug fixed 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-29; commit abc1234)" matched "fixed 2026-09-28" and counted on the sibling's day (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's two entryDates fixes, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** commit 02ed37a2 fixed the first-verb steal for `###` headings only; the `- ` branch's `tail.match(dateRe)` kept the same first-match semantics. A decomposition cross-reference conventionally sits inside the trailing parenthetical ahead of the entry's own completion record, so the sibling's date won whenever the sibling landed first. Reproduced with a failing regression test (the epitaph counted on the sibling's day, the entry's own day stayed 0) before the fix.
- **Fix:** src/report-data.ts's bullet branch now scans ALL matches of `dateRe` in the tail and takes the last, mirroring the heading branch; the date+commit epitaph guard is unchanged, so body bullets still count nowhere. Regression test in test/report.test.ts covers the steal shape symmetric to the heading test.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the heading fix's entry claimed the last-verb rule applied to "PLANS.md's `done <date>` and BUGS.md's completion verbs alike" without saying the bullet branch still took the first match, so the shape had to be reconstructed from the code before the residual inconsistency was confirmable as a bug rather than intended bullet/heading asymmetry.

### An epitaph bullet whose trailing parenthetical quotes a nested parenthetical silently drops its completion from the day report: the epitaph guard's flat `\([^()]*\)$` match saw only the innermost group, so a Done/Fixed line like "- <desc> (planned 2026-09-25, done 2026-09-28; commit 00cf2e98 (re-landed after review fix))" matched "(re-landed after review fix)" — text carrying neither the date nor the commit reference — and the entry counted nowhere (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's entryDates last-verb fix, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** `entryDates`' bullet branch extracted the epitaph tail with `line.match(/\(([^()]*)\)\s*$/)` — a parenthetical that itself quotes a parenthetical (a natural shape: a commit reference followed by a re-land/review parenthetical) ends in two closes, the flat match landed on the innermost group, and the date+commit guard failed silently. Scratch reproduction: a nested-paren epitaph bullet yielded `tail = ""` and counted nowhere; the sibling's day report undercounted with no error.
- **Fix:** src/report-data.ts replaces the flat match with a nesting-aware backward scan (`trailingParenthetical`) that returns the trailing group's FULL inner text — unbalanced text still yields "" so a body bullet stays uncounted. Regression test: a nested-paren epitaph counts on its date while a following body bullet with a bare dated parenthetical still counts nowhere.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the undercount left no trace (no assertion, no warning; the report printed a plausible smaller number) and no backlog fixture ever contained a nested parenthetical, so the shape was invisible until a scratch reproduction compared the epitaph's parsed tail against the report's own output.

### A backlog heading that mentions a sibling's completion steals the entry's count onto the sibling's day: `entryDates` took the FIRST verb+date match in a `###` entry's joined meta, so a heading like "found by qa 2026-09-29, decomposed from the X bug fixed 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-29" counted on 2026-09-28 — the day report moved the fix to the wrong day (or dropped it from the window) whenever a sibling landed the day before, for PLANS.md's `done <date>` and BUGS.md's completion verbs alike (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's re-landed-verb fix, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** the `###` branch of `entryDates` used `meta.join(" ").match(dateRe)` — first match wins — while the completion record is conventionally the heading meta's LAST dated verb (found-by, decomposition, and sibling mentions all precede it). The 2026-09-29 re-landed-verb fix explicitly guarded bare "landed <date>" sibling mentions because "a sibling's date is not this entry's", but a sibling mention riding a counted verb (`fixed`/`resolved`/`done <date>`) still won the match. Scratch reproduction: a Fixed heading with a sibling "fixed <d-1>" mention before its own "fixed <d>" counted on <d-1>.
- **Fix:** src/report-data.ts takes the last `dateRe` match of the heading meta instead of the first (bullets are unchanged — they keep the trailing-parenthetical-with-commit-reference epitaph guard). Regression test: a Fixed heading whose sibling mention carries an earlier dated `fixed` counts on the entry's own later date, and the sibling's day stays at zero.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the existing fixtures never put two dated verbs in one heading, so the suite encoded first-match behavior without pinning which of a heading's verb+date mentions is the entry's completion; that invariant had to be reconstructed from the collector's intent (and the sibling-date comment) before the fix could be shaped.

### The day report silently undercounts `bugsFixed` when a Fixed entry records its completion with a verb outside the collector's list: the `tsc exited ENOENT` entry — the fleet's biggest fix of 2026-09-29 — says "re-landed 2026-09-29", the collector matched only `fixed|closed|resolved <date>`, and `tumwater report` showed 27 bugs fixed that day when the Fixed section holds 28 (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's report-data work, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** `collectReport`'s BUGS.md completion regex pinned the completion vocabulary to three verbs, but the bugfix prompt pins the completion DATE, not the verb — and the re-land flow (the harness's own feature) writes "re-landed <date>" epitaphs for entries whose first landing was rejected in review. A rejected landing is not a fix; the re-land is. The match failed silently and the day report rendered a plausible-looking smaller number.
- **Fix:** src/report-data.ts adds `re-landed` to the completion-verb alternation, with the vocabulary rationale in a comment (bare "landed <date>" stays out — headings mention sibling landings, and a sibling's date is not this entry's). Regression test: a Fixed heading completed as "re-landed <date>" counts on that date; a heading saying only "was fixed" with no dated verb counts nowhere.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the undercount left no failing trace anywhere (no assertion, no warning; the report just printed a plausible smaller number), so confirmation rested on a scratch reproduction diffing the real BUGS.md's dated entries against the report's own output; the new regression test now pins it.

### The rebuild's compile spawn failing in the running build's context (the `tsc exited ENOENT` cluster: 30× `rebuild of <sha> failed — staying on build <sha>` over 2026-09-29 while the same tree compiled all day in landing build checks) cannot latch a false verdict or pin the fleet anymore, and the one spawn failure a retry can beat now recovers by itself: a spawn that never produced a process — a string code like ENOENT whatever output rode with it, or a tsc that died by signal — returns `rejected`, which the redeployer retries without blocking, so a transient environmental glitch no longer names a good commit as the failure and no longer pins the fleet past the glitch; the vanished-interpreter shape (process.execPath renamed by an upgrade while the fleet stays up — it kills only the spawns that use it directly while every PATH-resolved spawn keeps working, which fits the observed pattern exactly) retries through tsc's own shebang (env node resolved at spawn time), so the next rebuild succeeds and the catch-22 breaks: a rejection no longer blocks activation, attempts keep retrying, and the first success activates main's fixes (an operator restart does the same); a never-ran spawn also names every missing input — interpreter, cwd, toolchain — so the next occurrence is a diagnosis instead of a mystery (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-29; decomposed 2026-09-29 when the sustained-pin-visibility half was fixed; the spawn-classification and recovery half re-landed 2026-09-29 after a review objection to the fallback's classification was addressed)

- **Cause:** compileStaged's catch formatted any non-numeric `e.code` as `tsc exited ENOENT` with no evidence of what was missing, and the 48835efc classification (`typeof e.code !== "number" && !tail`) still read two never-ran shapes as compiler verdicts about the tree: a spawn failure carrying buffered output, and a tsc killed by signal (a null code) — either latching a false verdict about a good commit and blocking the head.
- **Fix:** src/build-stage.ts classifies by shape — a numeric exit is the only compiler verdict; signal deaths and spawn failures are rejections; when the interpreter itself is missing and the mirror exists, the compile retries through tsc's shebang, with the fallback's own outcome classified by the same invariant (its numeric exit is a verdict, never a rejection — the review objection); every rejection names the missing spawn inputs. Regression tests: the end-to-end vanished-interpreter rebuild, the missing-input diagnosis, a signal death with output attached, the fallback's numeric exit staying a verdict, and the fallback's own spawn failure naming both attempts.
- **Validation gap:** no-repro — the live fleet's actual cause never reproduced offline, so confirmation rested on constructed spawn shapes (vanished interpreter, missing cwd, signal death, fallback outcomes) exercised end-to-end against real tsc; whether the live pin clears on the next successful rebuild is observable only in a real bounded run.
- **Correction — the live incident, reconstructed afterward from events.jsonl:** the pin was already over when telemetry filed this at 2026-09-29 19:21, and the evidence fits the vanished-interpreter shape above exactly. The redeployer restarted onto build 66afeacd at 2026-09-28 03:52:58 PDT, starting its 12 h restart cooldown; Homebrew installed node 26.10.0_1 at 03:55:15 (its INSTALL_RECEIPT time), leaving it the only keg in /opt/homebrew/Cellar/node, so the `process.execPath` that 66afeacd's compileStaged spawned tsc through no longer existed. The first rebuild the cooldown allowed (15:53:03) died with `tsc exited ENOENT`, and so did all 109 through 2026-09-29 01:10:54, while the PATH-resolved `npm` landing checks kept passing. A hand restart (`orchestrator_stop` 01:11:47, `orchestrator_start` 01:11:51, no redeploy `restart` event) moved the fleet onto the new node at build 4b0ff35d, which already carried 48835efc and 13a8a36b; the redeployer then restarted itself at 01:17 and 15:00, with no failed rebuild through 21:15. The filing read the day digest's 24 h window, which still held the pre-restart warnings.

### A tick_end event's durationMs comes up undefined once per few full-suite runs: the error-tick test's `typeof ends[0]?.durationMs === "number"` failed in one `npm run test` run (dist/test/loop.test.js:470, ERR_ASSERTION actual 'undefined' expected 'number') and passed on the two following full runs and the isolated rerun (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 while validating the followFile rotation-drain fix, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** src/loop.ts logged `durationMs` only when `tickDurationMs > 0` — the omit-when-zero convention copied from the usage fields — but tick_end's renderer never prints the span, so the omission bought nothing while breaking the contract failure-data.ts's fold and the error-tick test both rely on: a tick that ends within the same `Date.now()` millisecond it started (an instant early-abort error tick, the shape a loaded parallel suite produces stochastically) or crosses a backward clock step dropped the field entirely.
- **Fix:** tick_end always carries `durationMs` (Math.max(0, …) keeps a backward clock step honest at 0); a regression test freezes Date via node:test mock timers around an error tick, pins the same-millisecond span deterministically, and fails with the reported `actual: 'undefined'` shape against the old conditional.
- **Validation gap:** no-repro — the suite reproduced it only stochastically (one red in a handful of full-suite runs, then three consecutive greens), so confirming it required building a deterministic same-millisecond repro (frozen Date.now via mock timers) that the existing test lacked.

### The sustained self-redeploy pin is invisible in aggregate: ~109 per-head "rebuild of <sha> failed — staying on build <sha>: tsc exited ENOENT" warnings over ~9 h name a different commit each time, `build_stale` fires only on the transition into staleness, and no warning ever says the fleet itself is pinned — the redeployer now escalates: once a build has stayed continuously stale past 6 h AND a restart attempt has failed during that same stale episode, one warning names the pin itself ("build <sha> has stayed stale for ~N h (M commits behind main) while rebuild attempts keep failing — the sustained pin itself is the problem, not any one head"), repeating at most daily; a red-main verdict is a correct deferral and never feeds it, and healthy churn (landed restarts, cooldown deferrals) produces no failed attempt so never trips it (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Cause:** every existing signal was head-scoped — per-head block warnings, a one-shot staleness transition, a static `restartBlocked` badge — so the aggregate "stale for a day, every rebuild dead" never crossed any threshold anywhere the operator reads. The escalation is keyed to the stale EPISODE instead (staleSince/lastRestartFailureAt/lastEscalationAt in src/redeploy.ts), with the failure marker set uniformly by every dead-end shape — refused boot gate (both asks share refuse(), so neither can be forgotten), a green check or compile that could not run, a failed compile verdict, a swap error — and evaluated before poll's early returns, so a latched block on a frozen main still escalates.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** the escalation in src/redeploy.ts plus regression tests in test/redeploy.test.ts that drive 31 simulated hours of the incident's shapes — a failing compile verdict on every head while main churns every 5 minutes (escalates once at 6 h, again at ~30 h, beside the per-head noise), a blocked head on a frozen main (same cadence with no further failures), a pin sustained purely at the refused boot gate — and the never-escalate shapes: a red main for 12 h, and 31 h of healthy churn whose restarts land inside the cooldown.
- **Validation gap:** no-fake — confirming the escalation needed a simulated multi-hour churn driver (a fresh failing head every five minutes with an advancing clock) that the suite's one-episode redeploy fixtures did not provide; a first version keyed to per-head failure timestamps passed its own single-episode tests while never firing in the churn scenario, and only the multi-episode driver exposed that.

### `tumwater tui` on a pty with no window size renders a wholly blank dashboard: the frame budget read `stdout.rows ?? 40` / `stdout.columns ?? 120`, and `??` passed a *reported 0* through as a real size, so every width-clipped line collapsed to empty and the operator saw only the clear-screen and the bare `director › ` prompt (found by qa 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** launching `tumwater tui` inside any pty whose window size was never set (macOS `script -q /dev/null tumwater tui`, some CI pty allocators, a multiplexer pane before its first resize) showed a clear screen, two dozen blank lines, and only the `director › ` prompt prefix — no header, no fleet table, no tab strip, no hint line, no error.
- **Cause:** `render()` in src/ui/tui.ts took the frame budget with `??`: a pty that reports 0×0 (no `TIOCSWINSZ` ever applied) passed both zeroes through as legitimate sizes, so `toneLine`/`renderStatusSpans` clipped every line to 0 columns and only the un-clipped prompt prefix survived. The gui reads sizes from the browser viewport, not a pty, and has no counterpart.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** the two `??` fallbacks in `render()` became `||` (src/ui/tui.ts), so a reported 0 degrades to the same sane defaults an unset size gets (40 rows × 120 columns). The fake-TTY harness in test/tui.test.ts gained an optional size override (guarded by `!== undefined`, since 0 is a real report), and a regression test renders the dashboard with rows=0/columns=0 and asserts the tab strip and fleet table survive; it fails under the old `??` code and passes under the fix.
- **Validation gap:** no-fake — confirming the fix needed a stdout that reports a 0×0 window, and the fake-TTY harness hard-coded 100×40 with no way to fake a degenerate report; the real-pty repro (macOS `script`) showed the symptom but could not run in the offline suite until the harness gained the size override.

### A rotation silently drops the lines a follow never consumed from the outgoing inode: `followFile` detects rotation by the dev/ino change, resets its offset to 0, and reads only the fresh file — but lines appended between its last poll and the rename sit in the renamed `<file>.1` archive at byte offsets the follow had not yet reached, so `logs -f` loses exactly the events written just before a 16 MB rotation, with no error or gap marker (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's three follow-seeding fixes, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `tumwater logs -f` promises to deliver every complete line at or past its seed exactly once, but a rotation (the events log's 16 MB cap, a role log's `logMaxBytes`) abandons the outgoing inode's unread tail: the poll that detects the inode change stats only the fresh file, so any lines appended between the previous poll and `rotateIfLarge`'s rename are delivered by neither the old-inode read nor the fresh-file read — silently, in exactly the view an operator runs while watching a busy fleet.
- **Reproduce:** deterministic: start a follow past a file's existing content, let one poll consume it, then synchronously `appendFileSync(file, "missed one\nmissed two\n")` immediately followed by `renameSync(file, file + ".1")` and `writeFileSync(file, "")` — no poll can run between the synchronous calls, so the next poll sees only the fresh inode. Before the fix the follow delivered only the fresh file's lines; the two missed lines never arrived.
- **Cause:** the inode-change branch did `offset = 0` and nothing else: the outgoing inode's unread byte range was dropped along with the stale offset, although the rotation leaves those bytes readable at the same offsets in the `<file>.1` archive `rotateIfLarge` writes. The two existing rotation tests only exercised appends after the rename, never the append-before-rename window.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** on the inode change the follow now drains the archive first (new `drainRotatedTail` in src/tail.ts): it stats `<file>.1`, trusts it only when its dev/ino matches the pair the offset was measured against (a second rotation inside one poll interval replaces the archive with an older generation, and matching it would deliver that older content as the missed lines — that rarer race keeps the pre-drain behavior), and reads the complete lines from the old offset through the archive's end before resetting to 0, so the missed lines arrive in order ahead of the fresh file's content. A fully-consumed or missing archive drains nothing, so the two existing rotation tests' behavior is unchanged.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the loss is silent (no error, no gap marker, the follow just stops mentioning those lines), and the existing rotation tests seeded from a fully-consumed offset so the append-before-rename window was never exercised; only a regression test that reproduces the window synchronously could confirm the drop and then the fix.

### A multi-byte event line leaves logEvent's post-append shape memo short of the file's real size, so the memo never hits on the fleet's actual traffic and every append silently re-runs the torn-tail check's fd cycle (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's logEvent memoization perf change, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** Since the same day's memoization of the post-append file shape in logEvent, a steady stream of events was supposed to cost one stat per event, not an fd cycle. For any event line containing non-ASCII text (an em dash, an arrow, a curly quote — most real agent output), the memoized size came up short of the file's actual post-append size, so `knownClean` was false on every later append and `terminateTornTail`'s open/fstat/last-byte read ran again each time — silently: no error, no event, no output, only the cost the change claimed to remove, on precisely the traffic the fleet produces.
- **Reproduce:** Deterministic once observable: log two events with multi-byte messages into one root (the first creates the file, the second memoizes the shape), mock `fs.openSync` with a call counter, log a third, and count — 1 open before the fix (the memo missed), 0 after. Arithmetic alone shows the discrepancy: `Buffer.byteLength(JSON.stringify({message: "timed out — 30s over"}) + "\n")` exceeds that string's `.length` by 2.
- **Cause:** `appendEventLine` recorded `st.size + (terminated ? 1 : 0) + line.length`, but `fs.appendFileSync` writes UTF-8 bytes while `line.length` counts UTF-16 code units — every multi-byte character made the recorded shape smaller than reality. The error direction is the safe one (a short memo can only cause a redundant re-check, never a skipped one, so the torn-tail safety property held and the suite stayed green), which is exactly why the loss was invisible.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** The memo records the byte-exact growth, `Buffer.byteLength(line, "utf8")` (src/events.ts), so a multi-byte event's next append stats a file that matches the recorded shape and skips the fd cycle like an ASCII one. Regression test: test/events.test.ts logs two multi-byte events, mocks `fs.openSync`, and asserts the third append opens nothing — with a control that an external append (a shape this process did not leave) misses the memo and opens exactly once, pinning the safety property alongside the win.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the miss degraded silently to the old fd-per-event cost with no error, event, or output to observe, so nothing in the existing suite could confirm the memo ever hit; only a mocked `openSync` call count made the absence visible.

### A follow seeded from `logs -f`'s covered byte end lands mid-line when the log's last line is torn: `readTailTextWithEnd` reported the scan's EOF — every byte it read — as `coveredEnd`, but a trailing torn line (no `\n` yet) is bytes the scan read and never parsed, so a follow seeded there sits past the torn line's start and once its writer completes the line, the follow delivers the line's tail as a garbled fragment with its head never delivered (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's covered-end seeding fix, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `tumwater logs -f` seeded at `coveredEnd` (the same day's fix for the both-neither gap) violates `readCompleteLines`' line-boundary offset contract whenever the events log's last line is torn at scan time — a write in flight, or the window between a crash and the harness's next torn-tail termination. The torn event's head is never delivered, and once its writer appends the rest, follow's next poll reads from mid-line and prints the tail fragment as its own garbled row — in exactly the view an operator runs while watching a failure live.
- **Reproduce:** deterministic: append a complete event line to events.jsonl, then append a partial JSON line with no trailing `\n`; before the fix `readEventsTailWithEnd` returned `coveredEnd === file size` (torn bytes included, 107 vs the line boundary 65). Completing the torn line and seeding `followFile` at that offset delivered only the completing fragment (`,"result":...}`), never the line itself.
- **Cause:** `coveredEnd` was defined as "the file's size at scan time", conflating the bytes the scan read with the bytes it parsed: `scanEventTailWithEnd` holds a torn trailing line back from the parsed events (the same policy as `readCompleteLines`) but still reported EOF as the covered end, and `followFile`'s engine assumes its offset always sits on a line boundary.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** `readTailTextWithEnd` now backs the reported end up to the last newline in the covered bytes — computed on the raw concatenated buffer, byte-exact even when a torn write ends mid-character — and to the covered region's start when it holds no newline at all (that can only be the whole file, since the scan's early stop requires newlines), so a follow seeded from it re-reads and delivers the completed torn line whole, exactly once (src/files.ts). A log ending in `\n` still reports its EOF, so the same-day pinned race test is unchanged. Regression test: test/events.test.ts builds a torn-tail log, pins `coveredEnd` at the torn line's start, and completes the line to assert followFile seeded there delivers it whole and once.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the failure is visible only once the seed's contract is reconstructed: `readCompleteLines` documents "offset always at a line boundary" three modules away from the seeding site and nothing asserted that `coveredEnd` is a boundary, so the existing suite (every fixture ended in `\n`) confirmed the torn-tail shape never.

### An event that lands between the follow view's initial read and its seed stat is printed by neither and silently lost: `cmdLogs` seeds `followFile` from a fresh `statOrNull(file).size` taken after `readEvents` and the print loop, so an event appended in that window lies past the printed tail but at or below the follow's starting offset (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's `logs --since` window work, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `tumwater logs -f` (and `logs --grep <text> -f`, `logs --json -f`) can skip an event the fleet logged in the few milliseconds between the command's initial tail read and its follow seed: the event is newer than everything the initial window printed but older than the byte offset follow starts at, so follow never delivers it and nothing marks the gap — on a fleet whose loops log events every second, an operator watching for a failure can miss exactly the event they are watching for. The `--role` transcript follow does not have this hole (`cmdLogsTranscript` seeds from `tail.end`, the exact offset its initial read covered), so the two follow views' seeding policies disagree.
- **Reproduce:** the append must land inside the synchronous window between `readEvents(root, limit)`'s read and the following `statOrNull(file)` in src/ui/log-commands.ts's follow path, so no black-box CLI sequence lands it deterministically; the code-read confirmation is that the seed is a fresh stat rather than a value derived from the read, so an event written while the file grew from S1 (the read's covered end) to S2 (the seed stat's size) is outside both. A regression test needs the same seam the fix creates (the read reporting the end offset it covered), so fix and test land together.
- **Cause:** the event-log follow path derives its seed from a second, later stat of the file, while the transcript follow path seeds from the offset its initial read actually covered. The initial read (scanEventTail via readTailText) always covers through EOF at read time, so the covered end is knowable — readTailText just discards the byte count of what it concatenated.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** the tail scan now reports the byte end it covered: `forEachTailChunk` delivers each chunk with its scan's `coveredEnd` (the file's size at scan time — the whole-file path reports the bytes actually read, the chunked path the opened inode's fstat size), `readTailTextWithEnd` returns `{ text, coveredEnd }` with `readTailText` kept as the text-only wrapper (src/files.ts), and `scanEventTailWithEnd` carries the end through the parse with a new uncached `readEventsTailWithEnd` export beside the per-poll cached `readEvents` — the stat-keyed cache holds only parsed events, and the follow seeds once per invocation, not per poll (src/events.ts). `cmdLogs` now reads its initial window and seeds its follow from that same read's `coveredEnd` (src/ui/log-commands.ts), so the two follow views share one seeding policy and an event appended between the read and the follow's start is delivered by follow's first poll instead of falling into the both-neither gap; a torn trailing line stays held back by followFile, and a missing log still scans to coveredEnd 0 without creating anything. Regression test: test/events.test.ts pins coveredEnd at the read's EOF and replays the race deterministically on the new seam — an event appended after the read arrives exactly once from followFile's synchronous first poll, and a missing log scans to `{ events: [], coveredEnd: 0 }`.
- **Validation gap:** no-repro — the append window between the read and the seed stat is a few milliseconds of synchronous code, so no black-box CLI sequence landed it deterministically and the existing suite (including the live `logs -f` follow test) could not confirm it; the test only became writable once the fix exposed the covered byte end as a seam to seed and assert against.
### A flag-valued npm option before the subcommand hides a full-suite run from the reviewer tripwire: `npm --prefix /tmp/rev test` (and `npm -C <dir> test`) runs the whole suite in a copied tree but `runsFullSuite` reads the flag's value as the subcommand and never warns (found by coverage loop 2026-09-29 while covering suite-rerun.ts's shell-composition edges, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** A reviewer that copies the worktree to a scratch directory and runs the suite there via an option that takes the directory as its value — `npm --prefix /tmp/rev test`, `npm -C /tmp/rev run test`, `npm --cache /tmp/c test` — holds the landing slot and loads the shared host with a full suite run, and no `suite_rerun` warning event is logged: review.ts's tripwire stays silent, so the digest cannot count the behavior the 2026-09-23 rule exists for.
- **Reproduce:** Before the fix, `node -e 'import("./dist/src/suite-rerun.js").then(m => console.log(m.runsFullSuite("npm --prefix /tmp/rev test")))'` printed `false` (`cd /tmp/rev && npm test` prints `true`); after it, the one-liner prints `true`. The npm branch of `segmentRunsFullSuite` found the subcommand with `firstNonFlag(words, 1)`, which skipped option tokens but not the values a flag consumes — `--prefix`'s `/tmp/rev` is a plain word, so it was read as the subcommand name, matched neither the test, run, nor ci alias sets, and the segment read as an innocuous npm invocation.
- **Cause:** `firstNonFlag` (and `hasFilter`) had no notion of flags that take a separate value; distinguishing `--silent` (no value) from `--prefix <dir>` (value) needed npm's option table or a conservative heuristic, which is why this was a bug record rather than a drive-by fix. The module header accepts that some shapes slip through cheaply, but this one was precisely the copied-tree suite run the tripwire was built to catch, via a flag reviewers plausibly reach for.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** `firstNonFlag` and `hasFilter` now take a set of value-taking flags and consume such a flag together with its next word, so the scan resumes at the real subcommand (src/suite-rerun.ts). The npm branch passes npm's value-taking globals a reviewer plausibly reaches for (`--prefix`, `-C`, `--cache`, `--registry`, `--userconfig`, `--globalconfig`, `--loglevel`, `--workspace`/`-w`, `--omit`), applied before and after the subcommand alike (`npm run --prefix /tmp/rev test` now flags, and `npm test --prefix /tmp/rev` no longer reads the flag's value as a filter); the node branch passes node's own (`--max-old-space-size`, `--stack-size`, `--require`/`-r`, `--conditions`, `--cpu-prof-dir`, `--eval`/`-e`). `--flag=value` form needed no entry — it is one token and already starts with `-`. An unlisted value-taking flag costs one missed warning, exactly the trade the entry's suggested fix described. Regression tests: test/suite-rerun.test.ts pins the value-flag shapes (flagged) alongside no-value flags keeping their next word a real filter, and `--prefix` + `--dry-run` ci staying unflagged.
- **Validation gap:** no-repro (closest tag — the bug reproduced deterministically the moment a test exercised the shape, but the existing suite had no case for a value-taking flag before the subcommand, so nothing confirmed it until one was written; the false negative also left no trace at runtime, making a silent absence the only symptom).

### A doubled-backtick code span in a dashboard table cell swallows the rest of the row: the cell splitter toggles code state per backtick, so the markdown form that quotes a literal backtick (`\`\` \` \`\``) leaves the flag stuck on and the row's later cells merge into the first (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the same day's pipe-in-code-span table fix, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** In the dashboard's Failures/Backlog markdown view, a table cell quoting a backtick with the doubled-delimiter form — the only way markdown can quote one — broke the whole row: the first cell's `<code>` opened and never closed, the row's remaining cells and their values rendered inside the first cell as raw text, and the span itself rendered as `` `` `` `` garbage instead of `<code>\`</code>`. The same per-backtick rule made `mdInline` mis-render the span in prose too.
- **Reproduce:** A scratch run of renderMarkdown on a row `| \`\` \` \`\` | 3 |` returned one `<td>` holding `` `` <code> </code>` | 3 `` before the fix; after it, `<td><code>\`</code></td><td class='r'>3</td>`. The day's earlier pipe-in-code-span fix (single-backtick form) still passes unchanged.
- **Cause:** `mdTable`'s `cells` helper and `mdInline` both tracked code state by toggling once per backtick character (or, in `mdInline`, by treating every other split part as code), so any span containing an odd total number of backticks — exactly what the doubled-delimiter quoting form produces — ended its cell with the code flag on, and the splitter read the following pipe as content. The functions' own comments even claimed per-run semantics ("a doubled backtick is one run") the code never implemented: per-run toggling still fails, because a quoted backtick's three runs leave an odd count either way.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** Both functions now parse backtick runs and match them by length, the CommonMark rule: a span opens at a run and closes at a run of the SAME length, a different-length run inside a span is content, and a run left open at the end is prose (src/ui/gui-client-markdown.ts). A span's content also sheds one bounding space on each side when both are present, so `` \`\` \` \`\` `` renders as `<code>\`</code>`. Regression tests: test/gui-client.test.ts pins the doubled-delimiter table row, the same span in prose, and a length-mismatched pair staying literal text.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant (closest tag — the suite also lacked the doubled-run input shape entirely, so nothing failed on it): the fixed code's comment asserted per-run toggle semantics the code contradicted, so the actual guarantee (same-length run matching) had to be reconstructed from the markdown spec before any test could say what "right" is.

### The failure digest's delta table presents a truncated baseline as a real trend: `windowComplete`'s `oldestEventDate < from` arm proved only the current window, but the deltas are computed from the 2× read's prior half — so a retained log starting between `priorFrom` and `from` carried no note and silently skewed every delta (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the rotation-spanning read fixed the same day, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `tumwater report --days 5` prints its `## Deltas` table from the prior 5-day half of a 10-day read, yet `partial: retained log starts <date>` fired only when the CURRENT window was truncated. On a busy fleet whose log rotated past day 5 but not past today, the digest compared current counts against a prior half missing its earliest days and presented the skew as a trend — the same silent-undercount shape the day report had.
- **Reproduce:** Seed a fixture whose oldest retained event lies inside the prior window (day 5 of a 5+5-day read, so `oldestEventDate < from` holds but the reader's `coversFullWindow` — relative to `priorFrom` — does not); before the fix `collectFailureReport(root, 5).partial` was false, after it the reader's proof is the completeness check and the render carries the note. See the new test in test/failure-report.test.ts, `a prior window the retained log cannot prove is reported as partial too — the deltas read it`.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the `partial` field's own doc comment ("the current window starts before the retained log does") pinned the exact semantics the code implemented, so nothing failed; confirming the bug required reconstructing first that the note was supposed to vouch for the digest's whole 2× read (counts and delta baseline alike), not the window the field doc named.

### The day report is the one windowed consumer that could undercount in silence: `collectReport` returned no coverage proof at all, so a window that outran the single kept archive generation (two or more 16 MB rotations inside a 90-day `--days` window) rendered its totals with no truncation note — while `logs --since`, `report --since`, and the failure digest all carry one (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the rotation-spanning read landed the same day, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `tumwater report --days 90` on a busy fleet aggregates only the events that survived rotation, and its output looks exactly like an idle fleet's — no note, no marker. The `--since` render prints "the log's oldest retained event lies inside this window; older events may have rotated out", the digest sets `partial: retained log starts <date>`, and the GUI usage tab shows nothing either.
- **Reproduce:** Seed a fixture root with an `events.jsonl.1` whose oldest event lies inside the window (e.g. day 3 of a 5-day window) and a live log with a newer event; before the fix `collectReport(root, 5)` carried no coverage field and `renderReportMarkdown` printed no note; after it the report's `coversFullWindow` is false and the markdown's last line carries the same hedged sentence the `--since` render uses. See the two new tests in test/event-window.test.ts, `the day report says so when even the archive starts inside the window` and `the day report stays silent when the archive proves the window's coverage`.
- **Cause:** The rotation-spanning read (b6b8ce82) fixed every windowed consumer through the shared `readWindowEvents`, and `eventWindowCovers` exposes the proof — but `SinceReport` and `FailureReportData` were the only shapes that carried it into a renderer; `ReportData` predates the coverage concept and was never widened, so the days report silently dropped the one field its siblings all surface.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** `ReportData` gains a required `coversFullWindow`, computed in `collectReport` through the shared `eventWindowCovers` (cutoff = the window's first local midnight, so the same-day arm degenerates harmlessly); `renderReportMarkdown` appends the truncation note as its final line when coverage is unproven (never over an empty or fully covered log), and the GUI usage tab appends the same hedged sentence to its window caption. The `--since` path and the digest are untouched.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the rotation-spanning read's own tests pinned `coversFullWindow` at the reader, but confirming this bug required reconstructing first that a coverage note was a contract every windowed consumer was supposed to uphold (from the `--since` and digest renders), since the missing field produced no failing assertion, only a silent absence.

### The verdict-recovery follow-up inherits the strike when it dies: a follow-up turn that itself fails (backend 5xx, quiet kill, timeout — `ok` false) counts `state.unreviewFailures` against the HEAD because the branch consults only the ORIGINAL review run's `pi.ok`, so three backend deaths after verdict-less reviews discard a finished, tested commit the reviewer never rejected (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the verdict-recovery fix landed the same day, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** The reviewer completes and replies without a VERDICT line, the gate runs its recovery turn (2026-09-29 fix), and the recovery turn runs into a dead backend — a transport error, a failed spawn, a quiet kill, a timeout. The strike is decided by `pi.ok` of the ORIGINAL run (true: it replied, just in the wrong envelope), so `state.unreviewFailures` advances; at `REVIEW_FAILURE_LIMIT` (3) the branch runs `resetWorktreeToMain` and the commit is gone, announced only as `discarding unreviewed leftover after 3 failed reviews`.
- **Reproduce:** In the gating suite, script a fake pi whose fresh review run prints a verdict-less assistant reply and whose `--continue` run exits 1 with no output (a dead backend); before the fix the tick recorded `state.unreviewFailures = 1` and left the commit at risk on the strike ladder; after it, the failure is recorded strike-free and the commit survives for the next tick's re-review. See the new test in test/review.test.ts, `a follow-up turn that itself fails (backend dies) is strike-free`.
- **Cause:** The follow-up branch was added inside the `!verdict` block but the strike-free exemption below it (`if (!pi.ok)`) was never widened to cover the follow-up's own failure flag — the 2026-09-20 invariant written into that very branch ("a dead reviewer must never destroy committed work") holds identically for a reviewer that died one turn later.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** The strike-free branch now also fires when the follow-up ran and ended `ok: false` (`if (!pi.ok || (followUp && !followUp.ok))` in src/review.ts), naming the follow-up's own `errorMessage` in the `review_failed` event and detail, and riding the failed follow-up back as `followUpRun` so its spend still folds into the loop totals. A follow-up that died is evidence about the world, never about the diff.
- **Validation gap:** no-fake (closest tag) — the fake-pi shim existed, but no test drove a recovery turn that itself fails (a `--continue` run exiting non-zero with no reply), so the strike-on-backend-death path was unconfirmed until this run wrote that scenario and watched the counter advance before the fix.

### A reviewer that completes but emits no parseable VERDICT line is counted as a strike against the HEAD and, at three, its committed work is discarded — while the harness recovers the author's missing SUMMARY line with a capped follow-up turn, so a reviewer's formatting slip can destroy a finished, tested commit that one cheap turn would have reviewed (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** The 2026-09-29 digest's Top review failure clusters carry `1× improve · 09-29 — "no parseable VERDICT line in the reviewer's reply"` — a reviewer run that completed and replied, but whose reply held no VERDICT line. The gate's response (src/review.ts, the `!verdict` branch after `parseVerdict`): log `review_failed`, then — because `pi.ok` is true, so the transport-failure exemption does not apply — increment `state.unreviewFailures` for this HEAD. At `REVIEW_FAILURE_LIMIT` (3) consecutive verdict-less reviews of the same HEAD, `resetWorktreeToMain` runs `git reset --hard <main>` and the commit is gone from the role branch, announced only as `discarding unreviewed leftover after 3 failed reviews`.
- **Reproduce:** In the gating suite, script a reviewer fake that returns a complete, on-topic reply containing no `VERDICT:` line (prose only); before the fix, three ticks that each commit work on the same role ended with the branch tip equal to main, the commit discarded, and the only trace the `discarding unreviewed leftover` warning plus three `review_failed` events. The pins in test/loop-3.test.ts covered the transport-failure side (`pi.ok` false never counts a strike) and the discard counter, not the verdict-less-but-completed shape's recoverability.
- **Cause:** The verdict-less branch was written when the only recovery machinery was the transport-retry ladder; `requestSummary` came later for the author path and was never carried across to the reviewer, whose prompt (src/gate-prompts.ts) demands the line but whose caller treats its absence as a verdict about the commit.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** Before counting a strike against the HEAD, the gate now asks the reviewer's own session for the missing line — one capped follow-up turn (`buildVerdictRequestPrompt` in src/gate-prompts.ts, `requestVerdict` in src/review.ts, mirroring `requestSummary`: `--continue`, 900 s timeout / 300 s quiet caps, the follow-up's usage folded into the loop totals via a new `GateResult.followUpRun`). A recovered verdict is logged with a warning and reviews as an approval; only a follow-up that also yields no parseable verdict counts the strike, so the ladder keeps its meaning — a reviewer that cannot answer about this diff — instead of punishing an answer that arrived in the wrong envelope. Regression tests: test/review.test.ts pins both the recovery path (one `--continue` run after the fresh review, no `review_failed`, state clean) and the follow-up-also-fails path (the strike counts, the detail message names the follow-up).
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the existing suite confirmed only that the strike ladder fired, and the bug turned on what a strike was supposed to mean (evidence about the backend vs. evidence about the reviewer's output format), which had to be reconstructed from the code's own transport-failure exemption before the missing recovery turn was visible.

### The dashboard's markdown table cells split on every pipe, even one inside an inline-code span: a digest table row quoting a shell pipeline (`\`grep a|b | tail\``) renders as three cells with raw backticks leaking into the cell text (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the 2026-09-29 GUI redesign, fixed 2026-09-29)
