- **Symptom:** In the dashboard's Failures/Backlog markdown view, any table cell whose inline code contains a `|` — common in the digest's error clusters, which quote the exact failing shell command — split at that pipe: the row grew phantom cells, the code span's backticks rendered as literal text, and the row's remaining cells shifted left of their columns.
- **Reproduce:** A scratch run of renderMarkdown on `| command | count |\n| --- | ---: |\n| \`grep a|b | tail\` | 3 |` returned `<td>\`grep a\`</td><td class='r'>b</td><td>tail\`</td><td>3</td>` before the fix; after it, the row keeps two cells and `<code>grep a|b | tail</code>.
- **Cause:** mdTable's `cells` helper stripped the outer pipes and then split the row on every `|` character, with no notion of the inline-code state mdInline gives the cell contents; the two functions' backtick rules had never met.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** mdTable's cells helper now scans the row character by character, toggling code state on each run of backticks (one run opens a span, the next closes it, a doubled backtick is one run) and splitting only at pipes outside code (src/ui/gui-client-markdown.ts). Regression test: test/gui-client.test.ts extends the table case with a quoted pipeline and a code-only pipe cell.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability (closest tag — the corruption is plainly visible in a rendered dashboard, but left no trace the offline suite could fail on): the markdown tests exercised tables only with pipe-free cells, so the delimiter-vs-content ambiguity had no failing assertion; the new fixture pins the code-span rule.

### The one-character ellipsis reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none…` (and `none… nothing to do`) fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29 latent-bug hunt over the fixed quote/punctuation bugs, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** The negation guard's sentence marks (`;` `:` `,` `.` `!` `?`) and its appended-note separator class both omitted the one-character ellipsis `…`, so a work-completed reply ending `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none…` — or `none… nothing to do`, the ellipsis opening an appended explanation — classified as a genuine refusal and the harness destroyed the tick's tested work. The ASCII three-dot ellipsis (`...`) already stripped via its dots; only the single-character mark was missing.
- **Reproduce:** `isNegatedRefusal("None…")` returned `false` (the new regression test, run before the fix); no existing case carried the mark.
- **Cause:** `bare()`'s trailing-punctuation class `[.!,;:?!]` and the appended-note separator class `[—–\-;:,.!?('"‘’“”]` both enumerated their members without the ellipsis, even though the guard's own rule — punctuation after the token is formatting, a letter is the reason — names no character distinction.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** `…` joins both classes (src/reply-contract.ts): `bare()` strips it as a trailing mark and the appended-note class accepts it as an explanation opener, still anchored right after the token so a reason continuing with a word (`none of the attempted fixes work…`) stays a refusal. Regression test: test/reply-contract.test.ts (`isNegatedRefusal negates none and n/a under the one-character ellipsis`) pins six negating shapes plus that refusal-preserving negative.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability (closest tag — the failure is plainly visible in a real tick's hard-reset log line but left no trace the offline suite could fail on): the guard's tests enumerated the separator members their author listed, so the missing member produced no failing assertion anywhere; only a character-class review against the guard's stated rule surfaced it, and the new fixture now supplies the pin.

### The lift of a fleet hold reads as a 429 even when the hold was a backend failure: `rate_limit_resumed` carries no kind and both renderers hard-code "429 hold lifted", so a connection-error hold's re-open is recorded in the event feed and the digest as the end of a rate-limit storm (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** The 2026-09-29 hold generalization gave the `rate_limit_hold` event a kind split — a backend storm renders "backend hold (connection error)" in the feed (event-format.ts) and the digest's Fleet state changes (failure-state-change.ts) — but the re-open path was left behind: `pollRateLimitHold` logged `rate_limit_resumed` with no payload and both renderers printed the literal "429 hold lifted". After the backend recovers, the record says a 429 storm ended — the one claim about the incident an operator can cross-check against the provider's side, and a lie for connection/timeout/5xx/model-load holds.
- **Reproduce:** Drive `pollRateLimitHold` with two roles' connection failures, then one poll past the hold's deadline: the resumed event carries no `kind`, and `formatEvent`/`describeStateChange` render "429 hold lifted". Confirmed by the new regression assertions before the fix.
- **Cause:** The kind split was applied only to the hold event's rendering; the resumed event's shape predates kinds and its log call was never extended, even though `FleetHold.kind` deliberately survives the re-open for exactly this kind of consumer.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** `pollRateLimitHold` logs the ended hold's kind on `rate_limit_resumed` (src/tick-timing.ts), the events.ts comment records the payload, and both renderers split on it the same way the hold line does: a backend kind renders "backend hold lifted (connection error) — role loops tick again", a rate-limit or kindless (torn/historical) line keeps the exact "429 hold lifted" wording. Regression tests: test/orchestrator-seams.test.ts pins the resumed event's kind; test/event-format.test.ts and test/failure-state-change.test.ts pin both renderings.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the bug left no failing trace anywhere (the suite passed with the lie in place), and confirming it first required deciding the contract the payload never had: that a hold's lift must name the kind it lifted, which meant extending the `rate_limit_resumed` event shape, not just its rendering.

### The status header's `main <sha>: green` badge reads only the default 200-event tail: a burst of quiet or failing ticks logs hundreds of events without moving main, so once the newest merge-scope `build_check` falls outside `readEvents`' default tail the badge silently vanishes and reappears as ticks tick by — "absent" stopped meaning the field's own contract (no merge-scope check has run) and started meaning "the window ended" (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

Fix: `mainCheckForPoll` grows its tail (×4 from 200, capped at `MAIN_CHECK_SCAN_MAX_EVENTS` = 5000) until a merge-scope check is inside it — every event the sha derivation needs after the check is newer, so one window holds the whole derivation; past the cap the badge drops, the same graceful loss log rotation already imposes. Regression tests: a check 300 events deep still badges, and one past the cap does not.
**Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — no existing test seeded an event log deeper than the read tail, so confirming the flap meant first reconstructing what "absent" had to mean (no check has ever run vs. the window ended) and pinning that as the invariant.

### The failure digest still splits the two tick-timeout shapes the storm reducer just learned to pool: `clusterMessages` and the loss-cause fold group by raw `normalizeClusterKey`, so a mixed fleet of plain and progressing timeouts renders two half-size clusters in the digest's error section and splits its timeout agent-hours across two loss rows — one of which a top-N cut can drop (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** After the 2026-09-29 storm-pooling fix, the error-storm warning treats pi's two tick-timeout message shapes as one cause on one knob, but the failure digest (`tumwater report --failures`) did not: `collectFailureReport`'s error clusters and its `lossCauses` ranking grouped by raw `normalizeClusterKey(error)`. A fleet mixing `timed out after 1800s` and the progressing `timed out after 1800s while still making progress — …` renders two clusters instead of one, and the top-loss table splits one cause's agent-hours across two rows — the same half-a-cause blindness the storm fix removed, on the surface an operator reads to decide whether `tickTimeoutSeconds` fits the serving model.
- **Reproduce:** Build events with `tick_end` errors `timed out after 1800s` (feature) and the progressing variant (bugfix), then `collectFailureReport(root, 1)`: before the fix `data.errors.clusters` held two timeout keys and `data.lossCauses` two timeout rows, each with half the ticks/ms/cost. Confirmed by the new regression test before the fix.
- **Cause:** The pooling the storm fix added lived in src/error-storm.ts (`stormKey`), applied only to storm observations; the digest's two groupings (`clusterMessages` in failure-data.ts and the `timeAndSpend` loss fold) each normalized without it.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** Moved the pooling home into failure-data.ts beside `normalizeClusterKey` as exported `poolTimeoutKey` (with `TICK_TIMEOUT_KEY`), applied it in both `clusterMessages` and the loss fold, and pointed error-storm.ts at the shared function — one owner of the two shapes' pooling, no circular import. Regression tests in test/failure-report.test.ts pin the pooled mixed-fleet cluster and summed loss row, and `poolTimeoutKey`'s identity on other causes; test/error-storm.test.ts's mixed-shape storm test still passes unchanged.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — nothing in the suite emitted the second timeout shape at digest scope (the storm tests did, at storm scope), and confirming the gap first required extending the storm fix's invariant — both shapes are one cause on `tickTimeoutSeconds`, so the digest must pool too — before the mixed-shape fixture could be written; the test now supplies it.

### The error-storm warning misses the timeout shape the fleet now actually emits: pi's progressing-timeout message normalizes to a different cluster key than the plain one, so `errorStormKnob` names no knob for it and a mixed fleet of both shapes splits into sub-threshold clusters that never trip (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** After the 2026-09-29 timeout fixes, a tick killed at `tickTimeoutSeconds` while still making progress emits `timed out after <dur> while still making progress — session and worktree edits preserved for resume` (src/pi.ts), not the plain `timed out after <dur>`. The new fleet-wide storm warning (src/error-storm.ts) clusters those under two different normalizeClusterKey keys: a storm of progressing timeouts warns with no `tickTimeoutSeconds` knob named, and a fleet mixing both shapes (e.g. some roles stalled, some working) splits 2/2 into two sub-threshold clusters and stays completely silent — the exact timeout-storm blindness the warning was built to end.
- **Reproduce:** `errorStorm(ERROR_STORM_QUIET, [three roles with the progressing lastError])` returned key `timed out after <dur> while still making progress — …` (no knob); a 2-progressing + 1-plain fleet returned `null` (no storm). Confirmed by the new regression test before the fix.
- **Cause:** `errorStorm` grouped observations by raw `normalizeClusterKey(lastError)`, and `errorStormKnob` matched only the bare key; pi's two timeout message shapes were never canonicalized to one storm cause.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** errorStorm now canonicalizes the progressing shape to the plain `TICK_TIMEOUT_KEY` (both pi.ts timeout messages are one cause with one knob) before grouping; `errorStormKnob` is unchanged and keeps naming `tickTimeoutSeconds`. Regression tests in test/error-storm.test.ts pin the pooled mixed-fleet storm and the wiring's knob for an all-progressing storm.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the existing suite passed because it pinned only the plain timeout shape; confirming the gap first required reconstructing what "one shared cause" must mean here (both of pi.ts's timeout emissions are the same storm on `tickTimeoutSeconds`) before any test could be written. Closest tag: the missing piece was a fixture emitting the second shape, not a shim.

### `tumwater help reset-counters` promises to "Zero ticks/commits/tokens/cost", but a role's `today` spend and the fleet budget header survive the reset, so the help overpromises what the command deliberately does (found by qa 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** After `tumwater reset-counters`, `tumwater status` shows every role's `cost` at $0.00 but keeps the per-role `today` column and the `budget: $X/$Y today` header at the day's spend. A first-time user reading the help line expects the whole cost picture to zero; the kept `today` figures look like the reset failed.
- **Reproduce:** In a scratch dir: `tumwater init "..."`, then give the state nonzero day costs (seed each `.tumwater/state/<role>.json` with `dayCostUsd: 0.5`, `totalCostUsd: 1.25`, `ticks: 7`; or run real ticks), then `tumwater status` (shows `$0.50` today per role, header `budget: $7.00/$50 today`), then `tumwater reset-counters`, then `tumwater status` again: `cost` and `total` drop to $0.00 while `today` stays `$0.50` and the header stays `$7.00/$50`.
- **Cause:** This is deliberate, not lost state: src/loop-state.ts documents that `dayCostUsd` is "deliberately NOT zeroed: the budget is a safety valve, not an observation window" — zeroing it would let an operator reset their way past the daily spend cap. Only the help line is wrong.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** Reworded the usage line in src/help.ts to "Zero lifetime ticks/commits/tokens/cost (fresh observation window; today's budget spend is kept — the daily cap cannot be reset past)", matching `zeroCounters`' documented contract in src/loop-state.ts. Behavior untouched. Regression test in test/help.test.ts pins both halves of the honest wording and forbids the old overpromise from returning.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the overpromise left no trace any automated check would catch: help copy is prose, and nothing tied the documented promise to `zeroCounters`' actual behavior until a human cross-read them; the new regression test now supplies that pin.

### A fleet-wide timeout storm raises no alarm: three or more roles timing out consecutively means `tickTimeoutSeconds` does not fit the serving model, but only each role's own `consecutive tick failures` warning fires (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, decomposed from the timeout-discard bug fixed the same day, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** On 2026-09-22 all 14 roles timed out at the 1800 s default for 8 hours; the only signal was 14 independent per-role streak warnings — nothing fleet-wide named the shared cause.
- **Reproduce:** Any config whose tick budget every role exceeds: each loop warns alone on its own streak; no digest event or warning aggregates the identical `timed out after <dur>` errors across roles.
- **Cause:** The streak warning lives per loop (loop.ts's ERROR_STREAK_WARN path) with no fleet-level aggregation of same-shaped failures.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** New POLICY module src/error-storm.ts on the 429-hold's shape (src/rate-limit-hold.ts): a pure reducer that trips when ERROR_STORM_ROLES (3, the per-role warn bar) distinct roles' consecutive error streaks share one normalized cause (normalizeClusterKey), edge-triggered with clear-and-re-arm and a strongest-cause tiebreak; the orchestrator poll (pollErrorStorm in src/tick-timing.ts, wired beside pollRateLimitHold) logs exactly one `warning` event per episode naming the roles, the cause, and the config knob when the cause has one (`timed out after <dur>` → `tickTimeoutSeconds`; no knob is invented for causes no setting controls). Observational only — it gates nothing, unlike the 429 hold.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the failure was an absence that left no trace: each role's own streak warning was individually correct and healthy-looking, so confirming that no mechanism aggregated the shared cause across roles required reading the whole poll loop rather than observing any output; the regression tests (test/error-storm.test.ts, 10) pin the new aggregate directly.

### The shipped `tickTimeoutSeconds` default (1800 s) is shorter than ticks on the current hosted model legitimately take, so a legitimately long tick burns its whole resume streak and then restarts the same long task fresh (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, decomposed from the timeout-discard bug fixed the same day, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** Since 2026-09-23, 29 ticks landed work after running longer than 30 minutes under the default 1800 s budget (the longest: telemetry at 185 min) — each would have been killed at 30 min, resumed up to `QUIET_KILL_RESUME_LIMIT` (3) times by the fix below, then abandoned to a fresh tick that re-derives the same long task from scratch.
- **Reproduce:** src/config.ts `defaultConfig` set `tickTimeoutSeconds: 1800`; the live config raised it to 54000 as a workaround. A fixture run that needs ~31 min under the default ends `quiet_killed` four times in a row and never lands.
- **Cause:** The default was set before the hosted model's legitimate tick duration was known; the 2026-09-22 meltdown (97 timeouts in 8 hours) was its first large observation.
- **Fixed 2026-09-29 by bugfix loop:** Raised the shipped default to 4 h (`tickTimeoutSeconds: 4 * 60 * 60` in src/config.ts) — above the observed 185-minute maximum, now that a timeout of a progressing run resumes instead of discarding (the entry below) and a byte-silent run is reaped at `max(2 × quietTimeoutSeconds, 30 min)` regardless of this knob, so no unbounded path widens. Regression test in test/config.test.ts pins the default above the longest legitimate observed tick.
- **Validation gap:** real-run-needed (closest tag) — no offline path could confirm the shipped default was too short: the bug only became visible as live fleet telemetry showing 29 legitimate ticks outlasting the 1800 s budget, which is what fixed the "legitimate" threshold.

### At a raised tickTimeoutSeconds a run that never emits a byte has no watchdog: the quiet kill exempts zero-byte runs on the assumption that the tick timeout bounds them, which the live 54000 s config breaks (found by human commit-history analysis 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** Latent; no instance observed yet. The live tumwater.json sets `tickTimeoutSeconds: 54000` (the workaround for the timeout-discard bug fixed below). A pi run whose model request hangs before the first byte (a dead connection, a wedged provider) is killed only by that timeout, 15 hours later, while it holds a concurrency slot.
- **Reproduce:** In src/pi.ts, `allowedSilenceMs` returned `Infinity` while `sawOutput` was false, and the quiet check additionally required `sawOutput`. A fixture pi that never writes, with `tickTimeoutSeconds` far above `quietTimeoutSeconds`, runs until the tick timeout.
- **Cause:** The zero-byte exemption (BUGS.md 2026-09-23, false quiet kills under merge-check load) delegated its bound to a separate knob that operators raise for unrelated reasons — the 2026-09-23 assumption that the tick timeout is near the quiet window's scale held only while `tickTimeoutSeconds ≤ 2 × quietTimeoutSeconds`.
- **Fix:** A byte-silent run now gets a finite bound of its own, independent of `tickTimeoutSeconds`: `allowedSilenceMs` returns `max(2 × quietTimeoutSeconds, 30 min)` while no byte has flowed, and the quiet check no longer requires `sawOutput` to fire. The 2026-09-23 protection is kept — ordinary startup latency (fork/exec starved by load, bytes written but not yet drained) sits far below 30 minutes, and the false positive the finite bound can cost is a quiet kill, which resumes the session and worktree like an interruption: a restart, not discarded work.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-29, regression test: test/pi.test.ts "a run that never emits a byte is quiet-killed at 30 minutes regardless of the tick timeout" (byte-silent fake pi, `tickTimeoutSeconds` 7200, asserts `quietKilled` after 31 logical minutes); the "slow to speak" test re-pinned — its one-minute startup advance stays under the new bound.
- **Validation gap:** slow-check — the only pre-fix repro was a real run that had to outlive `tickTimeoutSeconds` (hours) to show the missing reaper, so no offline suite test could afford to confirm it; the regression test pins the new bound on the logical watchdog clock instead.

### A tick that reaches tickTimeoutSeconds discards its worktree edits and session, while a quiet kill resumes them: on 2026-09-22 every role timed out at the 1800 s default for 8 hours (97 ticks, ~55 agent-hours discarded) (found by human commit-history analysis 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** From 2026-09-22 14:35 to 22:42 PDT, events.jsonl recorded 97 `tick_end result:error` events whose `error` is `timed out after 1800s`, spanning all 14 roles — ~55 agent-hours, 28% of all tick time in that window ([docs/commit-history-analysis.md](docs/commit-history-analysis.md)). The operator's workaround was to raise `tickTimeoutSeconds` to 54000; since 2026-09-23, 29 ticks landed work after running longer than 30 minutes, each of which would have been discarded under the shipped default.
- **Reproduce:** src/loop.ts's `pi.timedOut` branch returned `{ result: "error" }` — prompt re-queued, next tick resets the worktree — while the `pi.quietKilled` branch just above returned `quiet_killed`, which `applyTickOutcome` resumes with the worktree and pi session intact (`resumePending`). A fixture pi that emits one progress event past a 1 s `tickTimeoutSeconds` ended the tick as `error` with its half-done edit left for the reset.
- **Cause:** The timeout treated "slow" as "failed": a run still making progress when the deadline fired is exactly the case where discarding costs the most.
- **Fix:** runPi (src/pi.ts) records at deadline-fire time whether the run had a real progress event within the last `quietTimeoutSeconds` (`timedOutProgressing` — the same window the quiet watchdog honors, so the two watchdogs agree on what a healthy run looks like). The loop's timeout branch returns `quiet_killed` with `resumeCause: "timeout"` for such a run, so `applyTickOutcome` resumes the session and worktree promptly, bounded by `QUIET_KILL_RESUME_LIMIT` like any quiet-kill streak; buildResumePrompt's new "timeout" bridge asks for the smallest finish against the same limit. A run with no progress event at all (zero-byte, or bytes without one structured event) keeps the discard path — which is also what bounds the zero-byte exemption.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-29, regression tests: test/loop-5.test.ts "a tick timeout that fires on a run still making progress is resumed like a quiet kill" (asserts `quiet_killed`, `resumeCause: "timeout"`, and the half-done edit surviving) and "a timed-out run that emitted bytes but no progress event still discards"; test/loop-4.test.ts's transient-timeout-and-harness-timeout test re-pinned to `quiet_killed` with its exactly-one-invocation assertion kept.
- **Validation gap:** no-fake — closest tag: the fake-pi shim existed, but every timeout fixture modeled a zero-byte or hung run, so no existing test could confirm what a deadline firing on a still-progressing run should do; the discriminator's edge (structured progress vs. raw bytes) only became checkable once such a fixture was written.

### A NUL byte in the /api/prompt-cancel file field passes the basename guard and answers a 500 instead of the endpoint's promised 400: the fs layer throws `ERR_INVALID_ARG_VALUE` on a path no filename can hold, and the throw lands in the server's 500 catch (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `POST /api/prompt-cancel` with `{"file": "a\u0000.md"}` answers a 500 `{error: "The argument 'path' must be a string ..."}` — the raw Node TypeError text leaks to the client — where the endpoint's contract says a hostile or malformed file name is a user-input error answered 400 with nothing touched on disk. The same body is the 500 path of gui.ts's handler catch, so every NUL-bearing name is a server fault instead of a validation answer.
- **Reproduce:** `queueFileNameProblem("a\u0000.md")` returns null (the name passes the guard: no separator, not `..`, ends in `.md`), then `cancelQueuedFile` throws `ERR_INVALID_ARG_VALUE` from `readFileSync` — `takeQueuedFile` rethrows it because the code is not ENOENT (scratch run against the built dist confirmed both halves; pinned by adding the name to the endpoint test's hostile list, which answered 500 before the fix).
- **Cause:** the 2026-09-29 guard enumerated the hostile shapes its author imagined — path separators, `..`, non-`.md` — but a NUL byte is a character no filename can hold, so the fs layer never answers ENOENT for it; it throws. The guard's own doc claims fail-closed safety ("a caller that skips the guard fails closed"), yet the shape that slips through converts the endpoint's clean 400 into the 500 catch.
- **Fix:** `queueFileNameProblem` (src/inbox.ts) rejects any name containing a NUL byte with the same basename error, so the guard now covers every input that would make the fs call throw instead of answering ENOENT-or-gone; the endpoint 400s before anything touches disk, as its contract states.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-29, regression test: test/gui-server.test.ts adds `"a\u0000.md"` to the /api/prompt-cancel hostile-name list (asserts 400 with nothing touched on disk — verified failing before the fix: the same request answered 500).
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the suite's hostile list tested the shapes its guard author had enumerated, and confirming the gap meant reconstructing the guard's real contract first: not "rejects separators and `..`" but "rejects every name the filesystem cannot answer ENOENT for", a boundary the fs layer's throw behavior — not the guard's own spec — defines.

### The TUI's prompt line is budgeted in UTF-16 code units: a prompt holding a wide character renders wider than the terminal and wraps the prompt across two visual lines, breaking the module's own one-line-per-visual-line invariant — and a prompt whose columns fit but whose unit count trips the fit check is windowed while it could show whole (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `renderInputView` (the TUI prompt line, rendered by tui.ts as `> ${renderInputView(...)}`) compares `text.length` against `width - 3` and windows by code units: a six-character CJK prompt ("你好世界你好" — 6 units, 12 columns) at width 10 passed the fit check and was returned whole, so the rendered line was 2 + 12 = 14 columns on a 10-column terminal and wrapped; conversely any text whose unit length fit was shown whole regardless of its column width.
- **Reproduce:** `renderInputView("你好世界你好", 6, 10)` returned the full text — `displayWidth("> " + view)` = 14 > 10 (scratch run against the built dist confirmed; pinned as a regression test asserting the exact windowed view).
- **Cause:** the same UTF-16-vs-display-column unit mistake the 2026-09-28 aligned-tables fix removed from status-render/history and the 2026-09-29 fix removed from event-format, left behind on the TUI input view — whose window logic (`inputViewWindow`) is too intertwined with unit-index arithmetic for a one-call swap, so this fix rewrites the window placement per code point.
- **Fix:** both functions now budget in terminal display columns via `displayWidth` (src/ui/tui-input.ts): the fit check admits whole text whose columns fit beside the two-column prefix (≤ width − 2, one column freer than the old `room` check when no ellipsis is spent — windowing now engages only when the whole line genuinely cannot fit), and `inputViewWindow` decomposes per code point, spends `room` in columns, and still returns unit indices so `text.slice` works unchanged; the surrogate-boundary rules are preserved by construction (every cut lands on a code-point start) and the whole-character fallback survives as the case where the cursor's character alone exceeds `room`. The samples-loop oracle in test/tui-input.test.ts now measures in columns and carries CJK samples.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-29, regression test: test/tui-input.test.ts `renderInputView budgets the prompt line in display columns, not UTF-16 units` (asserts the exact windowed view "…界你好" at width 10, a whole-fit CJK line, and the unchanged ASCII window; verified failing before the change at 14 rendered columns on a 10-column terminal).
- **Validation gap:** no-observability (closest tag — like the sibling event-format entry, the wrapped prompt is plainly visible in the TUI but left no trace the suite could fail on): every width assertion in test/tui-input.test.ts compared `.length`, which for CJK under-counts columns, so the misrender passed every test while shipping; nothing else was missing once a wide-character prompt fixture existed.

### The event feed's loop column still padded in UTF-16 code units: a custom loop name holding a wide character renders that row's message column two columns right of every ASCII row's, in `logs`, the TUI activity pane, and the GUI feed alike (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-29, fixed 2026-09-29)

- **Symptom:** `tumwater logs` (and the TUI's recent-activity pane and the GUI event feed, all rendering through `formatEvent`) pads the loop column so every line's message starts at the same display column — except lines whose loop name carries an East Asian wide character or emoji: a CJK char is one UTF-16 code unit but two terminal display columns, so its `padEnd` cell renders wider than 9 columns and its message sits right of every ASCII row's. Custom loop names are user text (`customLoops` in tumwater.json), the same source the 2026-09-28 aligned-tables entry already named.
- **Reproduce:** `formatEvent({ loop: "翻译", type: "wake", reason: "main moved" })` beside the same event with `loop: "bugfix"`: measure the display columns from the loop column's start to the message — 11 vs 9 (a scratch run confirmed; pinned as a regression test).
- **Cause:** `formatEvent` (src/ui/event-format.ts:42) built the loop column with `String#padEnd(9)`, which counts UTF-16 code units, while the display-column fact the tables already use lives in `padToWidth`/`displayWidth` (src/text.ts) — the exact unit mistake the 2026-09-28 aligned-tables fix removed from status-render.ts and history.ts, left behind on the event-feed surface the fix's scope did not reach.
- **Fix:** the loop column now pads through `padToWidth(String(e.loop), 9)` (src/ui/event-format.ts), so a wide-character name occupies its real two columns per char and the message column stays put; names already past 9 columns pass through unchanged, exactly as `padEnd` behaved.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-29, regression test: test/event-format.test.ts `formatEvent keeps the loop column aligned when a loop name holds a wide character` (asserts the wide-name row's message column equals the ASCII row's, with the ASCII row's 10-column offset pinned — verified failing before the change at 11 vs 10).
- **Validation gap:** no-observability (closest tag — the drift is plainly visible in real `logs` output but left no trace the suite could fail on): every event-feed assertion matched lines by substring or whitespace-flexible regex, both blind to display-column drift, so the misrender passed every test while shipping; nothing else was missing once a wide-character loop fixture existed.

### A failed rebuild never leaves a state transition: `Redeployer.block` logs only a `warning`, so the state stream keeps reporting `restart pending — main <sha> green; compiling` after the compile has already died — the 2026-09-28 digest's last two fleet transitions are 34 minutes of "compiling" beside its 76× `tsc exited ENOENT` cluster (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the 2026-09-28 digest (470 ticks, one day) closes its Fleet state changes section with two harness transitions 34 minutes apart — 19:47 `restart pending — main c9db4539 green; compiling`, 20:21 `restart pending — main ff730052 green; compiling` — still "compiling" at the window's end, and `+123 older transitions hidden` shows the day's earlier episodes in the same shape. The same digest's top warning cluster is **76×** `rebuild of 762c5d13 failed — staying on build 66afeacd: tsc exited ENOENT`: `ENOENT` is a spawn failure — the compiler never ran, so each rebuild died in milliseconds (established by the filed-and-fixed attribution bug above: commit 48835efc — "`ENOENT` is not a compiler exit code"). The state section shows an in-progress build; the warning section shows the builds could not start; nothing in the state section records the failure — and the two newest transitions, which is what an operator reads as "what the harness is doing now", sit 34 minutes of "compiling" from one dead compile to the next.
- **Why the response was wrong:** the state-change stream (`STATE_CHANGE_TYPES`, src/failure-state-change.ts:17 — the digest's Fleet state changes section, src/failure-data.ts:355-358, replays the newest events of those types) has an event for the rebuild's start — `restart_pending`, logged when the compile begins (src/redeploy.ts:353) — and for two terminal outcomes, success (`restart`, :427) and startup refusal (`restart_refused`, :460), but none for the third: `block()` (:473 — red-main verdict, compile verdict, swap error) logs only a `warning`. The stream can enter "compiling" and leaves it only via the next start — here, main moving: the 19:47→20:21 gap is exactly two landed commits (c9db4539, ff730052, both in the digest's Landed list), each waking a fresh "compiling" state that dies in milliseconds — or via a success that cannot come while the toolchain is broken. The block's truth lives only in the `BuildStatus.restartBlocked` payload (status(), src/redeploy.ts:241), read by the status/doctor badge (src/ui/status-model.ts:288, src/doctor.ts:386) — a payload the transition stream and the digest never surface, so the digest's own Fleet state section contradicts its own warning clusters. Precedent: the 2026-09-23 entry "A self-redeploy whose new generation fails a startup precondition takes the whole fleet down with no event" was fixed by adding the missing event — the same class of gap, on the pre-restart side. The 2026-09-28 fix (48835efc) that split a never-ran compile off a compiler verdict did not close it: the rejected branch (src/redeploy.ts:370-377) warns once per head, drops the pending, and the next poll starts a fresh episode — a fresh `restart_pending` "compiling" state event and a fresh doomed spawn every poll, the warning deduped to silence after the first (the green verdict is cached per SHA, src/main-baseline.ts:80, so the churn is state events and doomed spawns, not suite re-runs). Either build — old (latched) or new (churning) — the operator watches an unending "compiling" while the fleet stays pinned on the stale build.
- **Reproduce:** src/redeploy.ts `poll()`: a compile start logs `restart_pending`; a failed compile reaches `block()` (a real compiler verdict) or the rejected branch (a never-ran spawn failure) — neither logged a state change, only a `warning`. Replaying that stream through src/failure-data.ts's state section left the newest state event at `restart pending — … compiling` until main moved or a restart succeeded. Live instance: the 2026-09-28 digest above (76× ENOENT cluster; 19:47/20:21 transitions; window ends mid-"compiling").
- **Fix:** give the terminal block a state event of its own — a `restart_blocked` type in `STATE_CHANGE_TYPES` (src/failure-state-change.ts:17) with a `describeStateChange` case rendering `restart blocked — <reason>; staying on build <sha>`, logged from `block()` beside its warning — so the stream exits "compiling" on failure the way it already exits on refusal; and, for the rejected path, log `restart_pending` at most once per head (or only on head change) so a broken toolchain stops emitting a fresh in-progress state every poll. Done exactly that shape: a `restart_blocked` event type (src/events.ts) logged from `block()` beside its warning — from the running build, to the blocked head, reason — rendered as `restart blocked — <reason>; staying on build <sha>` in the digest's state section (src/failure-state-change.ts) and as a full line in the event feed (src/ui/event-format.ts); `restart_pending` is now logged at most once per head (a `pendingLoggedHead` key in src/redeploy.ts, cleared when an episode ends in a state other than compiling — a refusal or a completed restart), so a broken toolchain re-attempting its doomed compile every poll no longer re-emits a fresh "compiling" transition each time.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-29, regression tests: test/redeploy.test.ts `a blocked restart leaves the state stream: a restart_blocked event beside the warning, once per head` (the blocked compile emits `{ type: "restart_blocked", from, to, reason }` exactly once for the latched head — verified failing before the change as a type error plus a missing event in the recorded sequence); the red-main and failed-compile tests now pin `restart_blocked` in their event sequences; `a rejected compile warns once per head, not once per retry` also pins one `restart_pending` per head across doomed retries; test/failure-state-change.test.ts renders the new digest line (reason under the 24-char field cap) and test/failure-report.test.ts includes it in the state-section cases. `npm run test` 2054 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the suite pinned the blocked path's event sequence (`["build_stale", "restart_pending", "warning"]`) as correct, so the intended guarantee — a state the stream enters it must also leave, via a typed event — had to be reconstructed from the stream's own shape (start, restart, refusal all typed; block not) before any test could fail.

### The sibling shape the `rejected` flag missed: a `deps.compile` that REJECTS (the mirror worktree checkout throwing in production) latches "rebuild of <sha> failed" with no retry until main moves, pinning the fleet on the stale build even after the environment recovers (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the 2026-09-28 ENOENT fix made `compileStaged`'s catch return `{ ok: false, rejected: true }` for a spawn failure, and `redeploy.poll` retried that shape instead of latching it. But production binds `deps.compile` to `async (h) => compileStaged(root, await mirror(h), h)` — when `ensureDetachedWorktree` throws (a git lock in the mirror worktree, disk pressure) or the staging setup throws, the promise REJECTS: `track()` records `compiled.error` with `result` still undefined, and `poll`'s `c?.rejected` check is blind to that shape, so the failure fell into the verdict branch — `block(mainHead, "rebuild of <sha> failed", …)` set `blockedHead`, "no retry until main moves". The mirror worktree breaking is the canonical "repaired environment must redeploy without main moving" case this fix class exists for, yet the most likely production throw path re-created the exact 64-warnings-a-day latch: an environmental failure named after a commit, pinned until main happened to move.
- **Why the response was wrong:** the "a failure before a verdict is retried, not latched" guarantee was applied to two of the three no-verdict shapes: the green check's thrown path (`green.error` → "could not run", drop, retry — BUGS.md 2026-09-16) and the compile's explicit `rejected` flag — but not the compile's thrown path (`compiled.error`), which was the only no-verdict shape with a pre-existing test asserting the latch was correct (the 2026-09-11 coverage test `a thrown compile blocks with its error text, not a bare failure` predates the 2026-09-28 fix and pins the old semantics; the newer intent wins).
- **Fix:** `redeploy.poll`'s compile-failure branch (src/redeploy.ts) treats a missing `CompileResult` — the tracked rejection — as a rejection alongside the `rejected` flag: no `blockedHead`, one warning per head through the same `compileFailedHead` dedupe key (message `rebuild of <sha> could not run: <error> — retrying on the next poll`, mirroring the green check's phrasing; the `rejected`-flag shape keeps its existing "could not start" message), the pending head dropped, and the compile re-attempted on the next poll. A compiler verdict arriving as a `CompileResult` (`ok: false` with a numeric exit or output) still blocks, unchanged; that remains safe in production because `compileStaged`'s catch converts every tsc exit into a result, so a rejection can only mean the environment failed before a verdict. The 2026-09-11 test now asserts the retried behavior and keeps its "not a bare failure" coverage (the warning carries `compiled.error`, not the "compile threw" fallback).
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/redeploy.test.ts: `a thrown compile is a rejection, not a verdict: its error text rides the warning, nothing is latched` — a rejecting `deps.compile` emits the "could not run" warning with the error text, leaves `restartBlocked` undefined, and the next poll re-attempts the same head with main unmoved (verified failing against the pre-fix latch before the change); `npm test redeploy` 43/43.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the pre-fix coverage test asserted the latch as the correct behavior, so the suite itself encoded the opposite guarantee; the intended one (a no-verdict compile is retried, not latched) had to be reconstructed from the 2026-09-28 fix and the green check's thrown path before a reproduction could be written to fail.

### A compile that never ran is latched as a verdict about the commit: `tsc exited ENOENT` is execFile's spawn-failure code, yet the redeploy path records "rebuild of <sha> failed" and blocks that head with no retry until main moves, pinning the fleet on the stale build even after the environment recovers (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** digest warning clusters, 2026-09-28, one day: **64×** `rebuild of 762c5d13 failed — staying on build 66afeacd: tsc exited ENOENT` (loop: harness) — the day's top warning cluster by a factor of seven, against ~178 merges. Sixty-four times the harness told the operator a commit's rebuild failed; not one message says what actually happened, which is that `tsc` never executed at all. `ENOENT` is not a compiler exit code — it is Node's spawn-failure marker on the `execFile` error object, meaning either the `cwd` (the mirror worktree) did not exist at spawn time or `process.execPath` (the running node binary, e.g. after a homebrew upgrade swaps it out) pointed at a vanished path. The failure is environmental, but every one of the 64 warnings names a commit as the thing that failed.
- **Why the response was wrong:** two wrong responses stacked. (1) **Misattribution / self-hiding:** `compileStaged`'s catch built `tsc exited ${String(e.code)}` identically for a spawn failure and a real compile error, so an operator reading 64 commit-failure warnings learned nothing about the broken mirror/toolchain that actually caused them. (2) **Stale latch:** the failure went through `block(mainHead, …)`, setting `blockedHead` — "no retry until main moves". A genuine compile error is a verdict about an immutable tree, so latching is right there; a spawn failure is a verdict about nothing, and the same method already knew it one step earlier: the green check's REJECTED path drops the pending head, sets no `blockedHead`, warns once per episode, and lets the next poll re-run the check (BUGS.md 2026-09-16). Repair the mirror or node_modules while main sits still and the fleet stayed pinned on the stale build indefinitely.
- **Fix:** `compileStaged` (src/build-stage.ts) now returns a `rejected` flag on its result: a spawn failure with no compiler output (`typeof e.code !== "number"` and empty streams — the ENOENT class) and a missing toolchain resolve to `{ ok: false, rejected: true, detail: "could not start the compile: …" }`, naming the environment instead of the commit; a real compiler exit (numeric code or output) is unchanged. `redeploy.poll` (src/redeploy.ts) treats a `rejected` compile like the green check's REJECTED path: no `blockedHead`, one warning per head (`could not start the rebuild of <sha>: … — retrying on the next poll`, a new `compileFailedHead` dedupe key beside `checkFailedHead`), the pending head dropped, and the compile re-attempted on the next poll — a repaired environment redeploys the current head without main moving. A real compiler exit still blocks, unchanged.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in test/redeploy.test.ts: `a compile that never ran is a rejection, not a verdict: retried instead of blocked` (stub returns `rejected` — no `restartBlocked` in status, warning names the environment, compile re-attempted and the same head reaches `restart` with main unmoved, while the real-compile-failure test above still latches); `a rejected compile warns once per head, not once per retry` (a second rejected compile of the same head emits no second warning while still re-attempting the compile; a different head is a new episode and warns again — pinning the `compileFailedHead` dedupe key); `compileStaged reports a spawn failure as a rejection, not a verdict about the tree` (a nonexistent mirror cwd yields `{ ok: false, rejected: true, detail: /could not start the compile/ }`); the missing-typescript test now also asserts `rejected: true`.
- **Validation gap:** no-fake — the suite's fake compile stub could only resolve ok-or-failed, with no way to say "the compile never ran", so no existing test could express — let alone contradict — the misattribution and the latch; confirming the gap needed the `rejected` shape first (closest tag: the stub existed but lacked the vocabulary the bug demanded).

### A whole-reason absence statement reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: nothing to do` (and `nothing refused`, `no refusal`) fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28 shape hunt over the fixed quote bugs, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the negation guard knew `none` and `n/a` — bare, decorated, quoted, dash- and punctuation-appended — but its alternation stopped there, so a loop that found no work and reached for the phrase instead of the token (`nothing to do`, `NOTHING TO REFUSE`, `nothing refused`, `no refusal`) classified as a genuine refusal: handleRefusal destroyed its finished, tested work, the same destruction the `none`-family fixes closed four times over.
- **Reproduce:** one-line node probe over the freshly built module: `isNegatedRefusal('nothing to do')`, `isNegatedRefusal('nothing refused')`, `isNegatedRefusal('no refusal')` all returned `false` while every recorded `none` shape returned `true` — and a probe over the rebuilt module after the first landing of this fix (rejected in review) confirmed the review's finding: `nothing refused` and `no refusal` still returned `false` because that landing only added `nothing to do` and `nothing to refuse`. This re-landing covers all four phrases; the probe over the rebuilt module now returns `true` for each. Pinned by the new regression test.
- **Cause:** the guard's negating-token list (`none|n\/a`) encoded "the loop wrote `none`" rather than "the reason states absence"; a loop that found no work but misremembers the sentinel contract writes the absence as prose, and the anchored appended-note class could not rescue it because the token itself is not `none`.
- **Fix:** the alternation carries all four absence phrases beside `none|n\/a`, under the same anchoring: a phrase followed by the appended-note punctuation class still negates (`nothing to do; the backlog is empty`, `no refusal; the objection was satisfied upstream`), while a phrase followed by a word (`nothing to do with the review`, `no refusal of my own`) stays a refusal.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/reply-contract.test.ts (`isNegatedRefusal negates a whole-reason absence statement`): all four phrases — bare, sentence-punctuated, uppercased, dash- and semicolon-appended — return true; `nothing to do with the review`, `no refusal of my own — the change stands`, and an objection beginning with `nothing` return false.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the suite's shapes all descended from refusals models had actually emitted, so nothing failed; confirming the gap meant first deciding the invariant the token list encodes (a reason that states absence negates, whatever words it uses) and which absence phrases are safe to negate without swallowing real objections. The rejected first landing also showed the gap runs the other way: a probe cited in the record but left out of the fix passes review unnoticed unless the reviewer re-runs it, so every phrase the entry names is now pinned by the test.

### A typographic-quoted negation reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: “none”` (and `”none ”nothing to do”`) fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28 review of the straight-quote fix, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the negation guard accepted straight-quoted dresses (`"none"`, `none "nothing to do"`) and markdown decoration (`**none**`), but its quote handling knew nothing of the typographic quotes a model emits just as often: `“none”`, `’none‘`, `“(**none**)”`, `none “nothing to do”`, `n/a ‘no open bugs’` all returned `false`, so a work-completed reply ending that way had its tested work hard-reset — the same destruction the straight-quote fix one entry below closed for ASCII quotes only.
- **Reproduce:** `isNegatedRefusal('”none ”nothing to do”')` returned `false` on the freshly built module (one-line node probe over the quote matrix); the suite had no case carrying a typographic quote. Pinned by the new regression test.
- **Cause:** the first quote landing added the straight pair `'` `"` to the three places punctuation is treated as formatting (the wrapping strip, the appended-note separator class, unbold) and stopped there; the typographic set `‘ ’ “ ”` — the same two roles, decoration around the token or the opening mark of an appended note — was left out of all three, and a model's quote direction is not reliable besides: a closing quote can open the wrap (`”none …`), so direction-specific classes leave that dress a genuine refusal.
- **Fix:** all four typographic quotes join all three places, and in BOTH anchored classes of the wrapping strip (opening and closing alike, direction-agnostic — the same trade the dash dress already carries), with the appended-note class and unbold carrying the full set too. Each quote is pinned in both roles in the test, including the combined shape `q none q…q` that only the strip classes plus the appended-note class can satisfy — unbold's quote-stripping alone would rescue a bare wrapped token and mask a strip gap, which is exactly how the first attempt at this fix (rejected in review) shipped `”` missing from the strip behind a test that could not see it.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/reply-contract.test.ts (`isNegatedRefusal negates none and n/a under typographic quoting`): for each of ‘ ’ “ ” — wrapped token, appended note, and the strip-sensitive combined shape — plus `”none ”nothing to do”` and `“(**none**)”` return true, while a real reason under curly quotes and a reason that continues with a word after a quoted clause stay refusals.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the recorded rule ("after the token, punctuation cannot begin a reason word") had never been extended to say which side of it the typographic quotes fall on or that both quote directions decorate both sides of the token, and unbold's quote-stripping masked any bare wrapped-token pin, so confirming the strip meant first deciding the invariant and then building a shape only the strip classes can satisfy.

### A flag-shaped `--grep` pattern impersonates the flag it spells: `tumwater logs --grep -f` silently enters follow mode and hangs, and `--grep --since` / `--grep --prompt` are falsely rejected as rival-flag combinations (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** every rival-flag scan in `cmdLogs` ran over the raw argument list, so the token at the `--grep` value's position was read both as the pattern and as the flag it spells. `logs --grep -f` engaged follow mode and hung instead of printing the no-match note and exiting; `logs --grep --since` and `logs --grep --prompt` failed with the "cannot be combined" errors meant for genuine rivals.
- **Reproduce:** in a scratch repo, `logs --grep --since` exited 1 with the combine rejection and `logs --grep -f` was still running three seconds after launch (killed by hand); a plain prose pattern was unaffected. Pinned by the new regression tests.
- **Cause:** `cmdLogs` detects rivals with `args.includes(...)`/`args.indexOf(...)` over the unfiltered argv, while `rejectUnknownArgs` — which runs first — does track flag-value consumption and so happily passed `--grep -f` through (the `-f` was a claimed value, not an unknown token). The two layers disagreed about what a token at a value position means, and the parser side lost.
- **Fix:** `cmdLogs` computes `rest` — args with the `--grep` value's one position removed — and runs every flag scan over it: the rival checks, `follow`, `-n`, `parseRoleScope`, and `showPrompts`. With no `--grep`, `rest` is argv unchanged and every check behaves exactly as before; a genuine rival still fails (it sits at its own position, which the removal never touches).
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in test/cli-logs-filtering.test.ts (`logs --grep treats a flag-shaped pattern as text, not as the flag it spells`, `logs --grep -f does not enter follow mode over its own pattern`): `--grep --since`, `--grep --prompt`, and `--grep -f` each exit 0 with the gentle no-match note — the `-f` case's plain exit within the test harness's 20s bound is the assertion, since pre-fix it hung in follow mode.
- **Validation gap:** real-run-needed (closest tag: the run was offline and local, so nothing about it needed a live model — what was missing was any test case at all handing a flag-shaped token to the parser; the suite's `--grep` cases only ever used prose patterns, so the misclassification surfaced only when the command was run by hand).

### A straight-quoted negation reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none "nothing to do"` (and the wrapped `"none"`) fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the negation guard accepted markdown decoration around the token (`**none**`, `` `n/a` ``) and any punctuation-dress appended note (`none; …`, `none - …`, `none (…)`) — but a straight-quoted dress classified as a genuine refusal: `none "nothing to do"`, `"none"`, `'n/a'`, `"(**none**)"` all returned `false`, so a work-completed reply ending that way had its tested work hard-reset — the same destruction the previous five fixes in this guard closed, one dress at a time.
- **Reproduce:** `isNegatedRefusal('none "nothing to do"')` returned `false` (scratch node run against the freshly built module, one line, probing the dress matrix after the punctuation-separator landing); the suite had no case carrying a straight quote. Pinned by the new regression test.
- **Cause:** straight quotes sat in neither of the two places punctuation is treated as formatting — the wrapping strip (which held only brackets) and the appended-note separator class (which held dashes, an opening parenthesis, and sentence marks) — though a quote is exactly punctuation: it cannot begin a reason word, so by the rule the previous fix recorded it belongs in the class, and around the token it is decoration like the brackets.
- **Fix:** quotes join both — the wrapping strip beside the brackets (`"none"`, `"(**none**)"` unwrap) and the appended-note separator class beside the opening parenthesis. This completes the recorded rule rather than chasing a sixth dress. Accepted tradeoff, same as the dash dress already carried: a real objection that opens a quoted clause right after the token (`none "of these work"`) now negates; a word after the token still refuses, quoted or not.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/reply-contract.test.ts (`isNegatedRefusal negates none and n/a under quoting`): seven quoted shapes return true — wrapped bare, wrapped with punctuation and markdown inside, and the appended-note dress in both quote kinds — while a real reason under quotes and a reason that continues with a word after a quoted clause stay refusals.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the rule on record ("after the token, punctuation cannot begin a reason word") did not say which side of it straight quotes fall on: decoration around the token like brackets and asterisks, or a reason-word opener — so confirming the gap meant first deciding that boundary for the quote character and checking both roles it plays.

### A punctuation-separated explanation after the negation reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: None; nothing to refuse.` fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the negation guard tolerated an appended explanation in the dash dress (`none — bug filed normally`) and the parenthesized dress (`none (no entry refused this run)`) — but the same note riding a sentence mark still classified as a genuine refusal: `None; nothing to refuse.`, `None. Nothing worth doing.`, `None, nothing refused`, `n/a; no open bugs` all returned `false`, so a work-completed reply ending that way had its tested work hard-reset — the same destruction the previous four fixes in this guard closed, one dress at a time.
- **Reproduce:** `isNegatedRefusal("None; nothing to refuse.")` returned `false` (scratch node run against the built module, one line); the suite had no case for any punctuation-separated shape. Pinned by the new regression test.
- **Cause:** the appended-explanation branch matched only a dash or an opening parenthesis right after the token; the separator class omitted the sentence punctuation (`;` `:` `,` `.` `!` `?`) that a model naturally starts a short note with — while `bare()` strips those marks only when they *end* the reason, so `None.` negated but `None. Nothing worth doing.` did not.
- **Fix:** the separator class now accepts any character that cannot begin a reason word — the ASCII hyphen and en/em dashes, an opening parenthesis, or sentence punctuation — as the start of an appended note, still anchored right after the token so a reason that continues with a letter (`none of the attempted fixes work; repro attached`) stays a refusal. This replaces dress-chasing with the rule the previous fixes were each approximating: after the token, punctuation is formatting, a letter is the reason itself. The hyphen is explicitly kept in the class and pinned by test — an earlier landing of this fix dropped it to `[—–;:,.!?(]`, regressing `none - …` / `n/a - …` replies to genuine refusals, and was rejected in review for exactly that. Accepted tradeoff, same as the dash dress already carried: a real objection that opens with the word (`none, I mean it: stop`) now negates.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/reply-contract.test.ts (`isNegatedRefusal negates none and n/a with a punctuation-separated explanation`): eleven punctuation-separated shapes return true — including both hyphen dresses (`none - nothing to do`, `n/a - no open bugs`) and the em-dash dress; a reason that continues with a word despite a semicolon later stays a refusal.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the docstring enumerated the accepted dresses (dash, parenthesized, trailing mark) without stating the underlying rule, so confirming the gap meant first reconstructing that rule (punctuation after the token cannot begin a reason word) and noticing the semicolon/period/comma dresses were still outside it; the review also caught the fix itself narrowing the dash dress, which no test pinned.

### A parenthesized explanation after the negation reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none (no entry refused this run)` fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the negation guard already tolerated a dash-appended explanation (`none — no entry refused this run`), trailing sentence marks (`None.`), and markdown decoration (`**none**`) — but not the same explanation in parenthesized dress: `none (no entry refused this run)`, `None (nothing to do)`, `n/a (nothing fits)` all classified as genuine refusals, so a work-completed reply ending that way had its tested work hard-reset — the same destruction the previous three fixes in this guard closed.
- **Reproduce:** `isNegatedRefusal("none (no entry refused this run)")` returned `false` (scratch node run against the built module, one line); the suite had no case for the shape. Pinned by the new regression test.
- **Cause:** the appended-explanation acceptance lived only in the dash branch of the final shape regex (`[ \t]*[—–-].*`); a parenthesized note after the token matched nothing, and the bracket-stripping pipeline only helps when the token sits *inside* the brackets (`(none) — …`), not when the explanation follows it.
- **Fix:** the shape regex also accepts a token followed by an optional space and an opening parenthesis (`[ \t]*\(.*`), anchored right after the token so a reason that merely begins with the word (`none of the attempted fixes work (repro attached)`) stays a refusal — the same tradeoff the dash-append form already accepts.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/reply-contract.test.ts (`isNegatedRefusal negates none and n/a with a trailing parenthesized explanation`): seven negating shapes return true; a real reason with a trailing note, a reason beginning with the word `none`, and a bracket inside the token (`(no)ne`) stay refusals.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the docstring's "with or without an appended explanation" did not say which dresses an appended explanation may wear, so confirming the gap meant first reconstructing that a parenthesized note is the dash-append form, not part of the reason.

### A zombie stream's content-free `tool_execution_update` keepalives reset the hang watchdog: `progressCount` counted every parsed event, so periodic empty-content updates pinned `allowedSilenceMs` at the short quiet window and a hung tool call could evade the quiet kill forever (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** `PiStreamParser.progressCount`'s docblock promises "a zombie stream's content-free keepalives must not reset the harness's hang watchdog", and runPi's quiet watchdog (src/pi.ts `allowedSilenceMs`) keys directly off it — yet `feedLine` incremented `progressCount` for every parsed event, so a stream emitting periodic `tool_execution_update` events with empty content kept `progressCount` rising and the watchdog never saw silence: the quiet kill (and the doubled zombie window it guards) never fired while the call sat hung.
- **Reproduce:** feed a `PiStreamParser` a `tool_execution_start` followed by three `tool_execution_update` events whose `partialResult` is content-free (`{content:[{type:"text",text:""}]}`, `{content:[]}`, `{}` — the exact shapes `toolUpdateHasContent` rejects for the stall clock); `progressCount` came back 4, one per keepalive, proving each had reset the watchdog. Pinned by the new regression test.
- **Cause:** the keepalive exemption was implemented only for `message_update` (skipped before parsing); content-free `tool_execution_update` events passed the same `progressCount += 1` that real boundaries get, contradicting the invariant the docblock and `toolUpdateHasContent`'s design comment both state.
- **Fix:** `feedLine` counts a `tool_execution_update` as progress only when `toolUpdateHasContent(event.partialResult)` holds — the same single definition of "a content-bearing update" the stall clock already uses — so a keepalive can no longer reset the hang watchdog. Content-bearing updates still count, and the open-call state machine is untouched (a content-free update was already a no-op there).
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/pi-parser.test.ts, merged from the former test/pi-stream.test.ts on 2026-09-29 (`content-free tool_execution_updates do not count as progress (no keepalive reset of the hang watchdog)`): three content-free updates leave `progressCount` at the start event's 1, one content-bearing update raises it to 2.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the guarantee lived only in docblocks that the code contradicted, so confirming the bug meant reconstructing which events count as progress before the scratch repro could prove the gap.

### The dashboards' stall flag never got the piped-stdout exemption the event feed's warning got: `stalledToolLabel` classifies no command shape, so a `npm run test 2>&1 | tail -8` past the threshold still renders `tool call stalled: bash npm run test 2>&1 |…` in the state cell — the same cry-wolf the event feed just stopped (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the previous fix (BUGS.md 2026-09-28) skipped runPi's stall *warning* for piped/redirected commands, but the TUI/GUI in-flight cell's stall flag (src/ui/progress.ts `stalledToolLabel`, surfaced by src/ui/status-model.ts as `tool call stalled: <label>`) shared the tracker yet not the classification: a long piped suite run past `toolCallStallSeconds` still flagged "stalled" on every dashboard poll, so the state cell kept crying wolf about the very command shape the tick prompt prescribes.
- **Reproduce:** parse a role log whose open bash call is `npm run test 2>&1 | tail -8`, then read `stalledToolLabel(calls, Date.now() + 301_000)` — it returned the label as stalled even though runPi's warning for the same call warns nothing. Pinned by the new regression test, which also proves a redirect operator past the 32-char label truncation is only visible through the raw command.
- **Cause:** progress.ts's `tool_execution_start` handler passed only the display label into the shared `applyToolExecutionEvent` state machine — never the raw command — and `stalledToolLabel` checked silence alone, so the shared tracker could not classify what the event feed's fix had taught runPi to classify.
- **Fix:** pi-event-line.ts gains `toolCallCommand(args)` (the `command`/`cmd` extraction pi-stream.ts had inline); pi-stream.ts and progress.ts both feed it to `applyToolExecutionEvent`, and `stalledToolLabel` now skips calls where `commandBuffersOutput(call.command || call.label)` — the same classifier runPi applies, so the state cell and the event feed agree on "stalled". A genuinely hung piped call stays bounded by the quiet watchdog and the tick timeout, as before.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in test/progress.test.ts (`stalledToolLabel skips a call whose stdout is piped or redirected — the tick prompt's own verify shape`): piped command not flagged, redirect past the label truncation not flagged (label shown to hide the `>`), bare sibling still named; the existing bare-command positive control still passes.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the state machine was documented as shared with runPi's warning, but no test pinned the intended guarantee (both surfaces must classify stall identically), so the invariant had to be reconstructed from the comment before the gap was confirmable.
### The harness tells every loop to pipe its verification through `tail`, and the tool-call stall watchdog then files a false "no output for 5m" warning against every suite run that outlasts 5 minutes, so the digest's top warning cluster is healthy runs and a genuinely hung call drowns among them (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** digest warning clusters, 2026-09-28, one day: 9× `tool call stalled: bash npm run test 2>&1 | tail -8 — no output for 5m` (bugfix, coverage, dry, feature +4 more) and 4× `tool call stalled: bash npm run test > /tmp/tw-test2.lo…` — the day's top and fourth warning clusters, thirteen false alarms. None of those calls was stalled: they are the verification runs each tick's own prompt prescribes (`src/prompt.ts` tick rule: "Leave the project working: verify with … Pipe its output through `tail` — only the failures matter", landed in 799f9fb6), and both `| tail` and `> /tmp/file` hold every byte in a pipe or a file until the process exits, so pi's parser sees zero tool-call output for the whole run.
- **Reproduce:** start a pi run whose bash tool call executes `npm run test 2>&1 | tail -8` on a suite that takes > 5 minutes (or a stub command that sleeps > `toolCallStallSeconds` before printing, piped through tail); within ~5 minutes the run logs `tool call stalled: bash npm run test 2>&1 | tail -8 — no output for 5m` though the call completes normally afterwards. Pinned offline by the new regression test, which stalls such a call with the fake-pi shim and asserts no warning.
- **Cause:** the detector (`src/pi.ts`, quietCheck interval, `silentMs = now - call.lastActivityAt`) cannot distinguish "buffered output" from "hung", and its message states "no output for 5m" as evidence of a stall when zero output is exactly what the prescribed command shape guarantees. The wrong response propagates into the event feed, the loop's state cell, and the digest's warning clusters — cry-wolf erodes the one signal a real hang carries.
- **Fix:** the classifier `commandBuffersOutput` (src/command-shape.ts since the 2026-09-30 extraction from src/pi.ts; src/pi.ts and src/ui/progress.ts both import it), which classifies the command text and returns true when stdout cannot reach pi live (a `|` pipeline, or a stdout redirect `>`/`>>`/`&>`; `2>&1`/`2>`/`2>>`/`>&1` keep stdout on pi's pipe and stay warnable). The stall check skips such calls — a genuinely hung piped call is still bounded by the quiet watchdog and the tick timeout. The classifier runs on the call's **full raw command**, which `OpenToolCall` now carries (`applyToolExecutionEvent` takes it from the start event's args): the display label `describeToolCall` produces truncates at 32 chars, so a redirect past that point would be invisible and the skip would silently not apply to exactly the long verification commands the bug is about.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/pi.test.ts` and, for the classifier's shape table, `test/command-shape.test.ts` (`commandBuffersOutput classifies the redirect shapes` — including `2>>` read as one operator, not a fresh stdout redirect — and `a stalled piped-stdout call warns nothing; the redirect is found in the full command, not the truncated label`, which stalls a piped call and a past-32-chars redirect call past the threshold and asserts zero warnings while the quiet watchdog still kills); the existing bare-command positive control (`a stalled tool call warns once with the command named`) still passes, so real hangs stay loudly warned.
- **Validation gap:** no-fake (closest tag — the fake-pi shim existed, but no fixture ever emitted a tool call whose command was piped or redirected, the exact shape the tick prompt prescribes): every stall test used a bare command, and the warning's label truncates at 32 chars, hiding any operator from a label-based assertion, so thirteen false alarms shipped and only the fleet's own digest exposed them.
### The aligned tables size and pad their columns in UTF-16 code units, so a cell holding a wide character (CJK, emoji) renders its row's later columns shifted right of the header and its ASCII neighbors (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** in `tumwater status` (and the TUI's status pane) and `tumwater history`, any row whose cell contains an East Asian wide character or emoji shows every column after that cell starting at a different display column than the same column in the header and the other rows — a ragged table. Custom loop names and tick summaries (model output) can carry such characters; both surfaces derive from user/model text.
- **Reproduce:** render a status snapshot whose loop's `lastSummary` is `翻译了三个文件`, or seed a history event log with `loop: "翻译"`, and compare the display columns of the following cells across rows: the wide-character row's cells sit 3+ columns right. Both cases are pinned as regression tests.
- **Cause:** `renderStatus` computed column widths from `String#length` and padded cells with `padEnd` — both count UTF-16 code units, while `clipToWidth` (which the cells were clipped with) correctly counts terminal display columns. `renderRow` in history.ts carried the same two mistakes. A wide character occupies two display columns but one code unit, so the cell renders wider than its column and the pad cannot bring it back.
- **Fix:** `text.ts` gains `padToWidth` (the display-width sibling of `padEnd`); `status-render.ts` now measures widths with `displayWidth` and pads with `padToWidth`, and `history.ts` does the same for its four padded cells.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/status-render.test.ts` (`status table keeps every column boundary aligned when a cell holds a wide character`) and `test/cli-history.test.ts` (`history keeps the loop column aligned when a loop name holds a wide character`).
- **Validation gap:** no-observability (closest tag — the ragged table is plainly visible in real output but left no trace the suite could fail on): every existing table assertion read cells by character index or matched rows with whitespace-flexible regexes, both blind to display-column drift, so the misrender passed every test while shipping; nothing else was missing once a wide-character fixture existed.

### `tumwater history`'s window growth only chases short row counts, so a window boundary that cuts inside a tick's own event block renders a started tick's duration as a dash even though the log holds its tick_start (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** a `history` (or GUI history tab) row shows `—` for a tick whose `tick_start` IS in the event log. The default scan window (`limit*2+50` events) can end inside a tick's own block: every `tick_end` the ask needs sits within the window, but the oldest one's `tick_start` sits just before the boundary — and because the row count is satisfied, growth keyed on short rows alone never re-reads. A busy fleet's interleaved non-tick events (merges, prompts) make the span between tick_ends large enough to hit this with everyday sizes.
- **Reproduce:** seed a 55-event log — `tick_start #1`, 41 unrelated events, `tick_end #1`, 10 unrelated events, `tick_start #2`, `tick_end #2` — and run `history -n 2`: the window is 54 events, so tick 1's start is dropped and its row reads `—` despite the log holding it. Scratch repro against the built CLI confirmed; now pinned as a CLI-level regression test.
- **Cause:** `readTickRows` grew its window only while `rows.length < limit`. But every `tick_end` the harness logs follows its `tick_start` (`tick()` logs the start before `runTick`, skipped results included), so an unpaired start is either just past the window or lost to rotation — the first case is fixable by scanning, the second only costs the bounded scan.
- **Fix:** the growth loop now also fires while any row's `durationMs` is null (same `events.length >= window` fill check and `HISTORY_SCAN_MAX_EVENTS` bound), so the window grows until unpaired starts are re-paired or the log's start proves them gone.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in `test/cli-history.test.ts` (`history grows the scan window to pair a tick_start the first window's boundary cut off`).
- **Validation gap:** no-observability (closest tag — the misrender was plainly visible in the command's output but left no trace the suite could fail on): pairing was unit-tested at `tickRows` level and window growth at row-count level, so both halves stayed green while the composed path rendered the wrong cell; nothing else was missing once the boundary-cut scenario existed.

### `tumwater logs -f` detects rotation by shrink alone, so a rotation whose replacement file outgrows the old offset within one poll interval reads as an append: the new log's head is never delivered and its first line comes out a mangled mid-line fragment (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** a `logs -f` (or any followFile) stream that spans a log rotation silently skips the replacement log's first lines whenever the fresh file is written past the old consumed offset before the next 500ms poll — and the one line straddling that offset is delivered as a truncated fragment starting mid-line. The shrink path (truncate-in-place, or a fresh empty file seen before it grows) was guarded; the inode-swap-with-growth path was not.
- **Reproduce:** follow a file seeded past its content, `renameSync` it away and `writeFileSync` a replacement LONGER than the old offset before the next poll → the callback receives the replacement's tail from the stale offset (e.g. 31 of 40 `x`s) instead of its whole content. Scratch repro against the built module confirmed; now pinned as a regression test.
- **Cause:** `followFile`'s poll compared sizes only (`size < offset` → restart); its sibling `withTail`'s TailState has carried a dev/ino rotation guard all along, so the follow path simply predates/missed the pattern.
- **Fix:** `followFile` now stats dev/ino each poll and restarts from offset 0 when either changes (the caller's seed offset is still honored on the opening poll); the shrink check stays for same-inode truncation.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in `test/tail.test.ts` (`followFile survives a rotation whose replacement outgrows the old offset within one poll…`).
- **Validation gap:** no-observability (closest tag — the failure left a visible trace in any affected follow stream but none in the suite): the only rotation test covered the shrink variant, so the inode-swap shape shipped with zero coverage and every existing test stayed green; nothing else was missing once that scenario existed.

### `tumwater history` drops an empty cell instead of padding it, so any tick with no usage renders its detail column one position left of its neighbors — a ragged table whenever usage and usage-less ticks mix (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** in a `history` table mixing ticks that carry tokens/cost with ticks that carry neither (skipped ticks, some error paths), the usage-less rows' detail text starts one column-width further left than the others: `renderRow` filtered empty cells out of the join (`cell !== ""`) instead of letting `padEnd` keep them, and `usage` is the one cell that can ever be empty — and it sits mid-row, so dropping it shifts everything after it. The shipped examples all had usage on every row, so the raggedness was invisible until a real fleet log mixed the two.
- **Reproduce:** seed an event log with one `tick_end` carrying `tokens`/`costUsd` and one carrying neither, run `tumwater history` → the detail columns of the two rows start at different character indexes (scratch repro via the built CLI; now pinned as a regression test).
