- **Cause:** the `.filter((cell) => cell !== "")` shipped with the original history feature (5d0f978e), presumably to tidy trailing columns — but `trimEnd()` already strips trailing pad, and the filter's only real effect was to unpad a mid-row cell.
- **Fix:** drop the filter; every cell is padded to its column's width and `trimEnd` removes only the trailing pad. No width computation changes (widths already counted empty usages).
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression test in `test/cli-history.test.ts` (`history CLI keeps the detail column aligned across usage and usage-less rows`: both detail cells must start at the same character index).
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the failure left a visible trace (the ragged table) but none in the suite: the existing CLI smoke assertions matched rows with whitespace-flexible `\s+` regexes, which cannot distinguish a padded column from a dropped one, so the misalignment passed every test while visible in any real output.

### The GUI's /api/history re-derived the fixed scan window the CLI had just outgrown: a role-filtered ask on a busy fleet silently returns fewer rows than the log holds, down to an empty set (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** `GET /api/history?role=qa&n=5` returned `{ rows: [] }` (or short) while the event log held 5 qa `tick_end`s: `handleHistory` computed `n * 2 + 50` and passed the role filter straight to `tickRows` — the exact dilution arithmetic the eaf73307 CLI fix had replaced with a growing window. The endpoint comment still claimed "the identical arithmetic cmdHistory uses", but the feature commit that added the endpoint (495525f6) landed after that fix and copied the pre-fix heuristic, so the dashboard's history tab shows `no ticks yet` for a quiet role in a busy fleet while the CLI prints its rows.
- **Reproduce:** seed an event log with 5 qa tick_ends followed by 60 clean tick_ends, request `/api/history?role=qa&n=5` → `rows: []` (pinned as a regression test in `test/gui-server.test.ts`; the red run is recorded above).
- **Cause:** the growth loop lived inside `cmdHistory` instead of next to `tickRows`, so the second consumer re-derived the stale window arithmetic from the comment rather than sharing the code.
- **Fix:** the scan is extracted to `readTickRows(root, limit, role)` in `src/ui/history.ts` — window growth and the `HISTORY_SCAN_MAX_EVENTS` cap included — and both `cmdHistory` and `handleHistory` call it, so the surfaces cannot drift again. No behavior change for the unfiltered path; the filtered path gains the growth the CLI already had.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/gui-server.test.ts` (a role-filtered `/api/history` returning all 5 of the role's ticks across a diluting sibling; and the honest-shortfall case where the log is exhausted below the ask — 5 rows, not an error).
- **Validation gap:** no-repro — closest tag, on the eaf73307 precedent: the bug reproduces deterministically once suspected (a seeded 65-event log and one fetch), but nothing in the suite reproduced it first — the endpoint's own test fixtures were small enough for the fixed window to cover, so the dilution failure existed only in production-shaped logs, and the find was diffing the endpoint's window comment against the CLI's now-different code rather than running anything.

### `tumwater history --role` scans a window sized for the unfiltered case, so a quiet role in a busy fleet silently prints fewer rows than its retained log holds — down to `no ticks yet` for a role that had ticks (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** `history --role qa -n 5` printed `no ticks yet` (or fewer than 5 rows) while the event log held 5 qa `tick_end`s: `cmdHistory` read a fixed `limit * 2 + 50`-event window, and the `--role` filter then discarded every other loop's events, so a sibling loop just 60 events busy swallowed the role's entire recent history. The unfiltered path was sized correctly by design (tick_starts and unrelated events interleave with the tick_ends scanned for); the filtered path reuses the same window without accounting for the dilution the filter itself adds.
- **Reproduce:** seed an event log with 5 qa tick_ends followed by 60 clean tick_ends, run `tumwater history --role qa -n 5` → `no ticks yet` (scratch repro before the fix; now pinned as a regression test).
- **Cause:** the window heuristic predates the `--role` filter and was never re-derived for it; the seeded-log tests could not see the gap because every fixture was small enough for the first window to cover all of it.
- **Fix:** `cmdHistory` grows the scan window (×4 steps, capped at `HISTORY_SCAN_MAX_EVENTS = 20_000`) while the filtered rows fall short AND the scan filled its window — `events.length < window` means the tail scan reached the log's start, so a real shortfall stops the loop and prints what exists, honestly. `tickRows` stays pure over the events it is given; the growth is an I/O concern and lives in the command half. One ask's worst-case read stays bounded (readTailText reads bytes proportional to the window's line count).
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/cli-history.test.ts` (a quiet role's ticks found by growth after a diluting sibling loop fills the first window; and the honest-shortfall case where the log is exhausted below the ask — 5 rows, not a hang).
- **Validation gap:** no-repro — closest tag, since the wording does not quite fit: the bug reproduces deterministically in seconds once suspected, but nothing in the suite reproduced it first — every seeded fixture was small enough for the fixed window to cover, so the dilution failure existed only in production-shaped logs, and the find was reading the window heuristic against the filter rather than running anything.

### The digest counts `main_red` in the Outcome table but itemizes its cause nowhere: the error-clusters section filters `result === "error"`, so the one outcome that blocks every merge fleet-wide reads as a bare count (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the Outcome table recorded `main_red` per role while no cluster section said what broke: the error-clusters filter took only `result === "error"` tick_ends, and the baseline path that produces the table's main_red cells (`mainRedGate`) set no cause at all — its `tick_end` carried no `error` field, so the cause survived only as a once-per-sha harness warning somewhere in the warning section's top-N, unlinked to the count that made it matter. The landing path (lander.ts `gate check failed: …`) rode a role warning the same way.
- **Cause:** the filter was written when the only concern was successful ticks' leftover `lastError` (BUGS.md 2026-09-21), and the main_red outcome was shaped around the same time without revisiting the digest; each main_red path raised its own alarm in the feed, so the gap only showed when someone read the digest instead of the log.
- **Fix:** both halves together, since either alone leaves the other unexplained: (1) `mainRedGate` now carries the cause on its outcome (`error: main <sha> is red (<script>: <headline>) — authoring skipped until main is green`), and loop.ts folds it onto `state.lastError` so the tick's `tick_end` logs it like every other failure's — written for that result, never a leftover, so the 2026-09-21 invariant (a successful tick must not contribute a leftover landing failure to the error section) is untouched; (2) the error-clusters filter accepts main_red tick_ends that carry an error, and the render's section title restates the widened contract — "Top error clusters (red-main causes included)" — so the section's total now reads as the error column PLUS the main_red cells, not the error column alone. The landing path's cause still rides its role warning: it never produces a main_red tick_end cell, so there is no count left unexplained.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/failure-report.test.ts` (a main_red tick's cause clusters with the tick errors and the title carries the red-main note; a causeless main_red stays out), `test/main-red.test.ts` (the gate's outcome pins the error text: sha, script, headline), and `test/loop-red-main.test.ts` (the red-main tick_end event carries the cause end to end).
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — nothing in the suite reproduced it because the contract itself had to be reconstructed first: which section owns a main_red cause, and which Outcome columns the section's total must equal, existed nowhere as an assertion (the 2026-09-21 filter invariant covered only leftover-pollution), so the closest tag is unclear-invariant rather than no-repro — the failure was deterministic, the missing piece was the spec it violates.

### A merge-scope build check whose own deadline-lateness names the host is rejected as a deterministic `build check failed` with no retry: the equally verdict-less killed case earns one retry, the timeout case earns none (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** digest rejection cluster, 1× 2026-09-28: `build check failed (\`npm run test\`): landing build check timed out after 356.7s (its 300s deadline fired 56.7s late: the host was asleep or the harness stalled)` — a landing whose check made no verdict about the tree was recorded as a deterministic tree failure and the author's tick was spent into a rejection. The merge-scope remap in `runScopedBuildCheck` turned any `timeout` or `killed` skip into `status: "failed"`, and `checkFailureReasons` prefixed it with `build check failed (\`npm run test\`)` — the same headline a genuine assertion failure gets. Its own comment said "a timeout or signal kill at a merge scope is not environmental" — exactly backwards for the timeout half: the same code path carries `run.deadlineLateMs`, and past `DEADLINE_LATE_TOLERANCE_MS` the harness's own reason text says "the host was asleep or the harness stalled". BUGS.md's 2026-09-21 fix measured this host: 31 of 32 over-bound checks overlapped a clamshell sleep and ran only 0.1–56 s awake — the check did not run a slow suite; it ran seconds and was killed at a wake.
- **Cause:** the remap (5b125c40) landed before the deadline-lateness measurement existed, when every timeout looked alike; the 2026-09-21 fix added the evidence (`deadlineLateMs`) and reworded the reason, and the killed-retry fix (e717f842) added the retry precedent, but neither revisited the remap's "not environmental" premise, so a check the harness knew was asleep-killed was still scored as a failed build on the first occurrence.
- **Fix:** at a merge scope, a timeout whose `deadlineLateMs` exceeds the late tolerance is environmental by the harness's own wording, so it now gets the killed path's treatment — one retry (each attempt priced as its own `build_check` event), and only the retry's verdict stands; an on-time timeout — real slowness, a genuinely hung or oversized suite — still rejects on the first occurrence. When a merge-scope kill or timeout does reject, `checkFailureReasons` passes the unverified reason through verbatim (the outcome carries `unverified: true`): no `build check failed` prefix, so the author is told the tree is unverified — not sent hunting a test failure that never happened. The 5b125c40 invariant stands: an unverified tree never lands.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/build-check.test.ts` (a late merge-scope timeout is retried once and a clean retry's verdict stands; a late timeout on both attempts rejects unverified, without the red-build prefix; the existing on-time-timeout test now also pins that no retry is owed).
- **Validation gap:** no-fake — confirming the distinction needed a clock fixture that did not exist for this scope: a Date-only mock that jumps the wall clock while the real deadline timer runs (the 2026-09-21 pin had it only at the gate scope), and a fully mocked clock silently makes every deadline fire on time, testing the wrong case while looking green.

### A markdown-decorated negation reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: **none**` fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** a work-completed reply whose sentinel reason is wrapped in markdown emphasis or
  code spans — `**none**`, `` `n/a` `` — is classified as a deliberate refusal; the tick's
  committed, tested change is discarded exactly like the bare-`none` destruction of 2026-09-23
  and the punctuated-`None.` destruction of the same morning.
- **Reproduce:** `isNegatedRefusal("**none**")` returned `false` (scratch node run against the
  built `dist/src/reply-contract.js` before the fix); the negation tests listed plain,
  parenthesized, and punctuated shapes only.
- **Cause:** the guard's normalizations stripped brackets and trailing sentence punctuation but
  never markdown emphasis (`*`, `_`, backticks), so a decorated token stayed unequal to
  `none`/`n/a` and the negation regex unmatched. Decoration also nests inside the brackets
  (`(**None**) — nothing refused`), so edge-only bracket stripping left the inner `)` wedged
  against the token.
- **Fix:** compose the normalizations into one pipeline — decoration (`*_~\`` → space), then
  trailing sentence marks, then every bracket run → space — each seeing the last's output;
  brackets become spaces, not deletions, so `(no)ne` cannot collapse into `none`. A real reason
  under emphasis (`**it would delete user data**`) still refuses.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/reply-contract.test.ts` (first landing of
  this fix was rejected in review for a comment claiming more than the code did — the composed
  shape now actually negates, verified by execution).
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant (closest tag — the missing piece was really an untested
  shape) — the suite pinned the plain, punctuated, and parenthesized negation shapes, but the
  invariant that formatting on the token is not part of the reason was never stated, so the
  decorated form only surfaced through a scratch repro against the built code.

### The gate's flake re-run verified a tree whose memory-bound assertion had just failed: `server retained ~1.1MB of a rejected body` in test/gui-server.test.ts measures whole-process heap, so a real transient retention and ordinary host noise are indistinguishable and both pass (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** the gate's pre-check failed the "oversized prompt bodies stop buffering at the cap
  (no unbounded growth)" test, the one immediate re-run passed, and the tree was verified with no
  fix run — an intermittent memory-invariant violation was waved through as weather, untrustworthy
  in both directions: host noise can fail a clean tree, and a re-run can pass a retaining one.
- **Cause:** the assertion measured a whole-process `heapUsed` delta across one gc'd 300 ms
  settle window — and `globalThis.gc` is a no-op without `--expose-gc`, so the delta counted
  garbage and every unrelated allocation on the shared host too; it said nothing about the rejected
  body's own bytes, which is the invariant it claimed to pin.
- **Fix:** made the invariant directly observable — `readBody` in src/ui/gui-endpoints.ts now
  tracks the wire bytes it holds (new exported `bufferedBodyBytes()`, zeroed at every settle;
  `MAX_BODY_BYTES` exported too), and the test asserts during the full 4MB upload that the
  counter never exceeds the cap plus one chunk and is 0 once the upload drains, instead of a
  process-wide heap delta. Simulating the retention regression fails the rewritten test in ~50 ms,
  so a retained buffer is distinguishable from host noise by construction.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/gui-server.test.ts`.
- **Validation gap:** no-observability — the invariant (buffered bytes capped, then released at
  rejection) had no observable: the only check measured whole-process heap, so real retention and
  host noise were indistinguishable and a pass confirmed nothing; the fix had to add a
  buffered-bytes counter before the gap was confirmable at all.

### A negated refusal with trailing sentence punctuation reads as a genuine refusal: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: None.` fails `isNegatedRefusal`, so the harness hard-resets the tick's finished, tested work (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

- **Symptom:** a work-completed reply whose sentinel line carries a sentence mark after the
  negation — `None.`, `N/A.`, `(none).` — is classified as a deliberate refusal; the tick's
  committed, tested change is discarded exactly like the bare-`none` destruction of 2026-09-23.
- **Reproduce:** `isNegatedRefusal("None.")` returned `false` (scratch node run against
  `src/reply-contract.ts` before the fix); the negation test listed unpunctuated shapes only.
- **Cause:** the negation guard compared exact tokens (`none`, `n/a`, parenthesized, dash-appended)
  without stripping trailing sentence punctuation, so a period left the token unequal and the
  negation regex unmatched.
- **Fix:** strip trailing punctuation (`.,!;:?!*`) from both the raw and unwrapped reason before
  the token comparison; a real reason with a period (`Nothing here was refused.`) still refuses.
- **Fixed:** 2026-09-28, regression tests in `test/reply-contract.test.ts`.
- **Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the suite pinned the negation shapes by example, but
  whether sentence punctuation after `none` still negates was unspecified, so the invariant had to
  be reconstructed (punctuation is not an objection) before the gap was confirmable.

### A rejection note rides every later prompt undated: a `reject` in `lastReview` is injected verbatim until some future landing overwrites it, so an objection main has since satisfied keeps telling the author to address a verdict that no longer holds (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-28)

**Symptom:** This tick's telemetry prompt carries "Your previous change was rejected in review: 1. md-only BUGS.md edit moves … to Fixed, but none of the symbols its Fix paragraph names exist on this tree: setLandingStage — land the fix in the same commit, or keep the bug Open until the code exists". That verdict was true when issued — `setLandingStage` entered main with 127157a4 (2026-09-24 01:32), after the rejected edit was gated — and became false half an hour later. The note carries no date, so telling stale from live took archaeology: the symbol's introduction commit from `git log -S`, plus the digest's outcome table (5 telemetry ticks today, none of them the editing tick) to establish the rejection predates the digest's window. The digest correctly reports telemetry rejections 0 today — the event is out of its 1-day window — so the undated note is the only trace of the stale verdict, and nothing else names it.

**Why the response is wrong:** The rejection is recorded with its time (`state.lastReview = { verdict, reasons, head, at: Date.now() }` in src/review.ts's reject path), but `buildRejectedReviewNote` (src/gate-prompts.ts) renders only the reasons, and src/tick-prompt.ts injects it whenever `lastReview.verdict === "reject"`. A `reject` persists until overwritten: the approve/failed paths set `lastReview` only on model-reviewed landings, while the md-only exempt fast path (the very kind of change this note is about) never touches it — so the note rode ~37 hours and at least 6 telemetry prompts across two later filings, repeatedly instructing the author to "address the objections" to an objection main had already answered. An author that obeys literally re-does work against a moving target or "takes a different approach" from a stale objection.

**Reproduce:** Reject any role's md-only BUGS.md edit through the false-fix check while the named symbol is genuinely absent from the tree; let another loop land that symbol on main; every later prompt of the rejected role (until one of its changes goes through a model review) still carries the rejection undated, with no hint that main moved past it.

**Expected:** The injected note carries the rejection's timestamp (and head), e.g. "Your previous change was rejected in review (2026-09-24 01:20):", so the author can weigh it against main's current state instead of treating it as fresh. A stricter variant re-runs the deterministic check the rejection came from (`falseFixReason` for md-only objections) at injection time and annotates objections that no longer reproduce.

**Suspected cause:** `buildRejectedReviewNote(reasons)` takes only the reasons array (src/gate-prompts.ts); the `at` and `head` fields sit on `state.lastReview` unused, and nothing revalidates the note against main before injecting it.

**Fix:** `buildRejectedReviewNote` now takes the whole `lastReview` record, not just its reasons: when `at` is recorded it renders "Your previous change was rejected in review (2026-09-24 01:20:32, head 8b58124):", naming the reviewed head in short form, and with neither field it degrades to the previous undated shape (src/gate-prompts.ts). src/tick-prompt.ts passes `state.lastReview` through instead of slicing out the reasons. Pinned by test/gate-prompts.test.ts (the dated shape with head, the degraded shape without) and test/tick-prompt.test.ts (the assembled next-tick prompt carries the date and head); the two orchestrator-level rejection tests (test/loop-3.test.ts, test/loop-4.test.ts) now assert the dated prefix. Full suite: 1873 pass, 0 fail.

**Validation gap:** real-run-needed — the note-shape unit test pinned the builder from reasons alone and stayed green while the bug rode live prompts, so confirming staleness took a real fleet run's prompt chain (37 hours of undated rides) plus `git log -S` archaeology to date the objection against main's history.

### A suite that a fleet runs inherits the operator's `TUMWATER_PI_BIN`, which outranks the fake pis the suite puts on PATH: every fake-pi test spawns the agent binary it names (found 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

**Symptom:** On 2026-09-28 a scratch `tumwater run --once --role clean` was started with `TUMWATER_PI_BIN` pointing at a Node pi stand-in. The tick's baseline check failed with an ENOENT on a test's prompts.log and reported "main f5a30628 is red". The stand-in had been invoked 150 times as author and 128 times as reviewer, all from the suite's temp dirs, and it recursed into further suite runs. With the stand-in first on PATH instead, the same tick ran clean. plans/portability.md makes `TUMWATER_PI_BIN` a supported way to point a fleet at a wrapper around pi. A real fleet started that way would therefore run the suite's fake-pi tests against the real agent: real model calls from tests (PRINCIPLES.md), real spend, a failed gate and a red main.

**Reproduce:** `TUMWATER_PI_BIN=<a script that logs its cwd and exits 1> node dist/src/test-runner.js pi.test loop-pi`. Before this fix, 30 of 70 tests failed and the script ran 36 times from test temp dirs. The same run without the variable passes 70/70. A direct `node --test dist/test/pi.test.js dist/test/loop-pi.test.js` under the same export fails the same way.

**Cause:** resolveAgentBin (src/pi.ts) resolves `TUMWATER_PI_BIN` → `agentBin` → `"pi"`, so the variable beats PATH. The suite installs every fake pi by prepending PATH, through fakePi in test/fake-pi.ts; fakePiIdle, recordingFakePi and the reviewerPi scripts all go through it. runPi and runScriptGroup start pi and the build check with the harness's own environment, so every tool call and every build check inherits the operator's export. suiteEnv (src/test-runner.ts) then built the suite's environment by adding to process.env and never removed anything from it. A suite run by hand never has the variable, which is why only suites a fleet started failed. Agents also run test files directly with `node --test`, which bypasses the runner entirely. That was 42 of the 236 test invocations in the fleet's retained pi logs, and it included fake-pi files: orchestrator-2.e2e (8 times), the lander files, pi and review.

**Fix:** suiteEnv now drops the harness's own variables from the suite's environment. `TUMWATER_PI_BIN` goes, and so does `SUPERVISED_ENV` (`TUMWATER_SUPERVISED`). The supervisor sets that marker for the orchestrator child, and a `tumwater run` that inherits it skips its supervisor half. No test depended on the marker: the full suite passes with it set, because test/cli-harness.ts already drops it from every CLI child. It is the same fleet-only difference, though, so it goes too. test/fake-pi.ts also deletes an inherited `TUMWATER_PI_BIN` when it loads, which covers a file run directly with `node --test`. Every test that can spawn pi, in-process or through a CLI `run`, imports that module. Tests of the variable itself (pi.test.ts, doctor.test.ts, startup-gate.test.ts, cli.test.ts) set it explicitly and are unchanged. NODE_OPTIONS is kept on purpose. The harness's LaunchServices preload there only changes where process.title is stored, the suite's assertions about it hold either way, and it also carries the operator's own Node flags. After the fix, the reproduction passes with no failures both through the runner and through a direct `node --test`, and the stand-in never runs. The full suite (1,870 pass) and the e2e tier (77 pass) also pass with the stand-in in `TUMWATER_PI_BIN`, the marker set and the preload inherited, and the stand-in never runs. A direct `node --test` over every test file under the same export never runs it either; its one failure is the doctor check under Remaining. Pinned by test/test-runner.test.ts (suiteEnv drops both variables and passes the rest through) and test/fake-pi.test.ts (a child that imports the helpers with the variable exported no longer sees it).

**Remaining:** A file that does not import test/fake-pi.ts still sees the variable when it is run directly with `node --test`. Today such files only resolve or report the binary and never spawn it: with the variable exported, doctor.test.ts fails one default-source check instead. A new variable that the harness honors has to be dropped in suiteEnv too.

**Validation gap:** no-observability — closest fit: the only trace was a red baseline with an ENOENT on a test's prompts.log, nothing records which binary a test's pi spawn actually ran, and a suite run by hand never has the variable, so seeing it took a real tick under the export and the stand-in's own invocation count; once suspected, a filtered runner run with a logging stand-in in `TUMWATER_PI_BIN` confirmed it offline in seconds.

### Every npm and pi process the fleet starts on macOS leaks a launchservicesd Mach port, until the kernel kills launchservicesd and the GUI session wedges (found 2026-09-28, fixed 2026-09-28)

**Symptom:** On 2026-09-25 the Mac running the fleet (macOS 26.6.2) wedged: apps could not be quit, Finder disappeared, and it needed a forced power-off. The unified log shows launchservicesd leaking Mach ports for twelve days. The "Excessive number of mach_ports for launchservicesd" fault first appeared on Sep 13 at ~28.7K ports. The count grew fastest on heavy fleet and test-suite days: about +27K per day on Sep 21–22, +38K on Sep 23, and +75K in the 10 h before the failure. At 09:57:23 on Sep 25 the kernel killed launchservicesd with termination reason (30, 267967, 9), where namespace 30 is OS_REASON_PORT_SPACE. A node crash report from that minute shows `node::ProcessTitleSetter → uv_set_process_title → _LSApplicationCheckIn`, and Force Quit listed "pi", "npm run test" and "npm run build" as apps.

**Reproduce:** Still leaks on macOS 27.0. Read the count with `top -l 1 -stats pid,command,ports | grep launchservicesd`. Then 200 runs of `node -e 'process.title="x"'` add ~200 ports, and they are still there a minute later; 200 runs of `node -e 0` leave the count flat. Before this fix, one `npm test` added +142 ports (276 check-ins in the unified log). One real `tumwater run --once --role clean` tick added +420 (830 check-ins). That tick had the baseline check, an author run whose tool call ran `npm test`, the gate check and a review.

**Cause:** Assigning `process.title` in Node calls libuv's uv_set_process_title. On macOS that registers the process with LaunchServices (`_LSApplicationCheckIn`). launchservicesd keeps one port for each process that ever checked in, and never frees it when the process exits: one port per process, however many times the title is set. npm sets its title in every run and pi sets one at startup. tumwater's own code never does, and neither do tsc, the node:test workers or the CLI children. A logging preload attributed all 276 check-ins of a suite run to 138 npm processes. One was the outer `npm test`. The other 137 came from build-check tests that go through runBuildCheck → runScriptGroup → `npm run <script>`: lander-restack, lander-bisect and review (26 each), build-check and main-baseline (17 each), loop-2 (8), lander (7), and loop-4, merge and redeploy (3 each). A fleet tick runs the suite about three times, plus two pi processes, which is exactly the 830. From events.jsonl and the pi sessions, Sep 21–23 comes to about 42–60K npm + pi processes a day. The observed growth was +27–38K. The estimate assumes today's 137 inner npm processes per suite run, which is probably high for those dates. On Sep 24 the fleet was nearly idle, and the count still grew +9.7K from hand-run suites.

**Fix:** src/process.ts `NO_LAUNCH_SERVICES_CHECK_IN` is a NODE_OPTIONS `--import=data:` preload that replaces `process.title` with a plain accessor, so an assignment only stores the string. `withoutLaunchServicesCheckIn` appends it to the env on macOS, once. runPi uses it, which covers pi and every tool call, and so does runScriptGroup, which covers every build check and every npm a suite's build-check tests start. The preload has to be an accessor: an `Object.defineProperty` with `value` goes through Node's native setter, so the process checks in by itself. The first version of this fix made that mistake and made every Node process check in. The preload is inline rather than a file, so a rebuild or swap of dist/ cannot leave NODE_OPTIONS naming a missing module. Measured after the fix: `npm test` produced 2 check-ins from one process, the outer npm (−4 ports). The same tick produced 0 check-ins (−3). Suite wall time was 20.6–20.8 s against 20.9–21.1 s, and one npm start 88 ms against 94 ms. src/launch-services.ts adds the health check: doctor's `mach ports` line, and a daemon fleet's `LaunchServicesWatch`. The watch takes one `top` sample every 15 minutes. It logs a harness warning at 100K ports and again every 50K, days before the ~268K kill. Pinned by test/process.test.ts, where the preload must keep a title out of `ps` and the `value` variant fails; test/pi.test.ts; test/launch-services.test.ts; and test/launch-services.e2e.test.ts.

**Remaining:** An npm that a person or another tool runs directly still checks in once, before any tumwater code runs: 1 port per `npm test` instead of 138. Only a restart resets launchservicesd, because it is a system daemon; logging out does not. A managed project whose toolchain runs Node older than 18.18 would reject `--import` in NODE_OPTIONS.

**Validation gap:** real-run-needed — the leak lives in a system daemon that nothing tumwater reads, so confirming it took bounded real runs on macOS with the port count read from `top` and the check-ins from the unified log; offline, the suite can only pin the mechanism, a title kept out of `ps`.


### A role prompt's over-cap rejection names the director's prefill whatever loop it targets: `tumwater prompt --role qa` past DIRECTOR_PROMPT_MAX_CHARS fails with "it rides into the director tick's prefill" (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25)

**Symptom:** submit an over-long prompt to any role loop (`tumwater prompt --role qa`, or the GUI/TUI per-row prompt bar): the rejection correctly refuses to queue it, but the message tells the operator the text "rides into the director tick's prefill" — the wrong loop. The operator trimming the text is pointed at a queue that was never involved.

**Reproduce:** `tumwater prompt --role qa "$(python3 -c 'print("x"*50000)')"` → the thrown message names the director.

**Cause:** `promptLengthProblem` (src/inbox.ts) hardcoded "the director tick's prefill" when the cap was shared with the per-role queues (PLANS.md "Per-role prompts 1/2"); neither it nor the GUI's shared `requirePromptText` validator takes the target loop.

**Fix:** `promptLengthProblem(text, role)` interpolates the target loop into the reason (`the ${role} tick's prefill`), defaulting to the director so every existing caller and the director-path wording are unchanged; `submitRolePrompt` passes its role and the GUI's `requirePromptText` takes the role its endpoint validated (`/api/prompt-role` after `rejectBadRole`, so the name is always a real loop).

**Validation gap:** no-observability — closest fit: the failure leaves a trace (a wrong 400/throw message), but the suite's only over-cap assertion checked a `prefill` substring on the director path, so no check ever observed which loop the message names and the miswording passed every run; the regression test now pins the target loop's name and the director word's absence.

### A fresh operator wake does not exempt a due maintenance tick from need-based deferral: `tumwater wake --role clean` on an idle loop is silently deferred for up to DEFER_MAX_MS (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25)

**Symptom:** `tumwater wake --role clean` on a maintenance loop whose last tick found nothing: the wake clears backoff and pulls `nextRunAt` to now, `isEligible` returns due (reason `scheduled`, via the `wokenAt` min-gap exemption) — and then the orchestrator's need-based deferral block defers the tick anyway: `deferTick` sees `lastResult === "no_change"`, a `lastMainHead` set by the just-ended tick, and no work landed since it, so the tick waits until work lands or DEFER_MAX_MS (3 h) expires. The operator's explicit "try again now" is silently ignored, the same no-feedback failure the wake-vs-min-gap fix (2026-09-25) removed from the gap gate. Discovered while validating the mid-tick wake fix: the e2e's woken `clean` tick was due on every poll yet never ran.

**Cause:** `deferTick` predates the wake lever and keys only on need (backlog, landed work, `lastResult`); a fresh `wokenAt` is exactly the explicit-demand signal `isEligible` already uses for the min-gap exemption, but the deferral predicate never consults it.

**Fix:** `deferTick` (src/scheduling.ts) returns false when `s.wokenAt` is newer than `s.lastTickEndedAt` — the same predicate isEligible's min-gap exemption keys on, so the two gates now agree on what an operator wake means. An operator wake is a demand, not idle maintenance: it runs even with the backlog open, the way the director's `inbox` reason already skips deferral. A wake older than the last tick's end was already consumed by the tick that ran after it, so that state keeps deferring as idle maintenance; `restoreMidTickWake`'s floored stamp keeps the demand armed across the end-save, so the post-tick wake tick runs too.

**Validation gap:** no-fake — closest fit: no shim was missing, but no existing test seeded the fresh-wake state (`wokenAt` newer than `lastTickEndedAt`) `deferTick` had never seen; the suite's wake tests stop at isEligible's min-gap exemption and its deferTick tests seed only idle states, so the interaction had no fixture until this run's regression test built it with the real `clearBackoff`.

### A plain `tumwater wake --role` consumed while the target's tick is in flight is still clobbered by that tick's end-save, so the "try again now" demand silently waits out the whole interval (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25)

**Symptom:** `tumwater wake --role qa` lands while qa's tick is running (no queued prompt — the queue-due-ness fix does not cover this): `requestWake` stamps the state file, `consumeWakeRequest` applies `r.wake()` to the in-memory state, but the in-flight tick's end-save stays authoritative — `applyTickOutcome` re-stamps `lastTickEndedAt` past `wokenAt` and schedules `nextRunAt` a fresh interval out, so the wake is gone: qa sleeps out its remaining two-hour clock with no feedback. The 2026-09-25 wake-clobber fix recorded this residual as "an operator can simply wake again"; a wake is the operator's liveness lever during exactly the long-tick stalls where re-typing it blind is the worst workaround.

**Reproduce:** Unit-level: a qa state with `lastTickStartedAt = now - 2 s`, `running`, `nextRunAt = start + 2 h`; stamp a mid-tick wake (`Object.assign(s, clearBackoff(s, start + 1 s))`, what `r.wake()` does to the shared state), then run `applyTickOutcome(s, cfg, "qa", { result: "no_change" })` — `isEligible`'s inputs now read `wokenAt < lastTickEndedAt` and a fresh-gap `nextRunAt`, so the loop is not due for a full interval despite the wake one second into the tick.

**Cause:** Same race the wake-clobber fix identified — wake-stamping cannot survive a concurrent tick's end-save by construction — but the prompt side got a durable record (the queue file) while the prompt-less wake was left to the clobber. The demand needs to survive the end-save itself: the tick knows a wake arrived mid-flight because `wokenAt` is stamped past its own `lastTickStartedAt` on the shared state object.

**Fix:** New `state.ts` `restoreMidTickWake(s)`, called by `LoopRunner.tick()`'s end-save after `applyTickOutcome`: when `wokenAt` is stamped after the tick's start (and no `resumePending` deliberate wait is set), it re-applies `clearBackoff` — backoff cleared, `nextRunAt` now, `wokenAt` floored just past the new `lastTickEndedAt` so `isEligible`'s min-gap exemption re-arms even on a same-millisecond stamp. A wake older than the tick's start was already honored by the tick that ran (self-clearing holds), and a cut-off/aborted outcome's `resumePending` keeps its deliberate one-interval resume wait — a mid-tick wake does not shortcut it.

**Validation gap:** no-fake — confirming it deterministically needed a fixture that stamps a mid-tick wake against a running tick's timeline (past `lastTickStartedAt`, ahead of the end-save's `lastTickEndedAt`) and then runs the real `applyTickOutcome`, which neither the existing wake tests (they seed hour-old ticks that dodge the race) nor any unit test built; the live race is timing-shaped and the fixed wake-clobber entry's own repro stopped at the prompt side.

### A wake consumed while the target's tick is in flight is clobbered by that tick's end-save, so a prompt enqueued mid-tick waits out a full fresh min-gap despite the `p:N` marker (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25)

**Symptom:** `tumwater prompt --role qa` lands while qa's tick is running: `requestWake` writes the state file and marker, `consumeWakeRequest` applies `r.wake()` to the in-memory state — but the in-flight tick holds the same state object and its end-save stays authoritative (loop.ts's wake() docstring says so itself): `applyTickOutcome` re-stamps `lastTickEndedAt` past `wokenAt` and schedules `nextRunAt` a full interval out, so the queued prompt waits out the whole fresh gap (qa: up to 2 h) even though the dashboards show `p:1`.

**Cause:** wake-stamping cannot survive a concurrent tick's end-save by construction, so any fix keyed on the marker races the same save. The durable record of the demand is the queue file itself — it survives every end-save — so due-ness should come from the queue, the way the director's `inbox` reason already works.

**Fix:** `isEligible` now treats a non-empty per-role inbox as due on its own existence (`reason: "inbox"`) — no min-gap, no backoff, no scheduled clock — placed after the resume gate so a queued prompt never preempts a cut-off resume's deliberate wait (a resuming tick builds `buildResumePrompt` and does not dequeue the queue; the prompt is consumed by the fresh tick the resume's end makes due immediately). The orchestrator passes each runner its own queue size (`inboxSize(root, role)`; the director's count is unchanged), and the wake event logs `reason: "inbox"` for the role. The `wokenAt` min-gap exemption stays for wake-without-prompt (`tumwater wake --role`). Note the residual narrow race the queue fix does not cover: a plain wake with an empty queue consumed mid-tick is still clobbered by the end-save — an operator can simply wake again. (Closed 2026-09-25: `restoreMidTickWake` now re-applies that demand at the tick's end-save — see the plain-wake entry above.)

**Validation gap:** no-fake — confirming it deterministically needed a fixture that seeds a queued per-role prompt against a fresh min-gap window with the wake already clobbered (fresh `lastTickEndedAt`, hours-out `nextRunAt`, no `wokenAt`), which neither the existing wake e2e (it dodged the gap by seeding an hour-old tick) nor any unit test built; the original mid-tick repro stayed timing-shaped until that fixture existed.

### An operator wake cannot pull a slow-clock loop in: `isEligible`'s min-gap check gates every reason, so `tumwater wake --role qa` and a queued per-role prompt's auto-wake silently do nothing for the rest of the interval the loop ticked inside (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25)

**Symptom:** `tumwater wake --role qa` (or `tumwater prompt --role qa "…"`, whose enqueue auto-wakes the role) right after qa ticked: the command reports "wake requested for qa — applies it", but the loop keeps sleeping for up to its whole `minTickIntervalSeconds` (qa 2 h, steward 6 h) — no tick, no feedback, the queued prompt sitting behind a `p:1` marker. `cmdWake`'s own contract says "the named roles tick within one poll instead of sleeping until their backoff expires".

**Reproduce:** Unit-level: a qa runner with `lastTickEndedAt = now - 5 min`, `nextRunAt = now + 2 h`, `backoffSeconds = 0`; apply `clearBackoff(state, now)` (exactly what `requestWake`/`runner.wake()` do) and `isEligible` still returns `{ run: false }` — the `sinceLast < minGap` check fires before `nextRunAt` is ever consulted.

**Cause:** `wake` was built as "skip backoff" and the min-gap check was never given an exception for it: after any tick, `lastTickEndedAt` is fresh, so the gap check rejects the woken loop regardless of the pulled-forward `nextRunAt`. The director's inbox is explicitly gap-exempt ("a queued prompt runs as soon as the previous one finishes"), and a per-role prompt is the same class of user request — the asymmetry was an oversight, not a throttle (a wake is one explicit demand, not a cadence change).

**Fix:** `clearBackoff` stamps `wokenAt: now` (LoopState, persisted), and `isEligible` exempts the min gap when the wake is newer than the last tick's end. Self-clearing: the woken tick's own end re-stamps `lastTickEndedAt` past `wokenAt`, so the ordinary gap re-arms without a separate erase and a stale wake cannot exempt later windows. (Superseded in part the same day: a queued per-role prompt no longer relies on its auto-wake at all — the queue itself makes the loop due, see the 2026-09-25 wake-clobber entry above.)

**Validation gap:** no-fake — confirming it needed a slow-clock wake fixture that did not exist: the existing e2e wake test seeded `lastTickEndedAt` an hour in the past precisely to dodge the min-gap gate (its own comment says so), and fastConfig zeroes the interval, so the suite never exercised wake against a live gap.

- A once round exits without running a role enabled mid-round: the live reload appends its runner to the array OnceRound watches, but the round's tick snapshot was fixed at construction, so the role's persisted history reads as already settled (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit fe6fbd42; gap: no-fake)
- The GUI's next-run cell counts down for a loop parked awaiting a slot: the `fmtNextRun` mirror of the TUI's `nextRunCell` lacks the parked-waiter case, so the two dashboards disagree (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 60df614f; gap: unclear-invariant)
- A `Retry-After: 0` hint sneaks the zero-wait 429 bug back in: a hint that asks for no wait is kept as a hint, so the retry fires instantly into the exhausted bucket (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit a4174554; gap: unclear-invariant)
- The budget fallback has no liveness check: an unreachable free model turns the spend cap into an hour of 100% tick failure instead of a pause (found by log analysis 2026-09-20, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 026f6a3f)
- The build check's 300 s timeout does not bound it: skipped checks recorded 331–1158 s (found by human investigation 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-24; commit d9dd4d2e; gap: no-observability)
- The grandchild-leak fix landed its kill but not its detector: `tumwater doctor` still reports nothing about orphaned worktree processes (found by human investigation 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 18b61398; gap: no-fake)
- The gate's "bounded" build-fix run has no time or resource budget: dry's run load-tested the live host for 90 minutes, stalling the whole fleet, then `pkill`ed every test run on the machine (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 47c06df2; gap: no-observability)
- A self-redeploy whose new generation fails a startup precondition takes the whole fleet down with no event: 4 h 44 m of silence after the 2026-09-22 22:42 restart (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 3b40b224; gap: no-observability)
- A 429 storm still has no fleet-wide hold: each tick now retries its own 429, but concurrent loops keep hammering a rate-limited provider instead of backing off together (found by human investigation 2026-09-21; spun out of the fixed 429-retry entry 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 1e24e197; gap: no-fake)
- A pi run that exits normally leaves its backgrounded tool-call processes running: qa's GUI check left an unauthenticated `gui --all-interfaces` listening on the LAN for 7.5 hours (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 3ff19995; gap: no-observability)
- The batch's one-at-a-time fallback re-reviews every change it already approved: the lander rebases before the gate, so the approved-head short-circuit never hits (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 45acd18b; gap: unclear-invariant)
- The status table's "last result" cell shows `queued` — the tick's live landing status, not the last completed result (reported by user 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 55b73143)
- The restart drain's `hold` does not stop ticks already parked in the semaphore: they start fresh pi runs mid-drain and are the ticks the restart then aborts (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 679e0f46)
- A live `tumwater.json` that disappears mid-run hot-reloads as built-in defaults: plan 4b's own landing deleted the config and the fleet ran 8.6 hours with zero successful ticks (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 9692bb68; gap: no-observability)
- Reviewers re-run the full suite in scratch copies under /tmp even when the prompt says the harness check already passed (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24; commit 07d205ee; gap: no-observability)
### The reviewer is never told about the gate's own build-fix commit, so a successful fix reads as an unclaimed change and the landing is rejected (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24)

**Symptom:** On 2026-09-23 dry's build-fix run (the build-fix budget entry, also under Fixed) committed `b020f67` "tumwater(dry): fix failing build check" (src/pi.ts, +22 −4) at 05:40. The re-check passed, and the gate went on to the model reviewer. The reviewer rejected it at 05:43:07: "The branch's diff includes an unclaimed production change (commit `b020f67`, `src/pi.ts` …)". Nearly two hours of fix run, and a gate re-check that passed, were thrown away by a reviewer doing exactly what its prompt asks: flag changes the author's summary and body do not claim.

**Repro:** Deterministic with a scripted reviewer: a pin whose gate check fails, a fake fix run that edits a src/ file and turns the check green, and a reviewer prompt capture. The prompt from `buildReviewPrompt` (src/prompt.ts:379) carries the author's summary and commit body plus `verifiedByHarness` (src/review.ts:284). Nothing in it says the diff (`aheadOfMainDiff`, src/review.ts:307) now includes a harness-authored commit, or which files that commit touched.

**Expected:** When the gate added a build-fix commit, tell the reviewer: name the commit, its files, and the failure it fixed, and ask for it to be judged as a fix to that failure, not as scope creep by the author. A fix that goes beyond the failure can still be rejected on its merits.

**Suspected cause:** 4803c07 added the fix run and carried its spend (`fixRun`) and its verified head (`verifiedHead`) through the gate, but never passed the fix commit itself into the review prompt. The prompt predates the fix run.

**Fix:** The gate now names its own fix to the reviewer. When the build-fix run changed the tree and the re-check did not stay red, `reviewAheadOfMain` (src/review.ts) records a `BuildFixCommit` through the new `buildFixCommit` helper. That record holds every commit from the author's head to the fixed head (the whole range, so a commit the fix run made itself despite its prompt is named too), the files those commits touched, the failure the fix run was handed (the headline plus the clipped tail, already bounded by clipBuildTail to ten lines of 300 chars), and whether the re-check passed. `buildReviewPrompt` (src/gate-prompts.ts) takes it as a new optional last parameter and renders one self-contained block after the author's commit body: the sha(s), the files, and the failure. The block says the author's summary and body could not claim the commit, so it is judged as the harness's fix to that failure and not as unclaimed scope. It can still be rejected if it is wrong, if it games the check (a deleted or skipped test, a weakened assertion), or if it changes more than the failure requires. A skipped re-check is stated as unverified, never as a pass. No fix commit means no block: a first-time green pre-check, a no-change fix run, or a still-red reject never reaches it. Pins: test/review.test.ts drives the entry's Repro end to end. The check is red until `src/fixed.ts` exists, a fake fix run writes it, and the scripted reviewer captures its prompt. The test asserts that the prompt names the fix commit's sha, lists exactly `src/fixed.ts` (never the author's file), and carries the failure headline; it failed before the fix. The existing green pre-check prompt test now also asserts that the block is absent. test/prompt.test.ts pins the block's wording and placement, the plural and skipped-re-check forms, absence without a fix, and "VERDICT:" still exactly twice. `npm test review.test prompt.test` 158/158 and `npm test lander.test build-check.test fix-claim.test` 71/71 passing; `tsc --noEmit` clean. Not covered: a later gate invocation that re-reviews an already-fixed tree (an under-cap review failure or an abort keeps the fix commit on the pin) runs no fix run, so it carries no block. Covering it needs the fix commits found from branch history and the failure persisted in the commit message.

**Validation gap:** none (closest fit) — the suite's existing pieces (the fake pi that tells fix and review runs apart by session name, and the argv prompt capture) reproduced it deterministically once combined in one test; what was missing was a test of what the reviewer is told after a fix run, not any fake or trace.

### Loops are never told the date, so the fleet stamps BUGS.md and PLANS.md with made-up dates (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24)

**Symptom:** On 2026-09-23 the fleet landed headings dated in the future: "found by clean 2026-09-26" (the `tumwater init <prompt>` entry in Open), "reported by user 2026-09-24" (the status-table `queued` entry and the maxConcurrent permit entry), "found by telemetry loop 2026-09-24" (the self-redeploy shutdown entry), and "re-opened by telemetry loop 2026-09-24" / "fixed 2026-09-24" in successive drafts of the refusal entry. About 50 lines added to BUGS.md and PLANS.md in the three days to 2026-09-23 carry dates of 2026-09-24 or later. `83f73cf` ("correct its 2026-09-26 dates") fixed one set by hand, and nothing stops the next set. The dates are what humans and the steward use to order events. They also go into `**Needs review <YYYY-MM-DD> …**` markers and plan deadlines, so a wrong date quietly corrupts the audit trail.

**Repro:** `grep -n 'new Date\|toISOString\|today' src/prompt.ts src/gate-prompts.ts` returns nothing that puts a date into a prompt. Any tick prompt from `tumwater logs --role <r> --prompt` carries no date. `grep -nE '2026-09-2[4-9]' BUGS.md PLANS.md` on the 2026-09-23 tree lists the future-dated lines.

**Expected:** Every tick, reviewer, gate build-fix and director prompt states today's local date (YYYY-MM-DD), which the harness already computes for `dayStamp`. Roles that write dated records are told to use it rather than inferring one.

**Suspected cause:** The prompts ask for dates in several places (`NEEDS_REVIEW_NOTE`'s `<YYYY-MM-DD>` in src/roles.ts:66, the "(found by … YYYY-MM-DD)" heading convention, Fixed-entry dates) but never supply one. The model fills them in from the newest dates it finds in the repo, which are themselves drifting, so the error compounds.

**Fix:** Every pi prompt now states the local day, from one helper: `dateLine` (src/prompt.ts) renders `Today's date is YYYY-MM-DD (local time).` from `todayStamp` (src/budget.ts) — the daily cost budget window's own local-day computation, so no second date formatter exists. The tick and director prompts carry it in `sharedPreamble`, right after the worktree sentence, followed by the one sentence that tells every role to stamp it on anything it records now (a new BUGS.md/PLANS.md heading's "(found by … YYYY-MM-DD)", a Fixed or Done date, a Refused or Needs-review note) and count plan deadlines from it, never inferring the date from the repo — so no role text was edited. The gate's conflict, build-fix and review prompts (src/gate-prompts.ts) carry the date line once, right after their opening paragraph. Each builder takes an optional trailing `today` (a `today` field on the tick input) that falls through to `todayStamp()`, so the call sites in src/loop.ts, src/review.ts and src/landing-merge.ts are unchanged and tests pin an exact date. test/prompt.test.ts pins the given date exactly once in all five prompts, the dated-records sentence in tick and director, the review prompt's two `VERDICT:` forms, and the default as `todayStamp()`'s day; the new tests do not compile against the pre-fix builders. `npm test prompt review merge loop` 283/283 passing. The already-future-dated headings of other entries are left for their own fixers.

**Validation gap:** none — once suspected, one grep of the prompt builders confirmed no date reached any prompt and a pure-function test reproduces it; what hid it was that a made-up date looks as valid as a real one, so nothing flagged the headings until a human read them against the calendar.

### `tumwater init <prompt>` in a repo whose brief already lives in TUMWATER.md silently drops the new prompt (found by clean 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24)

**Symptom:** A repo whose project brief is TUMWATER.md (plans/portability.md §7a/7) re-runs
`tumwater init "new prompt"`. init's guard (src/init.ts:147) only refuses when `readInitialPrompt`
finds nothing — TUMWATER.md carries the markers, so it passes — and the write at src/init.ts:175
calls `write("README.md", readmeTemplate(...))`, creating a README that carries the new prompt.
But `readInitialPrompt` resolves TUMWATER.md first (briefCandidates order), so every tick keeps
running with the OLD prompt; the new one lands in a file nobody reads.

**Reproduce:** Adopt a repo with TUMWATER.md owning the brief (copy README.md's managed sections
into TUMWATER.md, delete the README block), run `tumwater init "a different prompt"`, then
`tumwater status --json` / read a tick prefill — the original prompt is still what the fleet runs.

**Suspected cause:** init.ts still hardcodes README.md as the write target and only consults
readInitialPrompt for its refusal guard, without checking `briefFile(root)` — a brief file that
already owns the sections should either be rewritten in place or the new prompt should be refused
with a name-the-file error.

**Fix:** init now resolves the brief's owner (`briefFile(root)`) before any side effect and acts on that file rather than a hardcoded README.md (src/init.ts). A prompt given against a brief that already carries one must match it: init never rewrites an existing brief (create-if-absent, like every file it seeds), so a different prompt is refused with an error naming the owning file — `TUMWATER.md already carries a different initial prompt … edit it in TUMWATER.md between <markers>; to re-seed with the current one, re-run a bare \`tumwater init\`` — and nothing is created, git-inited, or committed. The README.md write runs only when no brief exists yet (the fresh repo), so a TUMWATER.md-owned repo never gains a README.md duplicating the managed prompt + status sections, on a matching-prompt re-run or a bare re-seed alike. The README-owned case had the same silent drop (a different prompt was accepted and ignored — the old `initProject accepts an existing README that already carries the prompt` test passed one), so it gets the same refusal for consistency; the same-prompt and bare re-runs stay idempotent. docs/how-it-works.md's Setup paragraph states the refusal. Pins in test/init.test.ts: the TUMWATER.md-owned refusal (no README.md, no backlog files, clean tree, old prompt still read), the TUMWATER.md-owned matching and bare inits creating no README.md, the README-owned refusal naming README.md, and the README-owned acceptance now passing the brief's own prompt; the fresh-repo test is unchanged. The three new tests fail on the unfixed code and pass after; test/init.test.ts 26/26, and the cli*, readme and doctor test files 122/122.

**Validation gap:** none — the existing init fixtures (`makeRepo` plus a hand-written TUMWATER.md) reproduced the drop deterministically offline; the gap was only that no test combined a TUMWATER.md-owned brief with a different prompt and a missing README.md.

### The `review_verdict` event records the reviewer's preamble instead of a finding: `**1.**`-style numbering is not recognized as a list (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-24)

**Symptom:** Since 2026-09-22, 11 approvals were logged with a preamble as their `reason`, for example "I checked the diff against the code, its callers, and its tests. Findings:" (bugfix, 2026-09-23 23:46), "All checks complete. Here is what I verified:" (organize, 20:48), and "## Review" (clean, 22:06). The 23:46 reply numbered its findings `**1. Scope matches the claim.**`, `**2. …**`, and none of that reached the event log or the feed.

**Repro:** `parseVerdict` on a reply that opens with a one-line preamble and then bold-numbered paragraphs (`**1. X.** …`), ending `VERDICT: approve`. `reasons[0]` is the preamble.

**Expected:** Recognize bold or heading-wrapped numbering (`**1.`, `**1)`, `### 1.`) as list items, and skip trailing-colon preambles and bare headings in the prose fallback, so `reasons[0]` is the first real finding. The same list feeds `review_rejected` and the author's rejection note, so rejections get the fix too.

**Suspected cause:** `reasonsFrom` (src/review.ts:65) matches only `^(?:\d+[.)]|[-*])\s+`. A leading `**` fails the match, the reply falls back to "every non-empty line", and `review_verdict` logs `verdict.reasons[0]` (src/review.ts:389).

**Fix:** `reasonsFrom` (src/review.ts) now reads list items through a `LIST_ITEM` pattern that also accepts a heading-wrapped marker (`### 1. X`) and a bold opener around the number (`**1.** X`, `**1) X**`, `**1. X.** body`), and a new `listItemText` strips the list's own markup — marker, heading hashes, and the bold pair around the marker, wherever its closer falls — so `**1. Scope matches the claim.** The diff…` records `Scope matches the claim. The diff…` with no dangling `**`; emphasis inside an item (`1. **X** body`) is the reviewer's own and is kept. The prose fallback now skips lead-ins and headings (`isPreamble`: an ATX heading or a line ending in `:`/`:**`), and a region of nothing but those still yields them rather than no reason at all. `parseVerdict` and every consumer are unchanged: `review_verdict`'s `reason`, `review_rejected`'s list and the feed line that renders its first entry, the author's rejection note (which used to double-number, `2. **1. X.** …`), the gate's `detail`, and the digest's `reasons[0]` clustering all read the same list, so rejections get the fix too. test/review.test.ts pins it with a table of the real reply shapes quoted above (bugfix 23:46, organize 20:48, clean 22:06, the bold-lead-alone-on-its-line organize shape, `**1.**`, `**1)`, `### 1.`, colon and heading prose), plus preservation rows for `1. **X**` and a lead-in-only reply; a rejection-note test; and a gate test asserting the logged `review_verdict` reason is the first finding. 11 of the 13 new tests fail on the old parser (the two preservation rows pass on both). Re-parsing the 413 archived reviewer replies under `.tumwater/sessions/_review` turns all 12 preamble-, heading- or bold-number-shaped first reasons into the reply's first finding. Ran `npm test review` 56/56 and `npm test prompt event-format failure` 158/158.

**Validation gap:** none (closest fit) — a one-line `parseVerdict` call on the logged reply shape reproduced it; the suite missed it only because every fixture used the bare `1.` numbering the review prompt advertises, never the bold numbering reviewers actually write.

### The failure digest's `## Outcome by role` separator row has no cell delimiters, so the table never renders (found by human investigation 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-24)

**Symptom:** `tumwater report --failures` emits `| --- | ---: ---: ---: ---: ---: ---: |` as the separator under a header with N+1 cells. Every markdown renderer needs the separator's cell count to match the header's; with two cells against seven the block is not a table at all and falls through as a paragraph of pipes and dashes. The section is the digest's primary per-role summary, and it is the one table in the document that is broken — `## Deltas vs the preceding N days`, two lines below, hardcodes its separator correctly and renders fine.

**Repro:** `node dist/src/cli.js report --failures --days 2` and read the fourth line of `## Outcome by role`; or `renderFailureMarkdown(collectFailureReport(root, 2))` in a scratch root with any two roles' `tick_end` events.

**Expected:** `src/failure-report.ts:474` builds the row as `` `| --- |${cols.map(() => " ---:").join("")} |` ``; the join needs the delimiter — `join(" |")` — to produce `| --- | ---: | ---: | … |`. A test asserting the separator's pipe count equals the header's would pin it.

**Suspected cause:** The row was written by analogy with the header line one line above, which interpolates `cols.join(" | ")` into a template that supplies the outer pipes. Copying that shape with a `.map()` in front drops the inner delimiter, and the digest is read almost exclusively as plain text in a terminal or in an event feed, where a broken table looks nearly identical to a working one.

**Fix:** The separator is now joined exactly like the header one line above it: `renderFailureMarkdown` (src/failure-report.ts; the cited line 474 had drifted to 126) builds it as `` `| --- | ${cols.map(() => "---:").join(" | ")} |` ``, so it emits `| --- | ---: | ---: | … |` with one right-aligned delimiter per result column. A comment beside it explains why the two rows must share their join. The renderer's only other table, `## Deltas`, hardcodes a five-cell separator under its five-cell header and was already correct. A new test in test/failure-report.test.ts, "every digest table's separator has one delimiter cell per header cell", parses every table in a rendered digest by splitting its rows on `|`. It runs three cases: one role with one result, one role with several results, and several roles with several results. In each it checks that both tables are found, that the separator has as many cells as the header, that every separator cell is a valid delimiter, that every body row is as wide as the header, and that the outcome separator is exactly `---` followed by one `---:` per result. The test failed before the fix (`one role, several results`: 2 separator cells against 4 header cells). The one-column case passed even then, because a single delimiter needs no join. After the fix it passes: failure-report 23/23, and tui plus gui-report (which render the digest) 50/50.

**Validation gap:** none — one offline render reproduced the bug deterministically; the only gap was that no test counted a table's cells, and a broken separator looks almost the same as a working one in the plain text where the digest is read, a gap the new test closes.

### A FLOW line without a parseable verdict is recorded as a pass: `extractFlow` defaults a malformed or truncated result to `passed`, latching a check qa never finished (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** The window's qa warning cluster — `pi finished without changes and without declaring nothing-to-do` (1×, 2026-09-23 digest) — is a reply that declared neither the nothing-to-do sentinel nor, necessarily, a finished flow verdict. The harness's response raises the right alarm but can still latch a false pass: `extractFlow` (src/reply-contract.ts:52, introduced by `492cbeb`) matches the `FLOW:` value against `/^(.*)\s*[—-]\s*(passed|bug)\s*$/i` and, when the verdict suffix is missing or cut off — `FLOW: gui-pause-resume` with no verdict at all, or a reply truncated mid-word at `FLOW: gui-budget-cap — pa` — falls through to `return { flow: value, result: "passed" }`. The no-sentinel path (src/loop.ts:647) then records that pass into the coverage ledger and advances the rotation past a check that did not complete, contradicting the very warning the same tick emitted. The `bdccac8` guard (fixed 2026-09-19, "Skip flow-coverage recording on a qa tick cut off before its verdict") only covers `cutOff`, which requires a *contentless* final message (src/no-change.ts:33) — the common truncation shape is `stopReason=length` with partial text still present, which is not `cutOff`, and a merely non-compliant reply is not either. The success path (src/loop.ts:746) has no guard at all.

**Repro:** Deterministic, no model needed: `extractFlow("FLOW: some-flow")` returns `{ flow: "some-flow", result: "passed" }`; and a fake-pi qa shim whose final message is prose ending in a verdict-less `FLOW: some-flow` line (so `finalMessageContentless` is false) ends its tick `no_change` with the no-sentinel warning *and* a ledger row recording `some-flow: passed`. Correlated: cluster key `pi finished without changes and without declaring nothing-to-do` (role qa) in the 2026-09-23 digest; commits `492cbeb` (introduced the default) and `bdccac8` (the narrow `cutOff` guard that missed it).

**Expected:** A `FLOW:` line without a parseable verdict is not a result. `extractFlow` should return null (or an outcome the caller declines to record) when the `passed|bug` suffix is absent, so a tick the harness itself warns about never advances the rotation on a verdict the run never declared. The 2026-09-19 Fixed entry below records the same latch for the contentless case; this is the parse-level remainder.

**Suspected cause:** The default-to-passed fallback was written when the only producer was a compliant reply and leniency cost nothing; the later truncation fix patched the class through the caller's `cutOff` flag instead of at the parse, leaving every other shape — textual truncation, omitted suffix, plain non-compliance — still latching `passed`.

**Fix:** The verdict is required at the parse, where the bug lived: `extractFlow` returns null when the `FLOW:` value carries no `passed|bug` suffix — a bare `FLOW: <name>`, or a reply truncated mid-verdict (`FLOW: gui-budget-cap — pa`) — so both record paths (no_change and the success path) decline to record and the rotation never advances on an undeclared verdict. The producer was fixed with it: the qa prompt (src/roles.ts, the sole FLOW producer) no longer says a bare `FLOW: <name>` counts as passed — it states the verdict is required and that a bare line is not recorded — and the test that pinned the leniency was flipped both ways: test/reply-contract.test.ts now asserts null for the bare and truncated forms, and test/prompt.test.ts forbids "counts as passed" while requiring the required-verdict sentence. Full suite 1445/1445 passing.

**Validation gap:** no-observability (closest fit) — the failure left traces (a ledger row recording a pass beside the tick's own no-sentinel warning), but nothing distinguished a latched pass from a real one, and the suite's only test on the parse path pinned the default-to-passed as correct, so neither the digest nor the tests could confirm the bug until the parse contract was re-specified.

### A build check killed by a signal is reported as "timed out after 300s", and at gate scope the change then goes to review unverified (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** On 2026-09-23 at 05:37:56 organize's gate logged `build_check scope:gate status:skipped durationMs:7388` with the warning `build check timed out after 300s; proceeding to model review`. The check had run for 7.4 s. It was killed by the build-fix run's `pkill -f test-runner` at 05:37:51–57 (see the no-budget entry's history), not by the timeout. Organize's change was then reviewed and landed with no verified check. The warning names a cause that did not happen and hides a check that was killed from outside.

**Repro:** Deterministic, no model needed: a check script that SIGKILLs itself (`scripts: { test: "kill -9 $$" }`) — npm re-raises a script child's signal death, so the group leader itself closes with the signal — well inside the timeout. Pre-fix `runBuildCheck` returned `{ status: "skipped", skipReason: "timeout" }` (the `if (r.timedOut || r.signal)` fold), the warning claimed the 300 s timeout fired, and at gate scope the change went on to model review.

**Expected:** Keep "timed out" and "killed by a signal" apart: a distinct `skipReason` whose warning names the signal and the real duration. A check the harness did not stop itself says nothing about the tree, so retry it once before proceeding unverified.

**Suspected cause:** The old comment ("The timeout fired (or the tree died on a signal)") folds the two together on purpose, assuming the only signal a check ever receives is the harness's own timeout kill. A tool call from another pi run on the same host breaks that assumption.

**Fix:** The two classifications are separate again: `runBuildCheck` returns `skipReason: "killed"` with `killedBy: <signal>` when a check closes on a signal it did not send (its own timeout resolves with `timedOut` already set, so any signal reaching that line is external — and `runScriptGroup` prices the timeout attempt, not the external kill, as the timed-out path). `buildCheckSkipWarning` has a killed branch naming the signal and the check's real wall-clock (`build check was killed by SIGKILL after 7.4s; …`), with a signal-less fallback for callers without the info (main-red.ts's baseline warning), so no skip warning ever again names a timeout that did not fire. `runScopedBuildCheck` retries a killed check ONCE at any scope — the first run's death is another run's doing, so one verdict from a clean attempt is owed — pricing each attempt as its own `build_check` event, and treats a persistent killed skip at a merge scope (landing/batch) exactly like a timeout: a deterministic reject whose reason names the signal and the real duration. Pins in test/build-check.test.ts: the killed classification (runBuildCheck), the gate retry whose clean verdict stands with both attempts priced, and the merge-scope killed reject whose warning never says "timed out". Full suite 1415/1415 passing.

**Validation gap:** no-observability (closest fit) — the failure left a trace, but a false one: the skip warning named a timeout that never fired, so neither the feed nor the suite's existing pins could distinguish an external kill from a real timeout until a regression test classified the two apart; the bug itself was confirmed only by human log analysis of the raw gate sessions.

### Any reply that merely mentions `TUMWATER_REFUSED` is treated as a refusal and its code is hard-reset: `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none` discarded two finished, tested bugfix ticks (found by human log analysis 2026-09-23; re-opened by telemetry loop 2026-09-24, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** Four commits on 2026-09-22 are refusal notes whose "reason" is a negation: `9cea8c3` (`bugfix: refuse — none`, 01:17), `e420415` (`bugfix: refuse — (none — no entry refused this run)`, 02:49), `cd0f477` (`telemetry: refuse — (none — bug filed normally)`, 03:19) and `4037522` (`steward: refuse — none`, 07:03). None of these runs refused anything: each ended a normal SUMMARY/WHY/RISK/VERIFIED reply with a trailing `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none` line, treating the sentinel as one more report field. The two bugfix runs had done real, verified work that the harness then destroyed:
- 01:17 (session `.tumwater/sessions/bugfix/2026-09-22T07-57-23-814Z_*.jsonl`): edited src/build-check.ts, src/pi.ts and test/build-check.test.ts — the build-check tree-kill fix — and reported `npm test` 1275/1275 passing. Only its BUGS.md move-to-Fixed landed.
- 02:49 (session `…/2026-09-22T09-42-15-778Z_*.jsonl`): edited src/lander.ts, src/landing-slot.ts, src/orchestrator.ts, src/review.ts, src/ui/progress.ts, src/ui/status-model.ts and three test files — the user-reported landing-cell fix — reporting 1292/1292 passing. Only BUGS.md/README.md landed; that bug was re-opened (its own Open entry) because its Fixed record described code that did not exist.
The steward later found the first phantom record and diagnosed it as a tick that *fabricated* a Fix paragraph (the "fix narrative can land on main without its fix" entry under Fixed); the code was in fact written, tested, and discarded by the harness. The `falseFixReason` guard added for that entry now blocks the orphaned note, but the code is still thrown away.

**Repro:** Deterministic, no model needed: a fake-pi shim whose final assistant message makes an ordinary src/ edit and ends `SUMMARY: x` / `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none`. The tick ends `result: "refused"` with summary `none`, the src edit is gone from the worktree, and only markdown (if any) is committed. On the live log: `git log --grep='refuse — ' --format='%h %s' main`.

**Expected:** A refusal is a deliberate, affirmative declaration, and treating it as one must not depend on the model never naming the sentinel. Detect it only from an anchored `TUMWATER_REFUSED: <reason>` line in the FINAL assistant message, and treat reasons that negate the refusal (`none`, `n/a`, `(none …)`, empty) as no refusal. A reply that carries both a SUMMARY and a refusal line with real work in the diff is contradictory and should be surfaced (warning event), not resolved by destroying the work. The prompt should say explicitly that the line is emitted only when refusing.

**Suspected cause:** `PiStream` sets `refused = true` on `text.includes(REFUSED_SENTINEL)` for any assistant message (src/pi-stream.ts:225) — deliberately looser than the anchored `extractRefusal`, by analogy with the nothing-to-do sentinel, where a false positive costs only a no_change. Here a false positive routes the tick through `handleRefusal` (src/refusal.ts:34, from src/loop.ts:609), which commits only `.md` files and discards everything else via reset --hard/clean. `extractRefusal` then accepts `none` as a valid reason. The prompt (src/prompt.ts:110) lists the refusal line right next to the reply-contract fields, which invites a model to fill it in on every reply. The pi sessions above still contain both runs' edit tool calls and could be replayed to recover the lost diffs until `sessionRetentionDays` prunes them (~2026-09-29).

**Carried to Fixed by phantom markdown five times; the code lands with this record (2026-09-23):** the four 2026-09-22 commits plus `cdcc54c` each recorded this fix under ## Fixed while landing markdown only — `git log -S isNegatedRefusal` named no code commit until now. This move to Fixed is backed by the code in the same change: `isNegatedRefusal` (src/reply-contract.ts), the anchored-only parser (src/pi-stream.ts), `refusalContradiction` (src/refusal.ts), the loop wiring (src/loop.ts), the prompt wording (src/prompt.ts), and both `falseFixReason` holes (src/fix-claim.ts), with pins in test/reply-contract.test.ts, test/pi.test.ts, test/refusal.test.ts, test/loop-2.test.ts, and test/fix-claim.test.ts; full suite 1405/1405 passing.

**Fix:** The refusal comes only from an anchored `TUMWATER_REFUSED: <reason>` line in an assistant message (extractRefusal, src/reply-contract.ts): src/pi-stream.ts no longer substring-scans the sentinel — a bare sentinel and a mid-sentence mention declare nothing, a negating reason (isNegatedRefusal: empty, `none`, `n/a`, or a parenthesized form of either, with or without an appended explanation) clears an earlier declaration, and the last anchored line wins in both directions while a message without an anchored line cannot erase one (the intermediate-turn regression pin keeps). A refusal contradicted by its own reply — refusalContradiction (src/refusal.ts) finds a SUMMARY beside non-markdown work in the worktree — is surfaced behind a warning naming the kept work, and src/loop.ts runs the normal flow (the review gate judges it) instead of handleRefusal's reset --hard/clean; handleRefusal keeps the note-only discard for a genuine objection. The prompt (src/prompt.ts) states the line is emitted ONLY when refusing, not as a reply-contract field. The review gate's falseFixReason (src/fix-claim.ts) had two holes that let this record carry as phantom markdown after the first re-open: an already-Fixed heading was skipped unconditionally via its baseFixed set (now skipped only when the entry's body is untouched, so an in-place narrative rewrite faces the same symbol check a new entry does), and the comparison base was main's tip (now the diff's own merge-base, so a stacked batch's Fixed→Open predecessor is what an Open→Fixed restoration is measured against). Pins: test/pi.test.ts (the negated shapes, the bare sentinel, the mid-sentence mention, last-line-wins in both directions), test/reply-contract.test.ts (isNegatedRefusal), test/refusal.test.ts (refusalContradiction), test/loop-2.test.ts (the three end-to-end regressions over a src edit), and test/fix-claim.test.ts (both holes, pure and at the gate).

**Validation gap:** no-observability — the suite pinned the loose substring scan as intended behavior and the destruction left no automated trace, so nothing flagged finished work being discarded until a human correlated refusal commits against pi sessions containing the edit tool calls (and the gate's fabricated-fix check had no test over an already-Fixed rewrite or a stacked comparison base, which is how the phantom record carried five times).
