### The active-state rows an operator counts against `maxConcurrent` do not track the real permit holders: a tick parked in the semaphore queue renders as `working`, so a landing that holds a permit looks like it runs outside the cap (reported by user 2026-09-24, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** Watching a live fleet, the number of loops in active states (working/reviewing/landing) visibly exceeds `maxConcurrent`, and a `landing <elapsed>` row coexists with a full complement of `working` rows — so the landing appears not to count as active for the cap on parallel loops, when the user's expectation (and the documented contract) is that it should. The harness's real accounting says otherwise: since 2eee180 (2026-09-18) the landing slot's whole body — reviewer run, build check, conflict resolution — holds one `maxConcurrent` permit (`withLandingSlot`, src/orchestrator.ts:184, tier −1), and the display sorts landing as active (`isActivePhase`, src/ui/status-model.ts:181). The visible discrepancy comes from the display, not the permit: `runner.state.running` is set to true BEFORE the semaphore wait (src/orchestrator.ts:749, "Reserve before the semaphore wait so we don't double-schedule"), and `loopPhase` renders every `running` loop as `working <elapsed>` (src/ui/status-model.ts:122) with no distinction between a tick that holds a permit and one parked in the semaphore queue. With the default cap of 6 (src/config.ts:36) and a dozen roles due in one poll, several parked waiters display as `working` beside the permitted ticks — and the landing, which holds a real permit and genuinely limits the fleet, rows up next to them, making the cap look unenforced and the landing look free. The same flag serves two meanings: "this loop has business in flight" (what `isEligible`'s `if (s.running) return { run: false }` needs so a parked waiter is not double-scheduled) and "this loop's pi is running right now" (what the dashboards render as an active state).

**Repro:** Live fleet, no model needed. Set `maxConcurrent: 2` with several roles enabled at short intervals and keep one role authoring changes so a landing runs. During the landing, `tumwater status` shows the landing row plus two or more `working` rows — more active states than the cap — while the backend actually serves at most 2 streams. Correlated history: the permit invariant itself is pinned only in the e2e tier since the split (test/orchestrator.e2e.test.ts:741, "a landing's reviewer run takes the same maxConcurrent permit as a role tick"); the gating suite has no pin, and no test renders a fleet whose active-state rows exceed `maxConcurrent`, so nothing reddened while the display drifted from the limit it mirrors.

**Expected:** The operator-visible active set must equal the real permit holders, so a landing visibly counts toward the cap. A tick parked in the semaphore queue holds no permit and runs no pi: it should display a distinct label (e.g. `awaiting slot <elapsed>` — its true state) instead of `working <elapsed>`, so the active rows — permitted working ticks, reviewing ticks, and the in-flight landing — add up to at most `maxConcurrent` (plus the director, whose documented bypass is a state of its own). Keep the landing's permit behavior unchanged; if the fix touches the semaphore seam, add a gating-suite pin for the landing-holds-a-permit invariant so it stops living only in the e2e tier.

**Suspected cause:** The merge-queue work (3/5) split the tick — permit held from start to end, label `working` — from the landing — permit held by the orchestrator's slot, label `landing` — but nothing revisited the display contract that maps loop states onto the concurrency limit. `running` was never split into "scheduled/parked" and "permit held", so the parked-waiter reservation made before the semaphore wait (needed against double-scheduling) leaked into the active-state rendering, and the user-visible count stopped tracking the limit it is supposed to mirror.

**Context added 2026-09-23:** the user's report was enqueued at 2026-09-22 14:13:16, eight minutes after the live `tumwater.json` was deleted and the fleet hot-reloaded defaults (`max_concurrent_changed 3 → 6` at 14:05:33; see the missing-config entry in Open). With the cap silently doubled, up to 7 ticks were really in flight against a configured cap of 3, which by itself produces the observation "more active loops than maxConcurrent". The display conflation described here is real, but re-check the symptom against a fleet whose config is intact before sizing the fix.

**Fix:** A third state, not a fork of `running`: `LoopState.parkedSince` (transient, never persisted) records when the orchestrator reserved a loop whose tick still waits in the semaphore queue; the wrapped acquire closure clears it the moment the permit is granted (defensively cleared in `applyTickOutcome` and at orchestrator start too), and the director — which never queues — never gets it. `loopPhase` renders a parked waiter as `awaiting slot <elapsed>` instead of `working`, so it falls outside `isActivePhase` on both dashboards (the GUI's browser copy of the sort rule already excluded it by prefix) and the active rows count exactly the permit holders — working ticks, reviewing ticks, and the in-flight landing. The landing's permit behavior is untouched, and the stale-live-detail paths (`status-payload`'s live read, `displayTokenMetrics`, `stateCell`) now treat a parked waiter as idle so it cannot show the previous tick's tokens or work item. Pins: the landing-holds-a-permit invariant gains its gating-suite pin (test/orchestrator-seams.test.ts, "a parked waiter holds no permit until acquire grants it, which the landing tier shares" — real Semaphore, capacity 1, waiter and LANDING_TIER both queue and clear their markers exactly at grant); test/status.test.ts pins the label (`awaiting slot`, sorted behind the active rows) and a rendered three-loop fleet (one permit holder, two waiters) that shows two `awaiting slot` rows beside exactly one `working` row.

**Validation gap:** none — the mislabel reproduced deterministically once the state shape existed (a `running` loop with `parkedSince` rendered as `working` in the pre-fix build), and the suite's gap was only that no test had ever rendered a fleet whose active rows exceeded the cap, which the new pins close.

### The digest's Review rejections section silently truncates at 5 alphabetically-first clusters: 7 of the window's 13 rejections are invisible while the Deltas table reports them (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** The 2026-09-22 digest's `## Deltas vs the preceding 1 day` table reports **13** `review_rejected` events in the window (bugfix 2, feature 2, coverage 3, dry 5, qa 1), but the same document's `## Review rejections` section itemizes only **5 clusters / 6 occurrences** with no truncation marker — 7 of 13, more than half the window's rejections, including every feature and qa rejection. The error/warning sections at least title themselves "Top … clusters"; this one read as an itemization of the window. The tie-break made it worse: `clusterMessages` sorts `b.count - a.count || a.key.localeCompare(b.key)` and rejection keys are prefixed `role\u0000`, so among equal-count clusters the survivors were simply the alphabetically-first roles. A secondary divergence path: the section filtered on `Array.isArray(ev.reasons)` while the delta counted every `review_rejected`, so a reasons-less event was counted but could never be itemized.

**Fix:** Clustering now returns what the cut hides: `clusterMessages` yields `{ clusters, hiddenClusters }`, and each section rides on a `ClusterSection { clusters, total, hiddenClusters }` where `total` counts every event the section owns in the window before any clustering filter — for rejections, every `review_rejected` including the reasons-less ones, so it equals the Deltas column and the remainder cross-checks against it. `renderClusters` titles the two review sections like their siblings (`Top review failure clusters`, `Top rejection clusters`) and, when `total` exceeds what is itemized, closes the list with `_+M more clusters holding K rejections_` (singular-aware), mirroring `roleList`'s `+N more` and the state-changes section's `+N older transitions hidden`. The byte bound holds: the remainder line adds a fixed handful of bytes, and the caps are unchanged. Pinned in test/failure-report.test.ts ("the top-N cluster sections mark their cut: remainder line, no silent truncation" — 7 rejections across 7 roles render 5 clusters plus `_+2 more clusters holding 2 rejections_`, same mechanism for review failures; and "the rejection remainder cross-checks the Deltas column even for reasons-less events"), both verified against the pre-fix build via a scratch events.jsonl that showed the Deltas table counting 7 while the section listed 5 with no marker.

**Validation gap:** no-observability — the truncation left no trace in the digest itself: a capped section rendered as a complete-looking itemization, and no existing test rendered more clusters than a cap, so confirmation required constructing a window that exceeds it (deterministic and offline, but nothing in the suite surfaced the cut on its own).
### The loop suite's quiet-kill resume test flakes under full-suite load: the quiet watchdog killed runs whose pi had not yet been scheduled to speak (found by coverage loop 2026-09-23, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** Two consecutive full `npm test` runs of the gating suite reported `fail 1`, both times the same test — "a quiet-killed tick keeps its edits and resumes promptly instead of discarding" (22.3 s in the failing run; the loop files are the slowest in the suite). In isolation and in other full runs it passes; the suite is otherwise green. A red gating suite sends code roles to `main red` and can trigger spurious fix attempts, so the flake has fleet-level cost beyond the false alarm.

**Fix:** Not a test-assertion bug — a harness semantic bug. `runPi`'s quiet watchdog stamped its clock at runPi entry and killed after `quietTimeoutSeconds` of silence, but under full-suite-plus-fleet load the OS can take seconds to fork and schedule the fake-pi process before it prints its first line, so phase-2 runs of that test (the resumed author, the reviewer) were quiet-killed during startup — the resumed tick came back `quiet_killed` instead of `queued` and the landing came back failed instead of `changed`, at the observed 22.3 s (phase 1's ~7.5 s kill plus the startup-starved second run). A run that has not yet produced progress is starting, not hung. The first form of the fix gave a zero-progress run one extra full quiet window (`2 × quietTimeoutSeconds`) before the kill, but that window still false-killed under merge-check load: the kill runs on an interval against the wall clock, so the firing check can predate the child's first bytes entirely — fork/exec starved by the same load, or bytes already written but not yet drained (a firing timer phase precedes the poll phase that delivers stdout). Startup latency is unbounded, so no finite window bounds it: src/pi.ts now gives a run that has emitted not a single byte no quiet kill at all — the tick timeout bounds it (at the production default `quietTimeoutSeconds: 1800` the tick already fired before the old doubled window, so nothing changes there). Once bytes have flowed the run has begun: bytes without progress keep the doubled window (the zombie-stream case), and once real progress has landed the ordinary `quietTimeoutSeconds` window applies unchanged. A genuinely hung run still dies: every existing quiet-kill scenario speaks first, then goes silent. Pinned in test/pi.test.ts ("a run that is slow to speak is not quiet-killed during startup"): a shim byte-silent for 12 s — past even the old doubled window — completes with `ok: true, quietKilled: false`; reverting to any finite startup window re-reddens the pin, and the pre-fix code killed the same shim at ~5 s.

**Validation gap:** no-repro — the flake failed 1-in-N loaded full runs and passed every isolated and synthetic-load retry (~15 attempts up to load ~40), so the race had to be reconstructed from the watchdog's clock semantics and pinned with a deterministic slow-to-speak shim instead of a live reproduction.

### The digest's Fleet state changes section silently keeps only the newest 6 transitions: on a busy window the oldest fleet decisions vanish from the causal frame with no marker (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** The `## Fleet state changes` section is a newest-`STATE_CHANGE_TOP = 6` cap rendered as a bare itemization (the unmarked `.slice(-STATE_CHANGE_TOP)` in src/failure-data.ts, a plain render loop with no truncation output path in src/failure-report.ts), so on a busy window the OLDEST fleet decisions — an early-morning `budget_fallback` or `fleet_paused` that would explain the Deltas table's error ticks — silently vanish with no marker, while a quiet window and a truncated one render identically. Deterministic repro, no model: 9 `tick_deferred` events with distinct timestamps in a scratch root's `events.jsonl` print exactly the newest 6 with no remainder.

**Fix:** `collectFailureReport` now carries the pre-slice count as `stateChangesTotal` on `FailureReportData` (the count is known before the slice), and the render prints a `+N older transitions hidden` line after the list when the cut was live — the same honesty convention as `renderClusters`' `+N more`. Under the cap the section stays a plain itemization (no false marker), and the byte bound holds (the added line is one number; the worst-case byte-bound fixture still passes). Pinned in test/failure-report.test.ts: 9 transitions render the newest 6 plus `+3 older transitions hidden` with the oldest three absent, and a single-transition window shows no marker.

**Validation gap:** no-observability — the truncation left no trace in the digest itself: the section rendered as a complete-looking itemization with no marker, so only a test that renders more than 6 transitions (none existed) could confirm the cut.

### A fix narrative can land on main without its fix: an md-only BUGS.md edit is exempt from the review gate, so a tick can record code that does not exist (found by steward 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-23)

**Symptom:** Commit `9cea8c3` ("bugfix: refuse — none", 2026-09-22) changed only BUGS.md, yet moved the build-check-leak bug (re-opened above) from Open to Fixed with a detailed **Fix:** paragraph naming `runScriptGroup` in src/build-check.ts, an exported `signalTree`, and a pinned regression test — none of which exist anywhere in main's history (`git log --all -S runScriptGroup` finds only that BUGS.md edit). Md-only diffs skip the review gate by design, so nothing verified that the described code existed; the readme role then trusted the Fixed heading and dropped the bug from the status open list (`59e64c6`). Net effect: a false fix record on main, a live bug (the build-check-leak entry below this one) invisible to every role, and no alarm anywhere.

**Fix:** `falseFixReason` in src/fix-claim.ts now cross-checks every md-only diff that touches BUGS.md: a `## Fixed` entry that is new in the diff (compared by provenance-normalized heading, since the move appends ", fixed <date>") must name only symbols that exist on the tree being landed — whitespace-free backticked spans from its **Fix:** paragraph are substring-matched against src/test/scripts code (markdown excluded: the entry names its own symbols) and path-matched against the tree. An entry with zero checkable symbols is left alone, so pure-documentation fixes keep landing md-only. Both consumers of the exemption enforce it: the review gate (src/review.ts) rejects deterministically — no reviewer run, reasons injected into the author's next tick — and the landing path's in-lock re-check (src/landing-merge.ts verifyLanding) refuses the same diff. Pinned in test/fix-claim.test.ts (unit parsing plus gate-level rejection/exemption through reviewAheadOfMain).

**Validation gap:** no-observability — a false Fixed record is indistinguishable from a real one to every role and gate, so confirming it took manual `git log -S` forensics; nothing automated watched what a Fix paragraph claimed versus what the tree contained.

**Correction 2026-09-23:** the Symptom's premise, that the tick recorded code it never wrote, is wrong. The 01:17 bugfix run did edit src/build-check.ts, src/pi.ts and test/build-check.test.ts and passed the suite. The harness discarded those edits because the reply ended `TUMWATER_REFUSED: none` and was treated as a refusal (see the Open refusal-sentinel entry). The `falseFixReason` guard is still useful, but the root cause is that open entry.

**Symptom:** Every code role skipped authoring and no change merged: the baseline check on main `03edeba6` failed inside "a timed-out build check takes its process tree with it (regression)" (test/build-check.test.ts) with `Error: ENOENT: … worktrees/improve/grandchild.pid`. The test gave the check a 600 ms budget; `runBuildCheck` resolves at timeout-fire, and the test then read the grandchild's pid file immediately. The grandchild — a freshly spawned `node -e` — had only the startup chain (npm boot → runner boot → its own node boot) to register its SIGTERM trap and write its pid before the group SIGTERM arrived; under the fleet's load (the suite's per-file `node --test` workers, the orchestrator itself running) that chain outlasted 600 ms, the grandchild died by default action before trapping, the pid file never existed, and the read threw. The test pinning the 12-day leak was itself load-racy — `test/pi.test.ts` had hit and fixed this identical startup race once already (its comment: "the watchdog fires on wall-clock startup — before the grandchild had written its pid file").

**Fix:** The budget is now 4 s — still far under any real check, and the runner idles via `setInterval` so the outcome classification is unchanged — and the pid read polls briefly for the file (deadline 5 s) with a named assertion when it never appears, instead of assuming it exists. The scenario pinned is unchanged: a SIGTERM-trapping grandchild survives the group SIGTERM and only the SIGKILL escalation stops it.

**Validation gap:** none — the gating suite itself reproduced and confirmed the failure (main reddened at `03edeba6` on the fleet's own buildcheck run); nothing was missing, the test was the bug.

### A timed-out build check leaks its entire test process tree: `execFileAsync`'s timeout signals npm alone (found by human investigation 2026-09-21; re-opened by steward 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-22)

**Re-opened 2026-09-22 by steward:** the 2026-09-22 Fixed record was false. Commit `9cea8c3` ("bugfix: refuse — none", an md-only diff, so exempt from the review gate) moved this entry to Fixed and wrote a **Fix:** paragraph describing `runScriptGroup` in src/build-check.ts, an exported `signalTree`, and a pinned grandchild-liveness regression test — but no commit on main contains any of that code: src/build-check.ts:290 still calls `execFileAsync` with the bare `timeout` option, `signalTree` is still private to src/pi.ts, and test/build-check.test.ts has no such test (`git log --all -S runScriptGroup` finds only the BUGS.md edit itself). The readme role then trusted the Fixed heading and dropped the bug from the status open list (`59e64c6`), leaving the leak untracked. The fabricated Fix paragraph is discarded; the original Symptom/Repro/Expected below stand unchanged. The structural hole that allowed this is filed separately below.

**Symptom:** `runBuildCheck` (`src/build-check.ts:281`) runs `await execFileAsync("npm", ["run", check.script], { cwd: wt, maxBuffer, timeout: timeoutMs })`. Node's `timeout` option sends `SIGTERM` to the direct child only. The real tree is `npm run test` → `node dist/src/test-runner.js` → `node --test <65 files>` → one worker per file (`--test-isolation=process`), so when the 300 s timeout fires npm dies and everything below it survives and reparents to PID 1. Four orphaned trees were alive on 2026-09-21 at 22:00, each matching a skipped check to the second: `node dist/src/test-runner.js` pid 63212 started 02:07:31 from the `perf` worktree (its `build_check baseline skipped` logged 02:24:35, durationMs 1028000) and pid 88052 started 05:59:31 from `bugfix` (`skipped` at 06:16:45, durationMs 1037000), the latter with a worker (pid 95649) that had burned **4 min 44 s of CPU** over 16 hours; a third, pid 1412, had been running since 2026-09-09 — 12 days. Worst shape: an orphaned suite keeps executing and eventually reaches the live-orchestrator e2e tests, which spawn a **real orchestrator** — pid 8198, `.tumwater/worktrees/perf/dist/src/cli.js run`, PPID 1, alive 18 h 34 m with its cwd a since-deleted test temp dir. This is a different spawn path from the one `c73363e` fixed: that patched `terminateChild` in `src/pi.ts` for pi's tool-call grandchildren and never touched the build check.

**Repro:** Deterministic and isolated, no fleet needed. A scratch package whose `test` script is `node runner.mjs`, where `runner.mjs` does `spawnSync(process.execPath, ["-e", "setTimeout(function(){}, 40000)"], { stdio: "inherit" })`; call it with `promisify(execFile)("npm", ["run", "test"], { timeout: 5000 })`. The promise rejects at 5.0 s with `killed=true signal=SIGTERM`, `npm` and `runner.mjs` are both gone — and the grandchild is still running with PPID 1. On the live fleet: `ps -eo pid,ppid,lstart,command | awk '$2==1 && /node/'` after any `build check timed out after 300s` warning.

**Expected:** A build check that times out kills everything it started, the same guarantee `terminateChild` now gives a killed tick. Spawn the check detached so it leads its own process group and signal the group on timeout (`process.kill(-pid, …)`, SIGTERM → grace → SIGKILL), falling back to the single pid where the group is gone. A regression test can assert a grandchild is dead after a timed-out check, mirroring `test/pi.test.ts`'s "a killed run leaves no grandchild behind".

**Suspected cause:** The 2026-09-20 grandchild audit scoped itself to pi, because pi is where the harness knowingly kills a running child. The build check kills a child too — through `execFile`'s `timeout` option rather than an explicit `kill` call — so it never appeared in that audit's blast radius, and the option's single-pid semantics are easy to read as a tree kill.

**Fix:** Landed for real this time, with source in the same commit and the review gate run over it. `runScriptGroup` (src/build-check.ts) spawns `npm run <script>` detached — its own process group, exactly like pi — and enforces the timeout GROUP-WIDE: when the timeout fires, `signalTree` (now exported from src/pi.ts, one home for the tree-kill both callers share) SIGTERMs the whole group, and anything still alive after `KILL_GRACE_MS` (10 s) is SIGKILLed. The escalation timer is armed WHEN THE TIMEOUT FIRES, never at spawn — the review gate rejected this fix's first draft of 2026-09-22 for arming it at spawn, which SIGKILLed every healthy check longer than the 10 s grace and misclassified it as a timeout, freezing all landing/batch merges of trees whose suites exceed 10 s — and it is unref'd and deliberately survives `npm`'s own exit, because npm dying on the SIGTERM must not cancel the SIGKILL a lingering grandchild still needs; the check resolves at timeout-fire so the caller's latency stays bounded while the teardown finishes in the background. Captured output is capped at maxBuffer per stream (classification reads the tail); outcome classification is unchanged. Two regression tests pin it in test/build-check.test.ts: a runner whose grandchild TRAPS SIGTERM (so only the escalation can stop it) is dead after the grace under a 600 ms budget and 700 ms grace, and a healthy `sleep 2` script with a 500 ms grace still reads `passed` (pinning the arming-on-timeout). Suite 1284/1284.

**Validation gap:** no-observability — the leak left no trace in any event, log, or assertion the suite could see: a timed-out check was already recorded as an honest `skipped` with the right duration, so nothing could fail until a grandchild-liveness assertion existed; the only evidence was orphaned processes in `ps`, found by human inspection.

### HTTP 429 is not a transient failure class, so a rate-limit storm errors two-thirds of the fleet's ticks instead of retrying (found by human investigation 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-22)

**Symptom:** `429 "Rate limit exceeded"` is the fleet's single largest failure source. On 2026-09-21 alone **69 ticks** ended `result: "error"` with that message (7 at 09:00, 8 at 10:00, 18 at 11:00, 11 at 12:00, 13 at 14:00, 7 at 15:00, 4 at 16:00, 1 at 13:00); over the 09-20→09-21 window the failure digest clusters **40** error ticks and **20** review failures on it, roughly two-thirds of all 56 errors. Every one discards a tick's work outright. The harness has a transient-retry path — `src/loop.ts:393` retries a run when `pi.transientServerTimeout || pi.transientPiCrash` — but 429 is neither: `transientServerTimeout` matches `/predict stream timed out/i`, an LM Studio string whose own comment in `src/pi-stream.ts:47-52` concedes "oMLX has no equivalent message", and `transientPiCrash` needs a `JSON.parse` failure on pi's stderr. So the one error class that is *by definition* retryable is the one class with no retry. Nothing rate-gates the fleet either: three loops keep issuing concurrent requests straight through a storm, and `Retry-After` is not read anywhere in `src/`.

**Repro:** Deterministic at the unit level — construct a `PiRunResult` with `ok: false` and `errorMessage: '429 "Rate limit exceeded"'`; `src/loop.ts:393`'s guard is false, so no retry is attempted and the tick ends `error`. From the log: `grep '429' .tumwater/log/events.jsonl` shows the `tick_end`/`review_failed` pairs with no intervening retry. End-to-end: point the fleet at a provider that returns 429 under `maxConcurrent: 3` and watch every loop climb the error ladder.

**Expected:** A 429 is the world saying "later", not the tick failing. Classify it alongside the other transient classes so the existing one-shot retry covers it, honour `Retry-After` when the provider sends one — both landed 2026-09-22: the parser gained `transientRateLimit` (+ `retryAfterSeconds`) in `src/pi-stream.ts`, and `runWithTransientRetry` in `src/loop.ts` folds it into its one-shot `--continue` retry, waiting out `Retry-After` first (capped at 120 s so a generous hint cannot eat the tick's run budget). The third ask — a short fleet-wide hold so three loops stop amplifying a storm they are collectively causing — is cross-role state the per-tick retry cannot express and got its own entry above (fixed 2026-09-24). The existing entry "A landing review failure latches the review gate's message onto the authoring tick's `lastError`" (fixed 2026-09-21) corrected how these are *reported*; how they are *handled* was never in scope.

**Suspected cause:** The transient classes were added one at a time, each from a specific local-backend incident (LM Studio's idle predict-stream kill, then a torn-chunk pi crash), so the taxonomy grew as a list of observed strings rather than as a policy about which failures belong to the world. The 2026-09-18 move to a rate-limited hosted primary introduced a failure mode that list was never extended for.

**Validation gap:** no-fake — the fake-pi test rig had no scenario emitting a 429-shaped provider error, so nothing failed until one was written; before that the only confirmation evidence was fleet logs.

### A load-sensitive live-orchestrator test falsely reddens main: the same sha failed and passed two minutes apart (found by human investigation 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-22)

**Symptom:** At 21:56:44 on 2026-09-21 `bugfix`'s baseline check on main `fe2de9a4` **failed**, and the harness logged `main fe2de9a4 is red (test: AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: the interlock held: no tick ever started) — code merges blocked until main is green`; `feature`'s tick 257 ended `main_red` on the spot. At 21:58:48 `organize`'s baseline check on **the same sha** passed (durationMs 123907). The assertion is the tail of a live-orchestrator e2e case that starts a real orchestrator, waits up to 60 s for a fake reviewer to touch a marker, then up to 60 s more for an aborted batch to settle. On a machine at load average 26 those budgets are not reliable, and the test rejects whatever commit is being gated rather than the code under test. A false red is expensive twice over: it blocks every code merge fleet-wide until some other worktree happens to run a passing baseline, and `redeploy`'s gate holds the auto-restart while main is red, which is the stale-build trap.

**Fix:** The structural option the entry argued for: the live-orchestrator cases are now their own e2e tier outside the gating suite. The three files (`orchestrator.e2e.test.ts`, `orchestrator-2.e2e.test.ts`, `orchestrator-3.e2e.test.ts`) carry `.e2e.` in their names; `selectTestFiles` excludes `.e2e.test.js` from the unfiltered `npm test` run — so the landing gate's deterministic pre-check and every baseline check no longer execute any wall-clock-bound live orchestrator — while an explicit filter still selects them: `npm run test:e2e` (filter `e2e`) runs the whole tier, `npm test orchestrator` brings a slice back, and CI runs `npm run test:e2e` after `npm test` so the tier keeps its coverage seat. Selection rules pinned in `test/test-runner.test.ts`; tier headers document the split.

**Validation gap:** no-repro — the failure needs a machine at load average ~26 mid-gate (the same sha passed 124 s apart), so no deterministic offline repro exists; the fix is the structural tier split, confirmed by the selection-rule tests and both tiers passing rather than by reproducing the red.

### The live-progress tail seeds from an arbitrary byte offset, so a first observation reports a turn count that means nothing (reported by user 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-22)

**Symptom:** `readLiveProgress` seeded a first observation from `size - TAIL_BYTES` (`src/ui/progress.ts`, 4 MB) with `freshProgress`, treating that byte offset as if it were a run boundary and counting every assistant `message_end` after it. Role logs rotate at `logMaxBytes`, default 16 MB, so a log is routinely larger than the window: a freshly attached observer (`tumwater gui`, or any first `readLiveProgress` call in a process) displayed `turn 9` for a run whose real count was something else, until a session boundary happened to enter the window. `parseProgress` on assistant `message_end` lines with no `session` line returned a positive `turns` — the reader could not distinguish "this run has N turns" from "I started reading N turns into some run". The steady-state path was correct (it only parses bytes appended since the previous poll, anchored by sessions already seen), so the flaw showed only on the first observation — exactly when nobody is checking.

**Repro:** Deterministic, no fleet. `readLiveProgress` over a log of one `session`, one assistant turn, then >4 MB of streaming deltas (the last 4 MB hold no session and no turns) reported `turns: 0` for a run whose real count is 1; over one `session` plus >4 MB of assistant turns it reported only the turns inside the last 4 MB. Both now regress in `test/progress.test.ts`.

**Fix:** `findSeedOffset` (`src/ui/progress.ts`) grows the seed window in doubling steps from `TAIL_BYTES` until it contains the log's last complete `session` event line (or 0 when the log carries none), and first observations seed from there instead of the bare offset. The session event resets the accumulator during the seed fold, so the turns a first observation reports are exactly the current run's. The scan runs at attach time only — steady-state polls still parse only appended bytes — is bounded (~log2 reads over a rotated log, ≤3 windows against the 16 MB rotation cap), and is cheap because the type-first pre-filter skips `message_update` lines without `JSON.parse`. `test/progress.test.ts` pins the scan (`findSeedOffset` grows past a session outside the window, returns 0 for a sessionless log) and the two end-to-end seed counts above. Suite 1287/1287.

**Validation gap:** no-fake — the suite only ever seeded logs smaller than the 4 MB tail window, so nothing exercised a first observation past its edge; confirming the fix required building oversized-log fixtures (>4 MB) that did not exist.
### One pi log per role, two pi runs writing it: a landing run's `session` event resets the turn counter the dashboard shows for the authoring tick (reported by user 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-22)

**Symptom:** The director's state cell read `turn 15`, then `turn 3` moments later — the in-flight turn counter running backwards inside one tick. `readLiveProgress` (`src/ui/progress.ts`) derives it by counting assistant `message_end` events since the **last `session` event** in the role's raw log, and `src/ui/progress.ts:115` restores the at-time-zero state on every one it sees; `src/ui/status-model.ts:46` renders that as `turn ${p.turns + 1}`. The module's premise is stated in its header at `src/ui/progress.ts:7`: "The log is append-only across ticks; each tick's pi run starts with a `session` event, so everything after the last one belongs to the current run." That is one run per tick, and it is false. `roleLogPath` (`src/paths.ts:94`) gives a role exactly one file, `.tumwater/log/<role>.pi.jsonl`, but a role makes several pi runs — the authoring run in `worktrees/<role>` and the landing/review run in `worktrees/_land-<role>` — and each emits its own `session` event into that one file. Director tick 109 (started 00:26:23 PDT 2026-09-22) recorded three, read out of `director.pi.jsonl` with their `cwd` field (timestamps converted from the log's UTC):

```
10:16:52 PDT (09-21)  cwd=…/worktrees/director        24 turns  → cell read "turn 25"
00:27:20 PDT (09-22)  cwd=…/worktrees/_land-director   7 turns  → reset to 1, climbed to 8
00:28:41 PDT (09-22)  cwd=…/worktrees/director        10 turns  → reset to 1, then 11
```

Two resets in 2 min 18 s. The counter is only half of it: between 00:27:20 and 00:28:41 the cell's `turn`, `ctx` and tool label all described the **landing** run while the label said `working` and the elapsed time counted the authoring tick — the row was reporting one run's progress under another run's heading. The harness's own accounting is unaffected: the commit trailer and the friction check read `PiRunResult.turns`, which is per-run and correct (`src/commit-message.ts:74`).

**Repro:** Deterministic, no fleet. The old reader over a line array holding a run's `message_end`s, then a `session` line, then two more `message_end`s returned `turns: 2` — the documented behavior, with nothing distinguishing whose session it was; live, `grep '"session"' .tumwater/log/<role>.pi.jsonl | jq -r '[.timestamp, .cwd] | @tsv'` on any role that has landed recently shows `worktrees/<role>` and `worktrees/_land-<role>` sessions interleaved in the one file.

**Fix:** Carry the run's identity into the reader (the shared transcript stays one file, as the operator's end-to-end `logs --role` view wants). A `session` event's `cwd` names the worktree the run started in — the role's own worktree (author runs) or its `_land-<role>` lander worktree (the review gate's runs: reviewer, conflict resolver) — so `readLiveProgress` now keeps **one accumulator per run kind** (`author`/`gate`, its new `kind` parameter) and demuxes: a gate session resets only the gate's counts, never the working tick's, and lines fold into the kind of the most recent session (the harness's `tumwater_run` label line flips the routing early; only `session` and the label line carry a run's identity, so lines from two truly concurrent different-kind runs land in the newer run's accumulator — the best attribution the line format allows). `progressKind` (status-model.ts) is the one home for which kind a phase reads: `gate` while `s.phase === "review"` (the reviewing cell), `author` otherwise — renderStatus and the GUI payload both derive their precomputed tail through it, and `loopPhase`'s review branch reads gate. `test/progress.test.ts` pins the demux (gate session mid-log, next author session, label-line routing); the reviewing-cell test's fixture now carries the real lander-worktree cwd. Suite 1281/1281.

**Validation gap:** unclear-invariant — the existing suite actively pinned the buggy behavior as the documented contract ("resets at a new session"), so confirming the bug first required reconstructing what the cell was supposed to guarantee: per-run attribution (whose turns is the working cell showing?), which only the `session` event's `cwd` can answer.
- A transient retry of a hint-less 429 waits 0 seconds, burning the one retry into the same exhausted per-minute bucket (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit f68ba587; gap: unclear-invariant)
- The check-permit test's granted-order assert is load-sensitive: concurrent waiters are resumed in scheduler order, not tier order, so a loaded run grants `other` before `merge` and fails (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 0f8517fa; gap: no-repro)
- A tick that throws between dequeuing the user prompt and the pi run silently drops the request: the catch records the error but never re-queues the dequeued (or reclaimed) prompt (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit aa7198cb; gap: no-observability)
- A resume-owning tick's requeued role prompt is never reclaimed: the resume fulfills the request in its session while the queue copy survives, and a later fresh tick runs the same request a second time (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit fc2f6385; gap: no-fake)
- A Done/Fixed entry's body bullets that mention a completion date count as entries, inflating the usage report's featuresDone/bugsFixed tallies (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit b997fd23; gap: unclear-invariant)
- `onceSummary`'s settle-map fallback re-derives backoff from `nextRunAt` alone, so a not-yet-due role in an aborted round's summary reads as "backoff" (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 41bb66ba; gap: unclear-invariant)
- `settleSkipped` reads a pending resume as "idle — nothing was due" in the once round's summary: a cut-off tick waits one interval with `backoffSeconds` 0, the same shape as a fresh scheduled clock (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 77f9cf96; gap: no-fake)
- Once mode's clock override is nullified by the `nextRunAt` gate it shares with the min-interval clock, so `run --once` right after a daemon run does nothing (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit be8087c4; gap: no-fake)
- `onceSummary` re-derives the skip reasons the orchestrator already settled on, so a deferred role reads as "idle — nothing was due" in the once round's summary (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 5dcee4ff; gap: no-fake)
- `fixedHeadings` and `bugEntryBody` in src/fix-claim.ts have no fenced-code state: a quoted `### ` line counts as a Fixed entry, and an entry body quoting a fence is cut at it, so a narrative rewrite after the fence compares equal and skips the false-fix symbol check (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 473c051a; gap: no-observability)
- `fenceTracker` opens a fence at a backtick-fence line whose info string contains a backtick, which CommonMark forbids: one unclosable fence swallowed 46 Fixed entries and the `## Verified` section (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit abae335e; gap: no-observability)
- `parseEntryDetails` and the report's date scan treat a `### `/`- ` line inside a fenced code block as entry structure: a quoted heading splits the entry on the dashboards and phantom `(fixed DATE)` lines inflate the usage report's fixed count (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 07b66820; gap: no-observability)
- `sectionLines` treats a `## ` heading inside a fenced code block as a section boundary, silently truncating entries and dropping every later one (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 27784994; gap: no-repro)
- `readEvents`' NaN limit passes the `limit <= 0` guard and returns the whole log instead of none: every comparison with NaN is false (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 6ea6a5de; gap: unclear-invariant)
- `readEvents`' non-positive limit returns a wrong window instead of none: `slice(-0)` is `slice(0)` and a negative limit is a positive-start `slice(k)` (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-25, fixed 2026-09-25; commit 419c34ae; gap: unclear-invariant)

- The build check's rejection headline is a node:test summary counter: two rejections reported as `build check failed (test): ℹ todo 0` (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-22, fixed 2026-09-22; commit 356c74c; gap: no-fake)
- A reviewer's verdict-last reply loses every reason it wrote: `parseVerdict` reads only the lines after the last VERDICT line (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-22; commit 637ea9a; gap: no-observability)
- The test suite has never deleted a temp dir: 1.68M abandoned `tumwater-test-*` directories make every `mkdtemp` 9,145× slower and time out the build check (found by human investigation 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-22; commit e9a6b88; gap: no-observability)

- The slow-but-talkative quiet-watchdog test dies quiet_killed on a loaded machine: 1s sleeps against a 3s window left ~2s of per-gap slack, and a landing gate's gap crossed it (found by the review gate 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-21; commit 3ab6acc; gap: no-repro)
- The grandchild-leak regression test raced its own setup: on a loaded machine the quiet watchdog killed the fake-pi shim before the grandchild recorded its pid, reddening main with an ENOENT (found by the review gate 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-21; commit dd3921c; gap: no-repro)
- Leftover recovery records a generic merge summary, so the failure digest's `## Landed in the window` cannot name recovered work (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-21; commit fe2de9a; gap: none)
- A persistent review failure never reaches the error-streak alarm: every recovery tick that re-fails resets `consecutiveErrors` (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-21; commit 69d0f5b; gap: no-observability)
- A killed tick leaks its tool-call grandchildren: `terminateChild` signals only the pi process, never its group (found by human investigation 2026-09-20, fixed 2026-09-21; commit c73363e; gap: no-fake)
- A landing review failure latches the review gate's message onto the authoring tick's `lastError`, so `tick_end` reports it as the tick's own error (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-21; commit b05c79c; gap: unclear-invariant)
- Every Fixed entry lacks the `**Validation gap:**` trace, so the repair-trace tally has no population and the Fixed backlog cannot be compressed (found by steward 2026-09-20, fixed 2026-09-21; commit bd8f3e6; gap: no-observability)
- The failure digest carries outcomes but not the harness decisions that produced them, so the telemetry role cannot apply its own load-bearing rule (found by human investigation 2026-09-20, fixed 2026-09-21; commit 606523e; gap: unclear-invariant)
- The dashboard loop table sorts the `landing` phase as inactive, so a landing role drops below queued and sleeping loops (reported by user 2026-09-21, fixed 2026-09-21; commit f131e19; gap: none)
- A dead reviewer backend destroys committed work: transport errors count toward `REVIEW_FAILURE_LIMIT` and hard-reset the worktree (found by log analysis 2026-09-20, fixed 2026-09-21; commit 5b32f62; gap: unclear-invariant)
- The GUI and TUI abbreviate millions as `k`: 13.8M output tokens renders as `13820.3k` (reported by user 2026-09-20, fixed 2026-09-21; commit e709f9f; gap: none)
- The restart-cooldown deferral warning now fires once per cooldown episode, not once per landed head (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-20; commit 02edcf5; gap: unclear-invariant)
- README promises `status --json` is "the same payload as the GUI's /api/status", but the served payload carries an extra `serverBuildSha` field (found by qa loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit 92caeb7; gap: real-run-needed)
- `inputViewWindow` can collapse to an empty window, blanking the TUI prompt line at narrow widths (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit 9bcbd4b; gap: no-observability)
- Friction's absolute turn threshold measures model speed, not difficulty: on the fleet's fast API model it flags 41% of changed ticks at 2–19 minutes (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit 207e560; gap: unclear-invariant)
- The failure digest's per-role rejection counter reads 0 on the live fleet: it counted a `tick_end` result no landing path emits (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit 2e02c16; gap: no-observability)
- Build-check rejections name the stack frame instead of the failing test (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit a3ee4d9; gap: no-observability)
- `changedFiles` read a porcelain rename line's `from -> to` field as one non-existent path (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit 9c63aed; gap: no-fake)
- `unquotePorcelainPath` decoded git's `\a`/`\b`/`\f`/`\v` escapes as bare letters (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit 47f5bad; gap: no-fake)
- The pi-log parser returned valid-JSON non-object lines as if they were events (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit be8c6e8; gap: no-fake)
- A qa tick cut off before declaring its outcome still advanced the flow-coverage rotation (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit bdccac8; gap: no-fake)
- Leftover recovery re-reviews a high-friction commit without its high-friction flag (found by telemetry loop 2026-09-19, fixed 2026-09-19; commit c016552; gap: no-fake)
- The TUI prompt window split emoji into lone surrogates and could re-anchor past the cursor (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit e2a4196; gap: no-observability)
- The TUI prompt's arrow keys land the cursor inside an emoji; backspace/delete then leave a lone surrogate (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit 39817b5; gap: no-observability)
- The restart drain's p75 sampled a tick's semaphore queue wait as its run time (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit e819cf5; gap: no-observability)
- The restart drain has never once completed: 38 of 39 redeploys held the fleet a full 30 minutes and then aborted 4–12 ticks anyway (found by log analysis 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit 5c365ac; gap: no-observability)
- `maxConcurrent` stopped bounding model load when landings moved off the author semaphore: 4 concurrent pi streams for a quarter of the day (found by log analysis 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit 2eee180; gap: no-observability)
- A transient `ENOTEMPTY` on `dist.prev` aborts the whole build swap: no `rmSync` in the harness passed `maxRetries` (found by log analysis 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit 0d05a19; gap: no-repro)
- A landing build check that times out merges to main unverified: one skip on 2026-09-16 turned main red for five hours (found by log analysis 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit 5b125c4; gap: unclear-invariant)
- `quiet_killed` is the only tick outcome with no strike cap, no backoff and no alarm: hour-long empty retries consumed 44% of the fleet over two days (found by log analysis 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit a3b5138; gap: no-observability)
- A deferred tick preserves the `no_change` that causes it: five maintenance roles were permanently off after their first idle tick (found by log analysis 2026-09-17, fixed 2026-09-18; commit 704139c; gap: no-observability)
- A red main latched by a single worktree deadlocks the fleet, and the warning that reports it names a stack frame instead of the failure (found by human investigation 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit bb8dc26; gap: no-observability)
- Two load-sensitive tests reject whatever commit is being gated: five unrelated commits lost in three days (found by log analysis and reproduced 2026-09-18, fixed 2026-09-18; commit bb8dc26; gap: no-repro)
- A torn land-queue head file clogs the landing slot forever: the live entry behind it never lands, and its role's interlock pins its ticks (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-17, fixed 2026-09-17; commit 060dff4; gap: no-observability)
- A rejected `mainGreen` latches a false "main is red" when the redeploy's own git call fails (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-15, fixed 2026-09-16; commit e383a10)
- No operator lever wakes a backed-off fleet: `reset-counters` preserves the schedule and a restart reloads it (found by human log analysis 2026-09-15, fixed 2026-09-15; commit 6322625)
- Repeated tick failures raise no alarm: 44 errors across all 13 loops looked exactly like a quiet fleet (found by human log analysis 2026-09-15, fixed 2026-09-15; commit 4dba580)
- A broken toolchain is reported as "main is red", and the verdict then latches until main moves (found by human log analysis 2026-09-15, fixed 2026-09-15; commit 70371b8)
- A tick that fails in 200 ms climbs the idle-backoff ladder: one broken `git` put the whole fleet to sleep for hours (found by human log analysis 2026-09-15, fixed 2026-09-15; commit 7f8c5f0)
- Display clippers violate their length invariant at degenerate budgets: truncate(s, 0) and clipToWidth(line, -1) return almost the whole input (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-14, fixed 2026-09-14; commit eaa5b83)
- Gate verdicts were persisted only at tick boundaries: a sudden death mid-run resurrected a stale reject note into every later tick (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-14, fixed 2026-09-14; commit a3501fb)
- Budget badge reads "n/a today", and its GUI link opens a cap editor pre-filled with 50 on all-free fleets (reported by user 2026-09-14, fixed by bugfix loop 2026-09-14; commit f6af8d3)
- Stall warning for a tool call pi started without a toolName names nothing (or with a leading space) (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-13, fixed 2026-09-13; commit d17fd5c)
- A stalled tool call is invisible until the quiet watchdog kills it — stall warning names the command in the event feed and state cell (reported by user 2026-09-12, fixed by bugfix loop 2026-09-13; commit fcfc66f)
- A single tool call with no timeout blocks a whole tick — trigger removed and quiet-kills now preserve work (reported by user 2026-09-12, fixed by bugfix loop 2026-09-13; commit 62086cd)
- Maintenance roles tick on their normal clock while planned features or open bugs wait — deferral is reactive, not backlog-aware (reported by user 2026-09-12, fixed by bugfix loop 2026-09-12; commit 4c9acc4)
- Slot wait queue is FIFO across polls: a due feature/bugfix tick queues behind maintenance ticks that requested slots earlier (reported by user 2026-09-12, fixed by bugfix loop 2026-09-12; commit c1e307f)
- TUI's Ctrl+B budget editor accepted hex and exponent notation as a dollar cap (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-12, fixed 2026-09-12; commit f485acc)
- TUI/GUI "reviewing" state shows only elapsed time — no turn/ctx/tool detail like the "working" state does (reported by user 2026-09-11, fixed by bugfix loop 2026-09-12; commit 3a0410e)
- readTranscriptTail scanned a stale stat size when rotation recreated the path with a smaller file between its stat and open: one line dropped from the window, `end` past EOF (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-11, fixed 2026-09-11; commit d8cb85d)
- Tail readers threw ENOENT when log rotation renamed the file between their stat and open: a poll landing in that window crashed the TUI or `logs -f` process (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-11, fixed 2026-09-11; commit a48e388)
- Auto-restart completes on every stale episode under churn: rate-limit completed auto-restarts to at most one per 12 hours (reported by user 2026-09-11, fixed 2026-09-11; commit 003714f)
- readEvents' torn trailing line occupied one of the limit slots: while events.jsonl ended unterminated, feeds showed at most limit−1 events (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-11, fixed 2026-09-11; commit b5b937f)
- logEvent glued a new event onto an unterminated trailing line: after a crash mid-append, one complete event was lost from every consumer (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-11, fixed 2026-09-11; commit ab239ec)
- /api/report coerced hex/scientific/signed `days` spellings instead of degrading to the default window (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-11, fixed 2026-09-11; commit e7028ed)
- forEachTailChunk ignored onChunk's early stop, so every poll of a grown log re-read it whole (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-11, fixed 2026-09-11; commit d961312)
- The review gate checks the pre-rebase tree, so the bytes that land on main were never run through a check (found by human analysis 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-10; commit 7ce70ae)
- README promises `tumwater init` seeds a git repo, but it refuses to run outside an existing one (found by qa loop 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-10; commit 118f73a)
- Budget badge shows `$0.00/$50` on free/local LLM fleets instead of n/a (reported by user 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-09; commit e0b817a)
- Auto-restart aborts an in-flight director tick after the 30-minute drain: the director should be exempt and waited for (reported by user 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-09; commit 00c7cc0)
  - Review ruling 2026-09-22: a later director attempt to also cap the ROLE-tick drain window by default (a hard cap below the 30-minute cold-start fallback on `RESTART_DRAIN_MAX_MS`) was rejected at the gate — this bug's recorded fix is the director-tick exemption alone, and role ticks keep today's behavior (waited for up to the 30-minute cold-start window or the observed p75, then aborted resumably). No drain test exercises a default-cap default (every one injects `drainMaxMs` through the constructor seam), so the sole behavioral change had zero regression coverage. Do not re-propose a default cap without new evidence that the window itself is wrong.
- Review gate rejects a change for contradicting an already-recorded bug/plan instead of letting the newer user instruction win (reported by user 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-09; commit 47a9590)
- `runPi` resolves before its raw log flushes: a load-sensitive race that flakes the suite and can truncate a transcript (found by human analysis 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-09; commit 77f4837)
- Auto-restart's drain clock restarted on every main move: a 30-minute cap held the fleet for 38 (found by human 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-08; commit 7b83df3)
- Auto-restart deadlocked on a self-referential test: `npm test` fails in every worktree without a local install (found by readme loop 2026-09-08, diagnosed by human 2026-09-08, fixed 2026-09-08; commit 9656e1a)
- Harness never picks up its own new build: the fleet ran a 2026-08-27 build for ten days while 350 commits landed (found by human log analysis 2026-09-07, fixed 2026-09-07; commit 9499cf5)
- pi crashing on malformed JSON abandoned the session: five ticks lost, one of them 2 h 39 m of director work (found by human log analysis 2026-09-07, fixed 2026-09-07; commit a274605)
- Cut-off resumes were bridged as "the harness was restarted" and the cut-off streak froze at the resume limit (found by human log analysis 2026-09-07, fixed 2026-09-07; commit 418f620)
- Changed ticks whose reply lacked SUMMARY landed as "<role> tick N" — 73 commits, 32 of them feature (found by human log analysis 2026-09-07, fixed 2026-09-07; commit c30e885)
- GUI dashboard executes HTML in backlog entry bodies — unescaped innerHTML injection (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-06, fixed 2026-09-06; commit 6fe4dfe)

- Non-ASCII text in pi output garbled when a multi-byte character straddles a stdout chunk boundary (found by bugfix loop 2026-09-03, fixed 2026-09-03; commit 07b7a7e)
- Tests red on main: feature tick 83's `today` column broke two layout assertions (found by readme loop 2026-09-03, fixed 2026-09-03; commit 02a8661)
- readTranscriptTail returns duplicated entries when a pi log starts with a blank line (found by coverage loop 2026-09-03, fixed 2026-09-03; commit 86e6559)
- Main's suite red: stale duplicate of the already-fixed fmtUsdCap test break — re-recorded from a pre-fix snapshot (re-recorded by readme loop 2026-09-02, closed 2026-09-02; commit 91a0843)
- Tests red on main: clean tick 91's fmtUsdCap assertion carries a stray quote and can never match GUI_PAGE (found by coverage loop 2026-09-02, fixed 2026-09-02; commit 91a0843)
- Main's build broken: organize tick 78 deleted git.ts's rebase/fast-forward/conflict helpers without landing their move into landing-merge.ts (found by readme loop 2026-08-31, fixed 2026-08-31; commit 2cff978)
- Interrupted tick on a slow-clock role does not resume promptly: the min gap holds it for a full interval (found by bugfix loop 2026-08-30, fixed 2026-08-30; commit f773a49)
- Orphaned merge lock (no pid file) can never be broken — every merge times out forever (found by bugfix loop 2026-08-29, fixed 2026-08-29; commit 6c44004)

## Verified

- 2026-09-22 run (real): first-time-user flow in a scratch repo — init + doctor green, then a bounded real run (one enabled role `organize`, maxConcurrent 1, fake pi shim on PATH) ticked three times; no_change backoff, tick_deferred, and wake-on-main-move all matched the docs; status/logs/`logs --role --prompt` (exact tick prompt under its run separator) confirm; process tree killed, no survivors, scratch deleted.
- 2026-09-28 run (real): fresh build (`npm run build`, worktree clean after), scratch repo via the README's literal usage — init green, bounded real `tumwater run` (one enabled role `organize`, maxConcurrent 1, review off, fake pi shim on PATH) ticked once to no_change and deferred; `status` table (ticks/last result/next run) and `logs` event stream both match the docs; `tumwater stop` drains and exits ("orchestrator stopped"); no survivors, scratch deleted.
- 2026-09-30 run (real): fresh checkout's dist/ against a scratch repo via the README's literal usage — init green, bounded real `tumwater run` (maxConcurrent 1, review off, fake pi shim on PATH) — config listing only `organize` under `roles` still left the other 12 default-enabled roles ticking (documented: "All 13 roles and the director are enabled by default"; disabling needs explicit `enabled: false`), all 14 ticked once to no_change under the cap; `status`, `logs --since`, `logs --role` (pi transcript), and the `--role --since` rejection all match the docs; `tumwater stop` drained ("orchestrator stopped") and the run pid exited; no scratch survivors, scratch deleted, worktree untouched but BUGS.md.
